- **`Apply? [y/N]`;** with no TTY and no `--yes` it refuses. Each file is backed up to `<file>.bak-switchboard-YYYYmmddHHMMSS` with the original mode (0600 if the original is more open), then written atomically (a `mkstemp` file, the mode set on the open descriptor, `os.replace`). Files are written first, then commands (`claude mcp remove --scope user switchboard`, only when the registered entry runs switchboard's MCP server for this home); a failed command is reported with the command to run by hand and makes the exit code 1, and the file edits stay.
- **Re-running** says "nothing to remove"; a missing file is skipped; an editable install of switchboard isn't refused (removing entries can't make an agent's edits run). `--user-home DIR` redirects `~` for tests.
- **`all`** plans claude, codex, cursor and devin in that order, renders one combined diff, asks once and prints a one-line summary per harness. A harness whose config can't be read is reported as an error in the diff and the summary; the others still apply and the exit code is 1.
- **`--purge-hooks`** also deletes `<home>/hooks/switchboard_hook-*.py`, only when none of the four hook configs (under `--user-home` and, if that is set, under the real `~` too) still runs one after this run.

See §23 for the rules of removal.

---

## 10. Commands

`broker/commands.py`: `parse_command(text) -> Command | ParseError` and `apply(cmd, room, actor)`. Commands are reachable **only** through `human.command` over the UDS (peer-checked) or through an authenticated web session. A leading `/` in an agent's `say()` text is stored literally and never parsed (tested).
- Commands that **reduce** activity are allowed at `human_cli`.
- Commands that **raise** activity need `human`: the web session, or test trust.
- Every command issued over the CLI posts a notice with the peer's short process chain, for example "/pause by alice via cli (zsh ← Terminal)".

| Command | Effect | Role | Enforced in |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/pause` | Sets `rooms.paused`; pause actions (§8.5) | human_cli | service + engine |
| `/resume` | Clears `paused` and `paused_reason`, resets `hop_count`, re-evaluates | human | service + engine |
| `/rename <new-name>` | Changes the room's name, stores and delivers a notice, keeps its id and memberships (§46) | human (web only) | service + store |
| `/pin <id>` | Pins a chat message of this room (at most 5, §48): stored, listed in the web header, shown at every join and in `/catchup`; posts a notice and a `pin` event | human (web only) | service + store |
| `/unpin <id>` | Removes a pin; a notice and an `unpin` event | human_cli | service + store |
| `/kick <name>` | Sets `left_at`, `kicked=1`. Deliveries become `revoked`, sinks resolve `kicked`, a notice is posted. A re-join by the same participant is refused. | human_cli | service.join, rpc auth |
| `/close` | Ends every membership (reason `closed`, not kicked), posts leave lines and a notice, renames the room `#name~closed-<id>`, hides it; sinks resolve `closed` (§28.3) | human_cli | service + engine |
| `/budget` | Shows `remaining/per_hour` and the reset time | human_cli | – |
| `/budget n` | Sets `budget_remaining=n` (n ≥ 0) | human_cli if n ≤ current, else human | rules |
| `/hops` | Shows `hop_count/hop_limit` (or "loop guard off" at 0) and whether the room is paused by the loop guard | human_cli | – |
| `/hops n` | Sets `rooms.hop_limit=n` (0 ≤ n ≤ 1000; 0 = loop guard off). Never un-pauses: a loop-guard pause still needs `/resume` (the reply says so). Posts a notice, writes a `hop_limit_set` event (`old`, `new`), pushes the room settings | human_cli if it doesn't raise activity (lower, same, or turning the guard back on from 0), else human (a higher limit, or 0) | engine reads the live row (§8.5) |
| `/hold <name>` | Sets `memberships.held`; held messages stay queued | human_cli | engine.evaluate |
| `/release <name>` | Clears `held` and re-evaluates | human | engine.evaluate |
| `/quiet <name>` | Sets `memberships.quiet`: unaddressed chatter never starts a turn for that agent (§50). Messages stay queued and still show at `read()` | human_cli | engine.evaluate |
| `/unquiet <name>` | Clears `quiet` and re-evaluates, so chatter already past the hold goes now | human | engine.evaluate |
| `/wake <name>` | Wakes an idle agent now for the chatter waiting on the hold (§8.2, #137): no budget, no one-peer-batch rule; a busy agent gets it once idle. Refused for a held member or a paused room, and says so when nothing is waiting | human (it raises activity) | engine.wake_now |
| `/catchup <agent> [on <member> \| on "<topic>"] [note]` | Posts one human chat message asking `agent` to get up to speed on a member's work, a topic, or the room, from their session history with its own tool, by a fixed protocol; the subjects get no delivery of it; a `catchup` event (§26) | human_cli (it only posts as the human, like `switchboard say`) | service (`human_say`) |
| `/review <url> [note]` | Opens the room's review board for `url`, as the person, and posts one human chat message that @mentions every agent in the room and asks them to review it on the board (§37.8). Until 0.4 it was `/catchup`'s alias (§26) | human_cli (it posts as the human, like `/catchup`) | service (`human_say`), `Boards` |
| `/meeting listen <name> [off]` | Listen to call chatter (§52.1); off also works from the person's CLI | human (off: human_cli) | engine.evaluate |
| `/who` | Members: name, harness, status, tier (+note; `verifying...` alone while a Codex thread proof runs, §9.3), away, ⚠/?, held, quiet, queued, in flight, parked, `session: <id> @ <host>` (§26) | human_cli | service |
| `/status` | Room: paused and reason, budget, hop count; per-agent queued, in-flight and expiry counts; the last 10 rule events; Codex link state; hook hash state; test mode | human_cli | service |
| `/help` | Lists the commands | human_cli | – |

In M1, `/kick`, `/hold` and `/release` on an agent-less room return "no such member" (as `/wake` does since #137, and `/quiet` and `/unquiet` do since #548).

`/hops` was added after M7 (§22), at the user's request: agents should be able to run up to about 30 messages without a pause, adjustable while the room is live. `/review` was added after M7, as milestone R1, and replaced by `/catchup` (§26).

Neither command can change `budget_per_hour` itself, the rate the room's wake budget refills to each hour: `/budget n` only ever sets what's left *this* hour. The room's own settings dialog does both at once, through a REST route rather than a command (§42).

---

## 11. Security model

**Threat model.** Every agent runs as the same user as its harness, on its own machine. Some run with approvals off (Claude `bypassPermissions`, Codex `never`) on purpose, so room text can make those agents act. **Joining a room is the opt-in**, and the buddy list shows ⚠, or `?` when the mode is unknown. A room is also a **bridge**: work posted by a sandboxed or prompting member can be carried out by a ⚠ member. The UI banner (§5.5) and SECURITY.md say so. The real isolation is the sandbox (SECURITY.md, SANDBOX.md); switchboard's job is never to *add* authority. A room's whiteboard (§56) is shared state its members write and the others read: shapes are text, cut and clamped, with no link, image or script, and what an agent reads back goes through the envelope so it stays data.

Since 0.38.0 (#385) an agent is a client of the broker over HTTPS with a token of its own (§49), and nothing of switchboard's runs on its machine but the plugin's hooks. That removed the largest part of this section's surface: a process on the broker's machine that had to be told apart from the human's (the MCP server's and the hook script's ancestry checks), a socket into Claude Code, a connection into the Codex app-server, and a hook script the agent could edit. §11.1 says what each used to guard.

**switchboard never does these.** Each item has a test or a code-level guard.
1. **Launch, wrap, or type into an agent or terminal.** tmux appears only under `tests/live`, and in `tests/evals`, which runs that driver (§12.7).
2. **Answer or auto-approve a prompt.**
   - The hook reply table (§7.3, `broker/hook_replies.py`) cannot express `permissionDecision`, `updatedInput`, `behavior` or `updated_mcp_tool_output`, and it writes `decision` only as `"block"` on a Stop (invariant and fuzz test: `tests/unit/test_hook_invariants.py`).
   - **No permission hook that can decide.** Never registered: a permission hook that can decide. Allowed, for status only: a report-only one, which outputs no decision. This was "never registers a PermissionRequest hook" until 2026-10-21, when the maintainer said yes to a report-only PermissionRequest hook for Codex and Devin. The question was whether to add one that "can never approve or deny anything"; the answer, in chat on that day, under #472, was "Do this". The yes goes exactly that far: the rule above, for those two plugins, and no other permission hook. Another one starts in an issue and needs the maintainer's yes again.
   - **How "can never decide" is made true** (§49.9). In Codex 0.158.0 an `allow` in an `mcp_tool` PermissionRequest hook's reply approved a call with no prompt (live), so a hook whose output is the server's reply could be made to approve by a hostile server. Hence: (1) the hook is a *command* that prints nothing, exits 0 and throws away the server's answer (Codex's runs `async`, which Codex documents as unable to "block, approve, rewrite, or otherwise control" the call; Devin's is a Python script with no output path), and the plugin has no `mcp_tool` PermissionRequest hook; (2) the server answers the report with exactly `{}` in every state, reads the session id of the request and no other field, takes it for Codex and Devin only, refuses it from the `hook` tool (an `mcp_tool` hook), and drops any output the engine might hand a report-only event (`AgentService.http_hook`); (3) `tests/unit/test_permission_hooks_static.py` reads every plugin's hook files, fails if any plugin but those two registers a permission hook or either registers a second or another kind, and runs both scripts against a server that answers with every decision it can (allow, deny, approve, block, a rewrite, stop, context, a huge body, an error carrying a decision): stdout is empty and the exit is 0. `tests/integration/test_approval_report_only.py` pins the server's half.
   - Nothing of switchboard's connects to a harness's own control channel: there is no Codex app-server client and no Claude inbox.
3. **Declare `claude/channel` or `claude/channel/permission`** (test on the MCP capabilities: `tests/unit/test_mcp_server_static.py`).
4. **Pass `--dangerously-*`, `--approve-for-me`, `--approve-mcps` or `--trust`.** Nothing launches an agent, and the static guardrail test below holds every such string to `guardrails.py`. Test-harness flags live only under `tests/live/harness`.
5. **Push anything into an agent.** There is no push route (§9): a message reaches an agent only as the answer to a call it made, the answer to a hook it ran, or the text that ends a Stop hook it is waiting in (§49.4). So no turn is started, steered or queued from outside, a session on another machine is never reached by the broker, and an approval prompt is never written into.
6. **Allowlist anything but its own tools.** It sets honest MCP annotations, and the plugins and templates (§49.5 to §49.7) name only switchboard's own tools, and never write `trust`, `hooks.state`, `crossSessionInbound`, sandbox or network keys (`tests/unit/test_guardrails_static.py`, `tests/integration/test_agent_plugins.py`).
7. **Deliver to a session that is waiting on approval.**
   - Nothing is pushed (item 5), so nothing can land in an open prompt: a busy session gets context only in the answer to its own hook after a finished tool, an idle one only through its `wait()` or the Stop hook it is in.
   - A session whose status is `waiting-approval` is delivered nothing at all (engine tests). Over HTTPS Claude Code's Notification hook and Codex's and Devin's report-only PermissionRequest hook set that status, and the session's next hook clears it (§7.4, §49.9); Cursor has no hook at a prompt, so it has no source for it.
8. **Let a room message change settings.** Commands need a human role, and raising commands need the web session. Tests: an agent calls `say("/pause")`; a peer with agent ancestry calls `human.command` and gets `forbidden`.
9. **Listen on anything but `127.0.0.1` and the 0600 UDS** (bind test), unless you set `--listen` together with `--public-url`, for a broker behind a proxy that terminates TLS (§30; a refused setting stops the broker before it touches its home). The UI is served only on `switchboard.localhost` (or the public URL's host, §30), with a strict CSP, `textContent`-only rendering, and no FastAPI docs routes. `/mcp` is on the same listener and answers only a live agent token and a request with no `Origin` (§49.2). Three more routes of it, `/mcp/listen`, `/mcp/session` and `/mcp/meeting/host`, also answer a listener key (§49.11, §54). Two routes under `/mcp/meeting/`, `push` and `speech`, answer only a hosted call's key (§54), and refuse an agent token, a session, a listener key and any request with an `Origin`; `/mcp/meeting/host` answers an agent token or a listener key and refuses a call key, a session and any request with an `Origin`.
10. **Log or forward secrets.**
    - A hook sends only the fields §7.2 names, cut and checked, and the batch tokens in a switchboard tool's own result; no environment value, no tool input and no other tool's result.
    - An agent token is shown once and kept as a hash, and a hook sends it only in an HTTP header, never on a command line (Claude shows a hook's command line to the model, F§3 2.2).
    - A **listener key** (§49.11), which Claude Code's command scripts present when the agent signed in through the browser and no token is within their reach, is made on the agent's machine and never sent except to the three routes it opens, in a header and never on a command line; the server keeps only its sha256, and the connection's own header carries that hash, never the key. A typed token travels in `X-Switchboard-Token` and is redacted by a proxy only if the operator says so (DEPLOY.md).
    - A **call key** (§54) is made on the machine that hosts a call and never sent but to the call's three routes, in a header; the broker is told only its sha256, by the host script's own request (no model sees even the hash), and keeps only that. The key is in no database row, log, notice, frame or message (`tests/integration/test_meeting_hosted.py`).
    - Broker logs hold ids, not text; uvicorn access logs are off; FastMCP and uvicorn log at WARNING without tool arguments.
    - `uninstall` diffs mask values, and backups are 0600 or keep their original mode. A canary test covers its output, logs and backups.
11. **Trust a caller-supplied session id, thread id or membership id without a check.**
    - A call is tied to its token's device, and a membership id (`m:<id>`) works only from the session it belongs to (§49.3); the device is part of every session key, so another device can't take a session by knowing its id.
    - A Claude Code, Cursor or Devin session id comes from a one-use hook report under the same token, never from the call itself (§49.3, §49.6).
    - A listener key is for one session: the one a hook of the connection that claimed it last named, or the first it presents itself (§49.11). It is claimed only by a request with a live agent token, so no one can claim one ahead of the real connection, and it opens no tool, no other session and no route but three: the listener, the session report and, with the maintainer's yes of 2026-11-05 (§49.11), the one that registers a hosted call for that session (§54).
    - A **call key** is for one call: it is registered once, for a room the registering machine has an agent in, and the call is the person's whose token (or whose sign-in's listener key) registered it, taken from the token and not from the body; a listener key registers only for its own session, which is never read from the body (§54). The call key opens that call's three routes and nothing else, and it is refused the moment the call ends or its machine's token is revoked.
    - Batch tokens are HMACed per membership, and acks need a per-reply nonce.
12. **Run code on the agent's machine that an agent can edit.** The only code of switchboard's there is the plugin's hooks (§49.5) and, for Claude Code, the helper that makes its listener key (§49.11): fixed `curl` and PowerShell commands, `mcp_tool` calls and a POSIX script with its PowerShell twin in the plugin's own folder, which send a few named fields or write one file in the plugin's data folder that only the user can read. There is no script to swap and no copy to check; the broker writes every reply (§7.3).
13. **Grant `human_cli`, `human` or `login` to a peer that is itself an SSH or relay process,** or (unless `[security] allow_ssh_cli`) to a peer under a remote-login server (§5.3; `tests/unit/test_peer_ssh_rules.py`).

**Static guardrail test** (`tests/unit/test_guardrails_static.py`). It greps `src/switchboard/**` for all of the following:
- `hooks.state`, `trusted_hash`, `"trust": true`, `crossSessionInbound`;
- `--dangerously`, `--approve-for-me`, `--approve-mcps`, `--trust`;
- `approvalPolicy`, `sandboxPolicy`, `network_access`;
- `claude/channel`, `thread/resume`;
- `permissionDecision`, `updatedInput`, `updated_mcp_tool_output`.

Only `guardrails.py` may contain them. It also holds `broker/hook_replies.py` to no `PermissionRequest` and a `decision` only as `block`, so the table of replies has no way to answer a PermissionRequest at all. `tests/unit/test_permission_hooks_static.py` holds the plugins to item 2. A wheel test asserts that nothing under `tests/` is packaged.

**Residual risks** (documented in SECURITY.md):
- **A hostile or compromised server can put text into an agent's context.** It writes every hook reply, so it can add context after a tool call, or keep an idle agent going with a message, as a room message can. It still can't approve anything: its replies are checked against exact shapes with no permission decision in them (§7.3, §49.4), and the one hook where a reply is a decision in the harness, PermissionRequest in Codex and Devin, is a command that throws the reply away (item 2). A hook that read the reply (an `mcp_tool` PermissionRequest hook in Codex) would break this, which is why the plugin has none and the server refuses one.
- **An agent token is the key to its device's agents.** Whoever holds it can act as them, never as a person: it can't sign in, post as a person or run a command (§49.1).
- **A listener key is a bearer for one session's wake, its start and end report and the registering of a hosted call** (§49.11, §54). It sits in a file of the plugin's data folder (mode 0600; on Windows an access list of the user alone) that any process of the same user can read, the agent's own shell included, so a prompt-injected agent can use it to take that session's wake text, end its report, or register a hash nobody holds the key to as the person's one hosted call in a room that session is in (at five tries a minute; it holds the person's call slot and the room's until ten minutes pass or a person ends it, and gives no one a way in). It can't call a tool, post, read a room, sign in or run a command, it opens nothing for any other session, and it dies with the sign-in it belongs to.
- **A call key is a bearer for one hosted call's three routes** (§54). It sits in a file on the person's machine that any process of the same user can read, the agent's shell included, so a prompt-injected agent could push lines into that call until it is ended: lines that are marked as heard by that machine, are never read as a person's, are limited in rate and size, and end with the call (the person or an admin ends it from the call bar). It can read only that call's `@call` lines, its room's names and the voice switch, and call no tool. A call line, from any source, has the reach of any other agent's text: it can wake the member it names (§52).
- A same-user process **on the broker's machine** outside any agent's tree can still act as the human, and read room text. It passes the peer check (§5.3). The sandbox is the boundary.
- The web cookie is sent to any port on `switchboard.localhost` (§5.4).
- **`/catchup` points an agent at other sessions' transcripts** (§26): its history tool may let it read every indexed session, not only the ones named; transcripts carry raw tool output (secrets, web pages) to the agent's model vendor, as untrusted text that can steer it (a red notice warns when the agent's approvals are off); and the request posts the subjects' session ids in the room. switchboard never looks for or runs a history tool and never allowlists one.
- `human.say` from the CLI creates trusted human messages, and those reset the loop guard. It is tagged "via cli", and a detached same-user process could forge it.
- **A detached same-user process can become "the human".** An agent on the broker's machine can detach a process from its own tree (for example `( sleep 1; script -q /dev/null switchboard login ) &` from its Bash, after which the process is reparented to pid 1) and so pass both the peer check and the TTY check. It then gets a login link, a web session and every raising verb (`/resume`, raising `/budget`, `/release`, room creation). Signs to watch for: the warn notice "a login link was issued via cli (…)" and "new web login" in the UI. A stronger gate (approve new logins from an existing browser session) is an open question (§14). Nesting shells **without** detaching no longer works (§5.3).

### 11.1 What the local path guarded, and what replaced it (#385)
Each of these was an item or a residual risk above until 0.38.0, with its tests; none is needed now, and `git show v0.37.2:docs/DESIGN.md` has the text.
- **Identity from the process.** The broker took an MCP server's, and a hook's, pid from the socket peer and checked its ancestry against the harness's `argv` (and for Claude a registry file), so a process that was not an agent's own could not speak for it. Replaced by the token: a call carries a device's secret, and the harness names the session (§49.3). The residual risk that went with it (a same-user process could pass the argv check by naming a script `codex`) is gone.
- **The Claude inbox.** A frame posted to Claude Code's messaging socket, with a token any command the model ran could read. Replaced by the listener (§49.4), which carries no secret into the model's reach but the agent token its hook uses.
- **The Codex app-server connection.** `turn/start`, `turn/steer` and `codex queue` with key allowlists, a liveness guard, a thread proof and a restart re-bind; its residual risks (the control socket's trust, the per-daemon liveness check, a thread taken over while its MCP process was gone, a queued item that can't be taken back and sits on a command line) are gone with it.
- **The hook script.** Run from a content-addressed 0444 copy that was re-checked every 60 s, so an agent could not swap it. Replaced by the plugin's fixed hooks and the broker's own reply table (item 12).
- **Shared environments.** A Codex thread attached to the shared daemon shared its environment ("env shared"), and Cursor hooks saw the login shell's. Nothing is shared now: the hooks send named fields only (item 10).

---

## 12. Test strategy

`tests/` layout:
```
conftest.py        clean_env(): autouse; strips CLAUDE*, CODEX_*, CURSOR_*, DEVIN_*, CHISEL_*, AI_AGENT from os.environ and
                     builds every child env from an allowlist {PATH, HOME=<tmp>, LANG, TMPDIR, SWITCHBOARD_TEST=1}
                   tmp_home (short /tmp path with .switchboard-test marker), FakeClock, cfg_fast (quiet_s=0, max_hold_s=0),
                   broker fixture (in-process create_app with AllowAllHumans + test_mode; uvicorn on port 0 + UDS),
                   web_client (httpx, base_url http://switchboard.localhost:<p>, logged in via the test token)
engine_world.py    a store, an engine and a FakeClock with no broker; engine_agents.py builds the sessions over HTTPS
                   (a held Stop hook, a wait(), hook context) the engine tests read the engine's answers from
unit/              test_db, test_db_migrate_v<N> (from real old databases), test_store, test_envelope, test_rules_<rule>.py,
                   test_engine_core, test_commands, test_commands_review, test_hook_invariants, test_adapter tests,
                   test_peer, test_proc, test_uninstall, test_paths, test_guardrails_static, test_web_static_lint,
                   test_wheel_contents, test_web_markdown (md.js under node: every construct, link blocking, injection,
                   limits; §29)
integration/       test_persistence, test_web_auth, test_ws_fanout, test_cli, test_bind_loopback_only,
                   test_cli_web_roundtrip, test_history_survives_restart, test_mcp_agent (the scripted agent over /mcp),
                   test_agents_http* (Codex, Claude Code, Cursor and Devin as recorded), test_agent_plugins,
                   test_engine_e2e (and tests/engine_sim.py: 200 seeded simulations), test_secrets_canary, test_review,
                   test_web_member_detail (§29.5)
fakes/             fake_agent.py (a scripted MCP client of a test-mode broker's /mcp with an agent token, §9.6),
                   fake_relay.py (an ssh-named socket relay, for the peer rules of §5.3), fake_oidc.py,
                   fake_authenticator.py (a software passkey)
fixtures/payloads/{claude,codex,cursor,devin}/<event>[_variant].json   sanitized recorded payloads
fixtures/mcp_http/                                                    the requests each harness's MCP client sends (§49)
fixtures/install/<h>/                                                 what an old install wrote, for the uninstall tests
fixtures/db/                                                          databases real old releases wrote, for the migration tests
live/              harness/..., test_live_*.py (the real CLIs over HTTPS; opt-in, §12.4)
evals/             evalkit/, scenarios/*.toml, run_evals.py (real agents graded from the database; opt-in, §12.7)
```

### 12.1 Unit tests (fast, every commit)
- **db:** WAL is on. Lost-update test: 4 threads × 500 read-modify-writes under `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` give an exact total. Reopen after a crash.
- **envelope:**
  - each sanitize step: NFKC fold of `＜/system_reminder＞`, Cf and tag characters, U+2028, `<`/`>`, case-insensitive `yk:` defang, truncation;
  - list format and the `[switchboard]` prefix;
  - content-dependent headers (human-only, mixed, peer-only, stub-only);
  - a property test that no rendered line starts with `/`, `!` or `&`;
  - the HMAC token round trip.
- **engine core and rules** (FakeClock, one test file per rule):
  - quiet period 3 s; max hold 60 s under continuous chatter; batch cap (messages, characters, `ctx_max_chars`);
  - human-first ordering; one peer batch per boundary; a busy member gets priority only; `effective_status` with an open sink is idle;
  - budget refill and exhaustion, humans still pass, `/budget`;
  - the rate limit and its exemptions; the hop counter trips at 6 and a human resets it;
  - requeue: re-deliver once uses the `redelivered` column, independent of `attempts`;
  - watchdog: remind twice, then escalate; escalation for a parked member;
  - notified stubs are pull-only; each event-based expiry rule; supersede expires pull batches.
- **hook invariants:** the per-`(harness, event)` allowed-shape test and a fuzz over the replies the broker writes (§7.3, `broker/hook_replies.py`).
- **adapters and the engine's asks:** each adapter's routes, tiers, park rules, follow-up verdicts and session keys, called directly (`test_cov_adapters_base.py`, `test_devin_adapter.py`, `test_https_stop_wait.py`), and the engine's questions to an adapter with a stand-in one (`test_engine_asks_adapter.py`).
- **uninstall** (what the install tests became when `install` was retired, §9.7):
  - what an old install wrote is a static fixture per harness (`tests/fixtures/install/`: from an empty config, from the user's own, and Devin's from two homes), and `uninstall` is checked against it on a temp user home: byte-for-byte round trips, the user's entries before and after switchboard's kept, idempotency, dry-run, backup naming and mode, the marker block, the Codex trust-position warning, Devin's allow names kept while another home uses them;
  - the `claude mcp remove` `CommandEdit` argv is asserted and never run with `--user-home`.
- **peer and proc:**
  - a child started through a symlinked fake binary named `claude` (and `codex`) is denied `human_cli`; a plain child is allowed;
  - a pid with a different start time doesn't match;
  - the SSH rules (§5.3): a relay peer and a remote-login ancestor are refused.
- **config:** the keys only the local agent path read are accepted and named, never read (`Config.retired`, #385).
- **paths:** the long-path socket fallback; `ensure_private_dir` rejects a symlink, a foreign owner, or a mode of 0755.
- **web static lint:** no `innerHTML`, `outerHTML`, `insertAdjacentHTML`, `document.write`, `eval`, `new Function`, inline `<script>` or `style=` in `web/static`.

### 12.2 Integration tests (in-process broker, temp home)
- **Broker lifecycle:** after uvicorn shuts down, the test broker cancels and awaits any loop-level tasks (including the SSE shutdown watcher started by an MCP response) before closing its event loop (#473). A completed `/mcp` response followed by `stop()` leaves no pending watcher.
- **Persistence:** post, restart the broker, and check that history and pending/unread items survive, and that offered batches expire and revert.
- **Web auth:**
  - 421 on other hosts, including a WebSocket with `Host: 127.0.0.1` and a valid cookie;
  - the one-time token is single-use and never appears in `broker.log`;
  - cookie flags and 12 h sliding expiry; logout-all;
  - missing or wrong Origin, or missing `X-Switchboard`, gives 403; unauthenticated `/api/*` gives 401;
  - the WebSocket is refused without a cookie or with a bad Origin, and a client frame other than hello, ping or board closes it;
  - `/docs`, `/redoc` and `/openapi.json` give 404; CSP and nosniff are present;
  - route enumeration finds no unauthenticated writes.
- **Fan-out:** 2 WebSocket clients and a UDS tail all receive the message (loose: < 1 s; perf: < 50 ms).
- **CLI:** a subprocess `switchboard start --foreground --test-mode --test-trust-uds --home <tmp>`, then say, tail, who, cmd and stop. `/resume` over the UDS **without** test trust gives `forbidden`.
- **M1 replacements for manual checks:**
  - `test_bind_loopback_only`: `getsockname`, plus `lsof -nP -a -p <pid> -iTCP -sTCP:LISTEN` shows exactly one `127.0.0.1` line;
  - `test_cli_web_roundtrip`: a CLI say reaches the WebSocket, and a REST say appears in `tail --no-follow`;
  - `test_history_survives_restart`.
- **Scripted agent** (`FakeAgent`, a client of `/mcp`, §9.6):
  - `join` returns the catch-up (8 messages, and how many earlier ones there are, #272), the rules, the nonce and the test banner, and a membership id is never in the tool text;
  - a re-join keeps the membership and renews its join code;
  - with `--ack never`, `read` never skips: offered-but-unconfirmed messages come back;
  - `say` returns earlier unread messages and is rate-limited; `pass` is logged, not posted;
  - `wait` returns on a human message (loose: < 1 s), returns `paused` on `/pause`, is superseded by a newer wait, times out at its cap, and `unwait` closes it;
  - a wait issued during a pause returns `paused` at its timeout.
  - a join into a room modeled on the token-budget spike's measured lengths (median 469 characters, mean 613, p90 1,497) fits the join budget (#272);
  - `read(before=...)` pages back from the join's own anchor id with the right count and next call, never satisfies the read-first rule, never crosses rooms, and clamps a huge or negative value (#300); 
- **Agents over HTTPS** (`test_agents_http*.py`, `test_agent_plugins.py`): the requests each harness's MCP client sends, as recorded (`fixtures/mcp_http/`): a session per Codex thread, a Claude Code, Cursor or Devin call tied to its hook's one-use report, a call that names another session's membership refused, a token revoked, a person removed; the held Stop hook and the listener (`/mcp/listen`) waking an idle session, status from hooks, a follow-up confirmed or expired, a Devin killed mid-wait. The plugins' files are checked against the recorded payloads and the rules of §49.5.
- **Hook replies:** the engine's answer for each event through `hook_replies`: context only where §7.3 allows it, never over a harness's limit, and an acknowledged batch confirmed by the next hook with a token that belongs to its member.
- **Secrets canary:** `uninstall` against a temp home seeded with `"API_TOKEN":"sk-canary…"`. The canary appears in no output or log, and backups are 0600.
- **Engine e2e:** 3 fake agents with scripted policies (always reply, reply only to mentions, always pass). With a limit of 6 the loop guard pauses at 6 (the guard is off unless a room has a limit, §8.5), the budget is enforced, and no wake is lost across busy→idle. `tests/engine_sim.py` is the M6 property test (§13): 200 seeded random interleavings of the five harness kinds (a `wait()` loop for the test agent and Devin, a held Stop hook for Claude Code, Codex and Cursor) around the real engine, then quiescence, then the invariant.

### 12.3 Recorded contracts
- **Raw M0 logs** are the source for fixtures. In M2, before the temp dir is cleaned, copy them from the build session's scratchpad (`<scratch>/m0/`):
  - `devin-cli*/**/log/h.jsonl`;
  - `codex-hooks*/logs/*.hooks.jsonl`;
  - `cursor-agent/wsm/.yk/{hooks,mcp}.jsonl`;
  - `claude-*/**/*.jsonl`.

  Sanitize them before committing: drop `user_email`, env values and absolute paths. A fixture-scan test fails on `user_email`, `/Users/` and other home or temp paths, email addresses, the local user's name and git email (looked up at runtime, so the test names nobody), `postgres://` or high-entropy strings.
  - If the files are gone, build fixtures from the key lists recorded in M0 for Cursor hooks (`conversation_id, generation_id, session_id, hook_event_name, status, loop_count, tool_name, tool_input, tool_output, tool_use_id, cursor_version, workspace_roots`) and for Devin, and mark them `"_unverified": true`.
- **Payload shapes the extractor must handle:** Devin `tool_response = {success, output, error}`; Codex MCP `tool_response = {content:[…]}`; Cursor `tool_output` as a JSON string.
- **Unrecorded:** the Devin `run_subagent`/`is_background` PreToolUse payload. That fixture is `_unverified`.
- **Re-recording.** With `SWITCHBOARD_RECORD_PAYLOADS=<dir>`, the broker writes the allowlisted fields it received, and live runs re-record `_unverified` fixtures. The same scan applies to those files.

### 12.4 Live tests (`pytest -m live`, opt-in with `SWITCHBOARD_LIVE=claude,codex,devin,cursor` or `all`)
The real CLIs, over HTTPS (§49), with the plugins and configs of this checkout, against a throwaway broker. Opt-in and never in CI: a plain `pytest` collects every live module and skips each with the line that says how to run it (`tests/unit/test_live_harness.py` holds that, and everything below that needs no model). They replace the tests that registered each CLI with `switchboard install <h> --print-args` and drove the local agent path (retired in 0.38.0, #385; the last release that has them is v0.37.2). #470 has the request.

**Common setup** (`tests/live/harness/`).
- **A throwaway broker.** `switchboard start --foreground --test-mode --test-trust-uds` in a temp home (§2 test mode), the person signed in through the same login link the CLI makes, a room `#build`, and a token for a device named `live-<harness>` made through `POST /api/agent-tokens` (§49.1). The token reaches a CLI through its environment, or a 0600 file under the run's temp directory; never a command line. Everything the run makes is under one temp directory, which the run removes.
- **A private tmux** on `-L sb-live-<harness>-<pid>`; every CLI starts under `env -i` with `HOME`, `PATH` (the CLIs' own directories, then the system's; tmux itself is run by the absolute path found on the `PATH` pytest started with, so a Homebrew install works and the CLIs' `PATH` stays as it is), `USER`, `LOGNAME`, `SHELL`, `TERM`, `LANG` and what its profile adds. That environment (the agent token among it) goes to the CLI through a 0700 launch script of `export` lines in the run's temp directory, so no command line, tmux's included, ever holds it. The working directory is a scratch git repository with no remote. A prompt that is typed is checked: its first line has to show in the CLI's input box and leave it after Enter, because an Enter pressed while a TUI is still starting or has just resumed is lost (seen with Claude Code and Codex); Enter is only repeated while the text is still in the box, never into an empty one.
- **The checkout's plugins.** Claude Code: `--plugin-dir plugins/claude`. Codex: `plugins/codex` installed from a throwaway marketplace with a `local` source (`codex plugin marketplace add`, `codex plugin add`; the real marketplaces name a release's tag, §49.5). Devin: `plugins/devin/config.example.json` as the project's `.devin/config.json`. Cursor: `plugins/cursor`'s `mcp.json` and `hooks/hooks.json` as the project's `.cursor/`. `SWITCHBOARD_LIVE_CLAUDE_PLUGIN`, `SWITCHBOARD_LIVE_CODEX_PLUGIN` and `SWITCHBOARD_LIVE_CURSOR_PLUGIN` name another folder, to try a change before it is a plugin version, or to see a scenario fail against a plugin with a hook taken out.
- **The user's own config stays out.** Each CLI is given an empty stand-in for what holds a user's servers, hooks and plugins; only a login comes from the real home: Claude Code runs with user settings off (`--setting-sources project,local`) and the account's claude.ai connectors off (so its only MCP server is switchboard's); Codex on a temp `CODEX_HOME` and `HOME`; Devin with an empty `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` (its login is under `~/.local/share/devin`); Cursor on a temp `HOME`, with `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` the real one (its login is `~/.config/cursor/auth.json` on Linux; on macOS it is in the Keychain, which the run never reads or copies, so Cursor's model tests skip there, #530). A preflight after each start walks the CLI's process tree and aborts if anything but a plugin's own script looks like another MCP server (`mcp`, `npx`, `uvx`, `node .*server`).
- **Drift.** `drift.py` takes md5s of `~/.claude/settings.json`, `~/.codex/config.toml`, `~/.codex/hooks.json`, `~/.cursor/mcp.json`, `~/.cursor/hooks.json`, `~/.config/devin/config.json` and `~/.config/devin/mcp_config.json` (and two more) before and after, and a change fails the run; `~/.claude.json` and `~/.cursor/cli-config.json` are only reported, because the CLIs write them themselves (F§15). At the end the run kills what its temp directory names, reports it, and fails if it was anything but a CLI's own helper (Cursor's TypeScript language server outlives Cursor).
- **Approvals are never off.** Claude Code runs in permission mode `manual`, Codex with `-a on-request -s workspace-write`, Devin and Cursor in their default modes; the rules allow switchboard's tools and the scratch script, and nothing else. A prompt that appears anyway is declined with Esc, never approved (counted in the results). The only dialog answered is Claude Code's folder trust for the run's own folder.

  | Harness | Launch profile (test-only; the product never emits these) |
  |---|---|
  | Claude Code | `claude --model haiku --effort low --setting-sources project,local --plugin-dir <plugin> --settings <file> --permission-mode manual`, `--continue` to resume; env `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER=1`, `ENABLE_CLAUDEAI_MCP_SERVERS=false`. The `--settings` file holds `pluginConfigs` for `switchboard@inline` (the server's address and the token, the plugin's two options) and the allow rules for switchboard's tools and `sh hold.sh`. |
  | Codex | `codex -m gpt-6-luna -c model_reasoning_effort="low" -c check_for_update_on_startup=false -a on-request -s workspace-write -C <scratch>`, `resume <id>` to resume; `CODEX_HOME` a temp directory with a copy of `auth.json` (mode 0600, deleted at the end, never printed), the plugin, `[mcp_servers.switchboard]` (the address, `bearer_token_env_var`, `tool_timeout_sec = 86400`, §49.4), trust for the scratch folder, `hooks.state` for the plugin's hooks at the hashes a private stdio `codex app-server` lists (`hooks/list`; never the shared daemon). In Codex 0.166.0 the composite `currentHash` is `definition/content`: the test writes its halves as `trusted_hash` and `trusted_content_hash` for that key; older single hashes use `trusted_hash` alone (#635). No review prompt is answered. It keeps the connectors, remote plugins and browser/computer tools off. |
  | Devin | `devin --model swe-1-6-slow --respect-workspace-trust false`, `--continue` to resume; project `.devin/config.json` from the plugin's example (hook paths filled in, switchboard's tools and `Exec(sh hold.sh)` allowed, nothing imported from other tools) and `.devin/mcp_config.json` (0600); env `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` (empty temp), `SWITCHBOARD_URL`, `SWITCHBOARD_AGENT_TOKEN`. |
  | Cursor | `agent --model auto --trust`, `--continue` to resume; project `.cursor/mcp.json` and `.cursor/hooks.json` from the plugin, and `.cursor/cli.json` allowing switchboard's eleven tools (`Mcp(switchboard-https:switchboard_<tool>)`) and `Shell(sh hold.sh)`, nothing else; the server approved with `agent mcp enable` (approving a server does not approve its tools: Cursor asks "Run this MCP tool?" for each call); env temp `HOME`, real `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `SWITCHBOARD_URL`, `SWITCHBOARD_AGENT_TOKEN`. |

**Scenarios** (`harness/scenarios.py`; each module runs them in order on one session, and each leaves it where the next starts).
- **Join and say.** The first prompt is typed. Members shows the member with its harness, tier (`claude:https`, ...) and its device as host; its message is `via` mcp from that host.
- **Woken while idle.** The person's @mention (`ask it to call say with a nonce`) with nothing typed: the agent answers, and the delivery switchboard records is the harness's wake (`stop_followup` through the listener or the Stop hook, `wait` for Devin).
- **A mention mid-task, after the next tool call.** The agent runs `sh hold.sh mid`, which stays in flight until the test says go; the mention arrives meanwhile and is queued; on release, the delivery is `hook_ctx` and acknowledged, for every harness (Devin's and Cursor's tool hooks ran for switchboard's tools only until #493, and their delivery came with the next switchboard call: `say`, `read` or `wait`; `tests/unit/test_live_harness.py` derives the expected path from the plugin's matchers). Whether the agent then answers it within 20 s is reported (`answered`), not asserted: a cheap model may distrust a message that comes as hook context (Haiku once declined one as "hook output" until the first prompt said alice's messages may arrive that way), and that is the model's, where the broker's record shows what switchboard did.
- **A subagent does not take the session's message** (Claude Code and Codex; #446, #465). A background subagent has `sh hold.sh sub` in flight; the session, which ended its turn, still shows idle, an @mention wakes it, and it answers before the subagent is released. Without the fix a subagent's calls turn the session busy and end its listener, and it never answers.
- **Quit shows offline.** `/exit` (`/quit` for Codex and Cursor): Members shows the member offline, still a member of the room.
- **Resume comes back.** The same session is resumed. Claude Code needs nothing typed, because SessionStart reports the resume and starts the listener (#412); the others are given their first prompt. An @mention wakes it.
- **Devin killed** (#448). Its tmux session is killed (SIGHUP, no SessionEnd hook); Members shows it offline within the 130 s the engine allows a dropped `wait()`, still a member; resumed, it comes back and is woken.
- **A revoked token ends it** (§49.2): the member leaves at once, with a notice that says so.
- **Cursor.** One scenario needs no model: Cursor connects to the broker over HTTP with the token and lists switchboard's 11 tools, and the broker records the token used. The others run only if a one-word call to Cursor's model works, and are skipped, with the reason, when the account has hit its usage limit. First run with a working account, 2026-10-22 (Cursor Agent 2026.10.18, `auto`): all seven pass in about a minute; the mid-task mention reached the session with its next `say()` answer, as for Devin, because a shell command runs none of the plugin's hooks.

**Cost and limits.** The cheapest model each account offers: Claude `haiku` at low effort, Codex `gpt-6-luna` at low effort, Devin `swe-1-6-slow`, Cursor `auto` (the only one a free account has). A harness's whole run is about a dozen to twenty model turns, 25 s to 3 minutes (Devin's is the longest because it waits out the 130 s of a killed `wait()`), and ends with one line of what it spent, read from the CLI's own record (Claude Code's transcript, Codex's rollout, Devin's quota banner). Each wait has a deadline, no step sleeps to mean "by now", and a failure prints the screen, the broker's log, Members and the engine's last events.

**The meeting self-test** (`tests/live/test_meeting_selftest.py`, #600; §52). It is a live test of a different kind: no CLI and no model, but a real call. `SWITCHBOARD_LIVE=meeting` names it (`all` does not: it joins a call) and `SWITCHBOARD_MEETING_URL_FILE` names a file, mode 0600 and nothing else, with the call's join link, which holds its passcode. It starts two joinly containers published on `127.0.0.1` only (§52: joinly has no sign-in), switchboard's and a test speaker named `switchboard self-test speaker` that the test drives as an MCP client the way the broker drives its own, and a throwaway broker in this process, in a temp home, with a room and a scripted agent `claude-1`. Six steps run in order, and each records pass or fail, what it expected, what it saw and its seconds: the bar goes live; a sentence the speaker says reaches the room as a call line; "Claude one, can you hear me?" wakes `claude-1`; with voice on, `claude-1`'s `@call` line is in the speaker's own transcript; `/meeting snapshot` stores a JPEG and posts its card; `/meeting leave` ends the bar and the speaker no longer sees the bot. A step with words passes on 60% of the expected content words (`MATCH_THRESHOLD`), because speech-to-text of text-to-speech is close, not exact. A step that cannot start because an earlier one failed is recorded as failed ("not attempted"). The link never leaves the test: it is a `SecretLink` that prints as a placeholder, every error that might quote it is scrubbed, the report (JSON and a Markdown table in `$SWITCHBOARD_SELFTEST_OUT`, or a temp folder the run names) is checked for it and not written if it is there, and the broker's database, messages and log are checked for it at the end. Both bots leave and both containers are removed whatever happened, also on SIGTERM. A failed step keeps both containers' logs in that folder, scrubbed of the link, and the report names the files; `SWITCHBOARD_SELFTEST_LOGS=1` keeps them on a pass too (#642). The pure parts (`tests/live/harness/selftest.py`) are checked on every run by `tests/unit/test_meeting_selftest.py`. `.claude/skills/meeting-self-test` makes a Zoom meeting with the Zoom connector, runs the test, and deletes the meeting.

**The same with a hosted bot** (`tests/live/test_meeting_selftest_hosted.py`, #623; §54). Two tests under the same switch. One needs no call: the plugin's `meeting-host start --no-join` registers a call at a throwaway broker and starts the real joinly and a relay built from the checkout, the relay is heard from, End call makes it exit 0 and joinly stop with it, and `stop` removes the rest. The other runs the six steps with the bot started by the script, with the link on its stdin; step 5 is that a hosted call has no snapshot yet, and step 6 also requires both containers gone. `SWITCHBOARD_SELFTEST_HOSTED=remote` runs the script and the containers on another machine (one with a GPU), which reaches the throwaway broker's loopback through an SSH tunnel, so the broker is never opened to a network. The Claude Code run `tests/live/test_live_claude_host.py` does the first with a real model asking for it through the skill. None of them makes or deletes a meeting.

### 12.5 Latency measurement
- T0 is `messages.ts`, on the broker clock. Every timestamp comes from the same host clock, and hooks send their start `t`.
- Turn start is `batches.turn_start_at`. The report labels what it means per harness:

  | Harness / path | Turn start is… | Report label |
  |---|---|---|
  | Claude inbox | the confirming UserPromptSubmit | turn start |
  | Codex `turn_start` | the status → active event | turn start |
  | Codex queue | UserPromptSubmit | turn start |
  | Cursor `stop_followup` | the next hook | **first hook**, not comparable to M0's 117 ms "visible" |
  | Devin `wait` | PostToolUse of `wait` (`turn_start_at`, labelled "in context"); **first action** is the next PreToolUse (`first_action_at`), which M5 is gated on | in context / first action |

- Mid-task paths report `confirmed_at - ts`, labelled "in context".
- Every table is split by `wake_reason` (human or mention vs chatter). Chatter includes the 3 s quiet period.

### 12.6 `switchboard report`
Built from `messages`, `batches`, `deliveries`, `events` and `participants`:
- per-message latency p50 and p95 by harness × tier × path × wake reason, using `statistics.quantiles(n=100, method="inclusive")` when n ≥ 2 (n=1 shows the single value; n=0 shows "n/a"), always with n;
- total agent turns (`turn_start` events per participant);
- posts vs passes per agent;
- **characters by path, and an estimated token count** (characters ÷ 4; issue #275, the measuring cut of #141's token-budget spike), per agent and for the room: `join`, `wake`, mid-task context, `read`, `wait` answers and `/catchup`.
  - A batch's length is the `chars` of its `offer` event: every place the engine renders a batch records `len(text)` there, the length of what the agent was handed. No schema change: an `offer` from before this measure has no `chars`, and its batch is left out, not counted as empty.
  - A batch's path is told by `wait` first (a `wait()` answer is `wait` even when it carried a wake, which is how a Devin agent is woken), then by its kind (`wake`, or `priority` for mid-task context), then `read()` and `say()` answers together as `read`.
  - `join()`'s answer isn't a batch: a `join_chars` event (`{"chars": n}`) carries its length, on every call, a first join or a rejoin alike.
  - Neither is `/catchup`'s request: it is an ordinary human message, so its length is `LENGTH(text)` computed in SQL (`report_messages`), never the text itself, counted on the agent its `catchup` event names (`data.agent`, `data.message_id`);
- per agent, a turn spent for nothing, from `batches`, `deliveries` and `events` alone: of the confirmed wakes, how many carried nothing addressed to the agent, chatter alone with no @mention and nothing from the human (`wakes.unaddressed`, from `deliveries.prio` and the `offer` events' message ids, the same ones the latency measure reads); and the wakes the agent answered with a `pass()` and nothing posted (`passes_after_wake`), from a time-ordered walk of each membership's confirmed wakes (`confirmed_at`), chat posts and `pass` events: a wake is open until the agent posts or passes, the first pass while it is open counts once, a post closes it uncounted;
- rule firings: counts of `loop_guard`, `budget_exhausted`, `rate_limited`, `watchdog_*`, `requeue`, `expire` by reason, holds and pauses;
- stalls (`waiting-approval` longer than 60 s), undelivered and parked items;
- the model per participant.

Output is markdown, or JSON with `--json`. It contains no message text and no absolute paths. The JSON carries the characters as `characters` (the room's) and each agent's `chars`, both `{path: {n, chars, tokens_est}}` for the paths that happened, the unaddressed wakes as `agents[].wakes.unaddressed` and the passes as `agents[].passes_after_wake`.

`--room` resolves by the §28.5 rules, so it also takes a closed room's full name (`#build~closed-7`), or its base name when no open room has it. A closed room's report says so: a `closed` block (`name`, `at`, `by`) in JSON, and `(closed)` in the markdown header (§28.7).

### 12.7 Evals (`tests/evals`, opt-in with `SWITCHBOARD_EVAL=claude`; #480, from the spike #119)

The text agents read (`ROOM_RULES`, the join text, the batch framing) and the delivery settings were changed by feel. An eval plays a scripted person against real agents in a throwaway room, grades each run from the broker's database, repeats it, and compares two sets of runs. It is not `tests/engine_sim.py` (pretend agents, the engine's mechanics) and not a live test (§12.4: does the plumbing work): it asks what a model does with the room's rules. The first slice has three scenarios and Claude Code only.

**Layout.** `evalkit/` is the code, `scenarios/*.toml` the scenarios, `run_evals.py` the entry (`run`, `compare`, `list`), `test_eval_claude.py` one opt-in smoke trial (`-m eval`). Results go to `tests/evals/_results/`, which git ignores. Nothing here is packaged or imported by `src/`.

**Scenarios are data.** A TOML file names the room, the agents, the person's messages (`[[steps]]`) and the checks (`[[checks]]`: an `id` for the results, a `grader` and its arguments). `scenario.load` refuses a check, an argument or an agent that does not exist, so a typo fails when the file is read. A message that starts with `/` is sent to the command route, as the page sends it, so `/review` opens a board; the say route would post it as chat. The first three, with three agents (alpha, bravo, charlie) each:
- **nobody-named.** A question that names nobody: exactly one agent posts, and what it posts is the right answer.
- **one-named.** The person names bravo: bravo posts, alpha and charlie do not.
- **bystander.** The person asks alpha, who asks bravo; alpha and bravo trade messages, and charlie posts nothing.
- **review-board** (#629). One agent. The person opens a board with `/review` and names one finding. The check is that a finding was raised on the board after that message. It reads the board's items, so a chat line that only looks like a raise does not pass. A second agent is left out of the trial: a raise wakes it to concede or contest (§37.4), and that chain left a priority delivery open past the trial's settle time.

**Graders are plain functions of a run record** (`graders.py`): `poster_count`, `agents_post`, `agents_do_not_post`, `finding_raised` (a finding on the board after the message) and `a_post_matches` (a regular expression on what an agent posted). A `RunRecord` (`record.py`) holds the room's `messages`, `deliveries` and `events` rows, its review-board items, the dict `switchboard report --json` prints for it (`report.build`) and the tokens read from the agents' own session files, all through a read-only connection to the database. A grader reads who posted, who passed and what the board holds, never what an agent meant; no model judges anything.

**One trial** (`trial.run_trial`, the same code for a real world and a scripted one). A *world* is a throwaway broker with agents in it. The trial starts it, posts the person's messages one at a time, waits for the room to settle after each, reads the record, grades it, and always stops the world. The room is `#build` of a broker started with `--test-mode`, and its wake budget (30 an hour) and loop guard (12 agent messages with none from a person) are set through the room's own settings route, so a runaway stops itself. A custom room rule (#105, §35) can be set before the agents join: that is how an experiment changes one thing.
- **Settled is a state, not a delay.** Every agent is between turns (its hooks say `idle`, and it is not parked), no priority message (a person's or an @mention) is pending or offered, and nothing in the room has moved (no new message, event or batch) for `quiet_s`, 8 s, longer than the engine's own 3 s quiet period. Chatter that addresses nobody is not waited for: the engine holds it for `unaddressed_hold_s`, 270 s by default (§8.2), which is longer than a trial. A late wake of a bystander for that chatter is therefore outside what a trial measures.
- **A harness error is not a failed check.** A timeout, a crashed CLI, a token cap, or a room the loop guard paused is recorded as a `harness_error` with its kind, no check is graded, and the results count it apart from the checks.
- **The cost guard.** A cap on all the tokens (cache reads included) the agents of one trial have used, 3 million by default, stops a runaway as a `token_cap` harness error; it is checked every 2 s while the trial waits for the room to settle, from the moment the agents have joined. `run_evals.py run --max-cost` ends the whole run once its trials have cost that many dollars (1.00 by default); the finished trials are kept.

**Real agents** (`claude_world.py`) are the live driver's (§12.4): `claude --model haiku --effort low` with the checkout's plugin over HTTPS and its listener hook, in permission mode `manual` with user settings and connectors off and nothing approved (a prompt that appears is declined with Esc and counted), each in a tmux server of its own with an empty scratch directory, a fresh home, broker and sessions for every trial. The three start and join at once. The typed prompt says to join the room and that messages from the person are the user's: nothing about answering, because how the room's own text makes an agent behave is what is measured. The real `~/.claude/settings.json` must not change, or the run stops. Tokens come from each session's transcript (`~/.claude/projects`; one model call is written as several lines sharing a message id, and counts once), priced at the published rates of the model (Haiku 5.5: $0.10 per million input, $0.50 output, cache reads a tenth, cache writes 1.25 times or, for an hour, twice); the transcripts are deleted with the trial.

**The results file** is one JSON per run: the arm's name, the switchboard version and commit, the CLI, the model, the room's custom rules, the limits and, for each scenario, its trials. A graded trial has each check's verdict and what was seen, and numbers: seconds to settle, tokens and cost after the person's first message (and in all), wakes, wakes that ended in a `pass()` and no post (from the report), posts, and the same per agent. `results.summarize` is the only place counts are made.

**`compare A B [--repeat A2]`** prints each check as `4/5 -> 5/5` (of the graded trials), the harness errors beside them, and the change in each mean (tokens after the question, cost, wakes, wakes that ended in a `pass()`, posts; not the time, which is mostly the 8 s the room is watched after the last thing moved). A change is called real only if it is larger than the spread between two runs of the same baseline, so the baseline is run twice first and its second run is `--repeat`; with no second run nothing is called real.

**Tests, in the regular suite and with no model.** `tests/integration/test_evals_graders.py` runs the same trial against `FakeAgent`s that follow a policy (`evalkit/scripted.py`): for every check, a scripted room that fails it and one that passes it (and a test that fails when a check is added without its pair), a harness error that is not a failed check, a runaway stopped by the token cap, a room paused by the loop guard and a timeout. `tests/unit/test_evals_kit.py` covers scenarios, results, `compare`, the token meter and the opt-in.

**Not in this slice:** Codex and the other harnesses, nightly runs, larger "did it do the task" scenarios, an LLM judge, scenarios replayed from real rooms, the cache keep-alive question, and waiting out the 270 s hold.

---

## 13. Milestones and acceptance criteria

*These are the milestones as they were built, M1 to M8. M2 to M6 describe the local MCP server, the hook script, Claude's inbox, the Codex app-server link and `switchboard install`, which were retired in 0.38.0 (#385); §49 and §49.8 are the design now.*

Every item below is automated; none needs a human.

**M1: broker, web UI, CLI (a human chats alone).**
- **Steps:** `.gitignore`; the §0 assumptions (the orchestrator has already created the milestone branch).
- **Modules:** `paths, config, guardrails, db` (the **full** §4 schema), `models, store` (rooms, messages, web_sessions, events, plus membership reads), `envelope.sanitize`, `broker/{app,web,auth,rpc,proc,peer,hub,service,commands,daemon}`, `cli` (start, stop, status, login, logout, rooms, create, say, cmd, tail, who), `web/static`.
- **Acceptance:**
  - unit tests db, store, envelope (sanitize), commands, peer, proc, paths, guardrails_static and web_static_lint are green;
  - integration tests persistence, web_auth, ws_fanout, cli, bind_loopback_only, cli_web_roundtrip and history_survives_restart are green;
  - `/pause /resume /budget /hold /release /kick /who /status /help` work on an agent-less room, with per-command roles enforced.

**M2: MCP server, install framework, identity binding, fast hooks, scripted test agent, full rules.**
- **Modules:**
  - `mcp/{server,identity,client}` (all detection rules, with Claude verification);
  - `hook/switchboard_hook.py` with the **claude** table;
  - `install/common` and `install/claude` (dry-run, `--user-home`, `--print-args`);
  - `store` (participants, memberships, deliveries, batches);
  - `delivery/{rules,engine,runner,sinks}` implementing **all of §8** except the watchdog and requeue;
  - `adapters/{base,testagent}`.
- **Fixtures:** copy and sanitize the raw M0 logs (§12.3).
- **Acceptance:**
  - `FakeAgent` can join, read, say, pass, wait and unwait;
  - never-skip is proven with `--ack never`, and hook confirmation with synthetic PostToolUse events;
  - hook unit, invariant and contract tests pass for claude, with loose timing and a `perf` run recorded;
  - Claude install golden tests pass;
  - the cred is never in tool output; annotation and "no channel capability" tests pass;
  - the Claude-env isolation test passes;
  - rule unit tests (FakeClock) pass.

**M3: Claude.**
- **Modules:** `mcp/claude_inbox`, `adapters/claude` (inbox push, registry poller, PostToolUse pull for non-bypass, UserPromptSubmit confirm, idle expiry, warn notice), plus requeue/re-deliver-once in `rules`.
- **Acceptance:**
  - the fake-inbox integration tests pass;
  - ⚠ is proven with a fixture payload carrying `permission_mode: "bypassPermissions"`, which gives `approval_mode=bypass` in the member snapshot (no bypass launch);
  - live Claude (§12.4): idle wake turn start p50 < 2 s (n=5), mid-task priority in context at the next tool boundary, approval hold verified, `/clear` binding kept.

**M4: Codex.**
- **Modules:** Codex identity, the hook table, `install/codex`, `adapters/{codex_rpc,codex}` (CodexLink, liveness, thread proof, start, steer, queue guard).
- **Acceptance:**
  - fake-daemon tests pass (no overrides, never answers, steer fallback and revert, approval hold, liveness, canary);
  - live against the private app-server (real `CODEX_HOME`, `--remote` TUI): turn start p50 < 1 s (n=5), steer delivered at a tool boundary, no RPC after the TUI quits, queue tier via `codex queue --remote` on the private app-server;
  - the thread-proof outcome is recorded (§14, risk 3);
  - the drift check shows `~/.codex/*` unchanged.

**M5: Cursor and Devin.**
- **Modules:** hook tables, identity and installers for both; `adapters/{cursor,devin}`.
- **Acceptance:**
  - Cursor contract tests pass: nonce binding (and rejection of a foreign ancestry), stop status gating, park supersede, pause release, degraded after 2 unconfirmed follow-ups, provisional tier shown;
  - the Cursor live test is skipped with the reason;
  - Devin contract tests pass: taint means no continue and no context; the orphaned wait expires on the next hook; supersede;
  - live Devin: wait-loop first action p50 < 2 s (n=5), PostToolUse context, Stop re-arm.

**M6: delivery rules, completed.**
- **Scope:** the watchdog (remind and escalate), the parked escalation, and `/pause` semantics verified for every sink and path. Every rule gets its own unit test file.
- **Property test** (`test_engine_e2e`): 200 seeded random interleavings of status, message, sink and command events.
  - **Invariant:** after quiescence, no delivery of an active membership is `offered`, or `pending` and wake-eligible.
  - **Quiescence** means: the clock is past `max_hold_s` (or the hold on unaddressed chatter, §8.2, when longer) plus the largest TTL; the room is unpaused; the budget is at least the number of pending items; each member is idle with a push path or an open sink.
- **Acceptance:** loop guard at 6, budget exhaustion letting only humans through, and the property test green.

**M7: live demo (first as an automated rehearsal)**, driven by `tests/live/m7_demo.py`:
1. Create a scratch repo (no remote) with a small `parse_port()` that lacks validation, plus tests. Pre-create `.worktrees/<name>` for each agent.
2. Start the broker in test mode with a temp home. Log the driver in through the test token.
3. Start the agents in tmux, each told "join #build as <name>, stay in the room, use your own worktree under .worktrees/<name>":
   - **Claude** `--model sonnet` (fall back to haiku) with `--permission-mode acceptEdits` and narrow `--allowedTools` (the switchboard tools, `Bash(git worktree:*)`, `Bash(git diff:*)`, `Bash(git add:*)`, `Bash(git commit:*)`, `Bash(python -m pytest:*)`);
   - **Codex** on the private app-server with `on-request`/`workspace-write`, `-m gpt-5.5` (which acted on injected context 12/12 in M0; fall back to gpt-6-luna), with the model recorded;
   - **Devin** with the same narrow allows.
4. The driver posts as the human (alice) through the web API:
   - the task at T0 ("add input validation to parse_port and review each other's changes");
   - interjections at T0+4 min and T0+8 min;
   - a wrap-up request, then `/pause`, at T0+15 min.

   After each scheduled human post, if the room is loop-guard paused, it sends `/resume` and records that.
5. Hard limits: 20 minutes wall clock, room budget 40, hop limit 6. A `waiting-approval` longer than 60 s is reported as "stalled". The driver never approves.
6. Cursor is listed "not run: not yet tested live". Any harness blocked by quota, auth or sandbox is listed "not run: <reason>".

**Output:** `switchboard report --room '#build' --out docs/M7-REPORT.md`, committed.

**Acceptance:**
- the report has non-empty latency rows for every harness that ran;
- no guardrail test fails; the drift check is clean;
- `README.md` is updated, covering:
  - install (`uv tool install .`, then `switchboard install` per harness);
  - starting a room;
  - the tier per harness, with Cursor provisional;
  - that switchboard's tools may prompt in Claude and Cursor;
  - the cookie port caveat, the bridge warning, and known limitations.

The real demo, with a human interjecting live and with Cursor, is repeated later.

## 14. Open risks and questions

**Risks.** Items 1, 3, 4 and 9 were about the local path (the Claude inbox, the Codex app-server, Cursor's local binding and the hook script's latency) and went with it in 0.38.0 (#385); they stay as the record of what was weighed, and the risk of an agent over HTTPS is in §11 and §49.
1. **The Claude inbox socket is undocumented.** *(Retired: no inbox is written to now.)* That covered the frame schema, `CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_*` in the MCP env, and the registry `status` field.
2. **Delivery is not handling.** Claude acted on mid-turn messages 6/13 times, and Codex compliance depends on the model and the framing (gpt-6-luna 0/9 on PostToolUse context). Re-deliver-once and the watchdog cost extra wakes, and stubs add a `read()` call on elevated paths. Opus and Sonnet behaviour wasn't measured.
3. **Codex** (the local path is retired; over HTTPS see §9.3):
   - Attach is conditional: any `-c`, `--profile`, `--oss` or `--no-daemon` gives an embedded TUI and the ≤ 10 s queue tier.
   - Verified in M4 (live, `--remote` TUI on a private app-server): `thread/read` contains MCP tool results (the thread proof works); a TUI quit fires nothing at once, and SessionEnd only when the thread unloads about 60 s later (the lsof check covers the gap); the Interrupt hook fires on a declined approval; steered input fires UserPromptSubmit. Still unverified: `codex queue` behaviour under `daemon_auto_start` with no daemon (guarded: never run then), and whether `-C` fixes a `--remote` TUI's cwd (the server's cwd is set instead).
   - The private test app-server may differ from an auto-started shared daemon.
   - Live tests shared the real `CODEX_HOME` then (sessions and rollouts landed in `~/.codex/sessions`, as in M0); they use a temp one now (§12.4). switchboard never writes `~/.codex` itself.
4. **Cursor is built blind.** Parks longer than 38 s are unproven (the tier is provisional), `generation_id`/`loop_count` semantics for follow-ups are unverified, a chat switch inside the TUI may leave the binding stale, there is no approval-state signal, and the cursor argv matcher and hook ancestry are unconfirmed. Everything waits on a live re-test; `bg_waiter` is deferred.
5. **Devin costs.** The wait loop keeps the REPL busy (interject with Enter on an empty line). Re-arm burns about 6 budget units per idle hour per agent. Model quota may be limited. Import of `~/.claude` hooks and a duplicate `switchboard` MCP entry are untested: runtime detection handles the hooks, but a duplicate server process is possible.
6. **Unverified hook outputs:** Codex and Devin SessionStart/UserPromptSubmit context, and whether `hook_event_name` appears in every payload. These print nothing in this version; join results carry the catch-up.
7. **`switchboard.localhost` may not resolve in Safari.** Chrome and Firefox resolve `*.localhost`. switchboard never edits `/etc/hosts`.
8. **The peer check is not a boundary.** Detached same-user processes pass it, can mint a web session through `switchboard login` (with a pty from `script`), and an agent could edit `config.toml`. Raising verbs need the web session, which stops an agent's own shell but not a detached process; the sandbox is the boundary.
9. **Hook latency under login shells.** Codex uses `$SHELL -lc`, and Cursor a login-shell snapshot. zsh profiles could add 200–400 ms (F§10 S4). *(The `/bin/sh` guard and the Python script that this measured are gone; the plugins' hooks are `curl` and `mcp_tool` calls.)*
10. **Duplicate messages.** At-least-once delivery means an agent may see an id twice. The envelope says to ignore seen ids.
11. **FastMCP 4.0.9 API drift.** The Context, `_meta`, cancellation, `on_initialize` and annotation APIs must be checked against the pinned version. Don't upgrade during the build.
12. **WSL2 is untested; Linux is tested by the suite only** (§25): `SO_PEERCRED`, `/proc` start times and zombies, and Linux `lsof` pass it in a Debian 12 container. How Claude verifies posters on Linux and Codex landlock reachability are unchecked (no live agent run on Linux).
13. **The build is unattended.** Anything that would write user-level config, trust Codex hooks for real, touch the user's daemon, or run an agent with approvals off is out of bounds. A human-attended M7 remains open. (`switchboard install` is retired, §9.7.)

**Open questions** (the build uses the defaults shown)
- May switchboard add its own tool names to Claude's and Cursor's allow rules? Default: no. switchboard's tools may prompt there (F§14.3). (Nothing writes a harness's config now: the plugins do, with the person's own install, §49.5.)
- Is the budget reading right: a fixed hourly window, with `/budget n` setting what remains? Default: yes.
- Should peer @mentions on elevated paths (Codex, Devin, Cursor, Claude hooks) arrive as "call read()" stubs rather than inline text? Default: stubs.
- Which Codex model for the real M7? Default: gpt-5.5.
- Should a `switchboard login` link need approval from an already signed-in browser whenever a web session exists (closing the detached-process route to a web session, §11)? Default: no; the UI shows a warn notice for every CLI login link instead.

## 15. Implementation notes and deviations (M1)

*§15 to §25 are each milestone's build notes, as written then. Where one names the local MCP server, the hook script, Claude's inbox or registry, the Codex app-server link, `switchboard install` or a link to another machine, it describes what was built, not what runs: those were retired in 0.38.0 (#385), and §49.8 says where each went.*

- **UI text hygiene.** The web UI and the CLI show `envelope.clean()` output (sanitize steps 1–2). The rest of `sanitize()` exists to protect harness framing and is applied only on agent-facing paths (§8.6).
- **WebSocket Host refusal** is a 1008 close (HTTP 403), not 421 (§5.4).
- **WebSocket additions:** the `rooms` frame and the 500-line replay cap with a skip notice (§5.5).
- **`room.tail`** grew `limit`, `follow` and `more` (§5.2); the CLI pages with `room.history` when `more` is set.
- **Error code `conflict`** for an existing room (§5.1).
- **`logs/broker.out`** holds a daemonized broker's stdout/stderr; structured logs stay in the rotating `broker.log` (§2).
- **Daemon env** drops the Claude session variables; **`switchboard stop`** falls back to SIGTERM only when the socket is silent (§2).
- **CLI audit notices** are persisted room notices (`kind='notice'`), so they show in history and `tail`; state-changing web commands post one too (§10).
- **Sessions:** `/api/me` re-sets the cookie so its 12 h Max-Age slides with the server-side session; the maintenance task (every 60 s) also closes WebSockets whose session expired or was revoked, and logout closes the session's sockets at once.
- **Restart recovery** is implemented in M1 (`store.recover_on_start`): offered batches expire (`restart`), their deliveries go back to `pending` with `attempts+1`, every participant goes `offline`, and participants whose agent `(pid, start)` is gone are ended with a leave line. On Linux the broker pins this home's boot time before that liveness check, so a wall-clock step cannot end a live local session (#60). Delivery rows are created on insert (§8.1) even though no agent can join until M2.
- **Tests:** `hatchling` is a dev dependency so `test_wheel_contents` builds offline. The in-process broker fixture points `codex.control_socket`, `codex.bin` and `claude.sessions_dir` at the temp home. CLI test children run in a new session (no controlling terminal). Production-policy refusal tests run CLI or RPC children under a stand-in agent harness, so their verdict does not depend on who runs pytest or whether its launching shell exits (#437, #458).
- **Review fixes (M1):**
  - The peer check walks the whole ancestry to pid 1 and fails closed (§5.3); `ps` is called by absolute path.
  - `/budget n` decides raise vs lower after the hourly refill; `sys.status` shows the refilled budget.
  - The WebSocket replay-cap notice counts the skipped messages of that room (ids are global across rooms).
  - Text that is empty once `clean()`ed (only control or zero-width characters) is refused as an empty message.
  - Bad `/login` tokens write at most one `login bad_token` event per 60 s (with a count), so an unauthenticated endpoint can't grow the DB.
  - Config values are checked against the field's declared type (float fields take fractions) and must be ≥ 0.
  - The "a login link was issued via cli" UI notice is a **warn**.


## 16. Implementation notes and deviations (M2)

- **Module split.** Agent operations (`mcp.hello`, `agent.*`, `hook.*`, liveness) live in `broker/agents.py` (`AgentService`) rather than in `service.py`; `RoomService` calls into it through a `DeliveryHooks` interface. `delivery/engine.py` stays synchronous; `delivery/runner.py` owns every future and timer.
- **Participants are created at `join`**, not at `mcp.hello` (§6.3: a hello and bye with no membership changes no state).
- **One `PullAdapter` serves every harness in M2** (`adapters/base.py`): an open `wait()` gets wakes, mid-task priority is pulled by the next hook or tool call, otherwise the member is parked. Claude's tier is `claude:hook` (§9.2's non-inbox tier); Codex, Cursor and Devin are `mcp-only`, and their hook outputs stay off until M4/M5 even though the hook script already carries the full §7.3 table.
- **`wait()` turn boundaries.** Each new `wait()` increments `boundary_seq`. For members whose status comes from sinks (the test harness, and sessions that never sent a hook) "one peer batch per turn boundary" would otherwise allow only one peer batch ever; after the M2 review this applies to hooked members too (see review fixes below).
- **Pull-answer confirmation (§8.7 wait/read/say row), refined:** the `pull_ack_s` expiry applies only to members that have sent hooks; a hook-less member's answer is confirmed by its next switchboard call and never times out. "Any other hook first" is limited to turn-boundary events (UserPromptSubmit, Stop, SessionStart, SessionEnd, Interrupt) that started after the answer, because a parallel tool's PostToolUse can legitimately arrive first. `agent.unwait` never counts as a next call.
- **Tokens** confirm only on a successful PostToolUse or on UserPromptSubmit, from any tool's output (not only switchboard's): the HMAC binds a token to its member, and peer text has `yk:` defanged.
- **Wait fills** carry the released items plus any notified stubs (a pull path shows everything); a notified stub alone never wakes.
- **Hook replies** carry one room per hook call (the room holding the highest-priority item).
- **Claude hook context cap** is 10,000 characters in the hook's table (no Claude limit was recorded); broker batches are fitted to `min(batch_max_chars, ctx cap)` (6,000 for Claude), and the hook prints nothing rather than cut (§7.3).
- **MCP handshake.** FastMCP 4.0.9 / mcp 2.2.0 also speak MCP 2026-07-28, where a client sends `server/discover` instead of `initialize`. The server takes `clientInfo` from `initialize`, or else from each request's `_meta["io.modelcontextprotocol/clientInfo"]`, before sending `mcp.hello`. Tool annotations use the SDK v2 snake_case names; they serialize as `readOnlyHint`/`destructiveHint`/`openWorldHint`/`idempotentHint` on the wire.
- **Install (retired, §9.7).** A re-install replaced older switchboard hook entries for the same home (a different hook version) instead of adding a second copy. `--dry-run` and `--print-args` are allowed from an editable checkout because they write nothing.
- **Fixtures (§12.3).** Claude's M0 hook loggers recorded key sets and selected values, not whole payloads, so the Claude fixtures are rebuilt from those and marked `_unverified`. The Devin background `run_subagent` PreToolUse *was* recorded (in the M0 Devin verify run). Tool-output text in recorded fixtures is replaced (some raw outputs were env dumps); ids are replaced and paths rewritten to `/ws`.
- **Review fixes (M2):**
  - Hook resolution: exact session keys for Codex and bound Cursor, and no other agent process between a hook and its session's agent (§5.3).
