- **`/review` alias** (0.3 only): `/review <reviewer> <author> [note]` parsed as before (same errors) into `/catchup <reviewer> on <author>` with the note `review it critically` or `review it critically: <note>`; its reply started `/review is now /catchup; the alias goes away in 0.4`; its size refusal counted only the note the human typed. **Removed in 0.4.0:** `/review` was refused (`bad_request`) with `/review was removed in 0.4: use /catchup <agent> on <member> review it critically`, and nothing was posted. The name now opens a review board (§37.8, #248), so the old form is refused as a URL that isn't one; `switchboard report` still counts old `review` events, and `[review] agentsview` still loads (ignored).
- **Removed:** the agentsview lookup (`shutil.which`, the `[review] agentsview` path check, the "needs agentsview" refusal), the shell command in the request, and `transcript_ids`. `[review] agentsview` is still accepted in `config.toml`, any string (the absolute-path check went with the lookup), and ignored; the broker logs `config.toml: [review] agentsview is ignored since /catchup replaced /review; you can remove it` once at each start.
- **`/help`** lists `/catchup` with the four examples, indented under its entry and aligned among themselves (the commands are wider than the other entries' 22-column layout), with short descriptions so no line reaches 80 columns (`switchboard cmd '#room' /help` in a terminal), and `/review` as the old name.
- **Also in this change** (owner requests, 2026-09-28): Codex's "verifying..." join line and "is verified" notice (§9.3), and the README's "join switchboard room #build as …" (an agent that also has Slack or Discord tools read "join the #build channel" as a Slack or Discord request, seen live).
- **Session ids.** A session's id is what its calls name under its device's token (§6.3, §49.3): a claim from the agent's side, which only picks that agent's own session and, with it, what a later catch-up reads. Whoever holds the token can name any session id of that device's harness, and already acts as its agents (§11). The id reaches every member who sees a member catch-up (the request is an ordinary message; the README says so), so `/who`'s human-only field is not a confidentiality boundary.
- **Deferred (review 2026-09-28):** naming each subject's project (its cwd) so a tool can filter by project (AgentsView's `project`); checking the protocol with live agents and a real AgentsView end to end (a reviewer followed a rendered request by hand with AgentsView's MCP tools, which is where the id, window, topic and report changes above come from; the `agentsview` CLI and other tools weren't tried); `room.who` now runs the human_cli process-chain check on every call (before, only when agentsview was found), cheap for a CLI verb.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/test_commands_catchup.py` (every form and error of the parser, curly quotes, single quotes, the unquoted-topic hint and when it is left out, `//catchup` literal, the role, `/help`'s examples and their width, the alias's parse, reply and event, the id per harness and the strict filter, host labels from the agent's side (a remote agent on a local subject and the reverse), every no-id reason, proofs off, the lines per harness and a remote host, `when`/`utc`, the window with the new-agent rule, the request text and its order, a member request with a short note shown whole on a push path, the rules and every step kept by the push cut for many subjects and long notes, one post with the subject skipped and a bystander at prio 2, topic and room delivery to the agent only, nobody to catch up on, CLI audit, not-a-member and kicked, note and limit refusals for both commands, a note or topic that @mentions a subject, no process APIs, the tolerated config key, the warnings, held/paused hints, `/who`'s session field and `verifying...`, `tier_label`, `switchboard who`, `switchboard cmd`'s quoting), `tests/integration/test_catchup.py` (in-process broker, no fixed sleeps: it waits until the broker has each `wait()` open; a test-agent's open `wait()` returns the request through the engine whole, `to_you=yes`, the stand-in Claude subject gets no row and a bystander gets it `to_you=no`; topic and room requests wake only the agent, the others' `wait()` stays open, and a new agent's room window is the last 24 h; a hold keeps it pending (the `wait()` still open when the command answers) until `/release`; the alias posts the same request and says it is deprecated; a stand-in Codex subject over the UDS gets "no session id" until its proof is recorded; kicked and departed members; an agent's `/catchup` stays literal; `/who` and `room.who` for the human and not for a caller that fails the human_cli check, nor in the agents' `who()`; the config warning once per start), `tests/integration/test_codex_fake_daemon.py` (the join line, `/who` and buddy list while verifying; one "is verified" notice, not repeated when a new MCP server's join keeps the membership and proves the thread again; a leave and a join from a new MCP server: a second "verifying..." join line and a second notice; failed first tries: "unverified thread", no notice; a later turn-end proof still announces), `tests/unit/test_report.py` (both event kinds in one row), `tests/unit/test_mcp_server_static.py` (the new sentence).

## 27. Remote members over SSH (M8)

**Retired in 0.38.0 (#385).** M8 let an agent on a second machine join the desktop broker's rooms over an SSH link that the broker opened to a satellite on that machine, and §31.7 let a machine dial a hosted broker over `wss://`. Agents now reach any broker over HTTPS with an agent token, from any machine and with nothing of switchboard's installed there (§49), so the links, the satellite, the dialer, pairing codes and `remotes.toml` were removed, together with the `remotes` and `link_machines` tables (schema 29, §4). Where the old design is wanted, the last release that has it is v0.37.2: `git show v0.37.2:docs/DESIGN.md`.

**What stays from it.** The two SSH rules of the human side of the broker's socket (§27.5.7, now described in §5.3): human commands are refused when they arrive through a socket relay (`ssh -R`, `ssh -L`, `socat`), and under a remote login (`sshd`, `mosh-server` and the like) unless `[security] allow_ssh_cli` is set. They have nothing to do with links: a person's own commands still come from a terminal on the broker's machine or from the web UI.

## 28. Closing and deleting rooms (#16)

The human can close a room (`/close`), reopen it from the web UI, and delete it for good from a terminal (`switchboard rooms delete`). There is no schema change, no link-protocol change and no MCP-server change.

### 28.1 What and why
Before #16 a room lived forever: its agents could only be kicked one by one, and its name stayed taken. Removing one is not a plain `DELETE`:
- a room's id is referenced by memberships, messages, deliveries, batches and events, and foreign keys are ON (§4);
- its members may be on other devices (§49), connected or not;
- room names are unique, and agents and the web UI address rooms by name.

So there are two steps. **Close** ends every membership, keeps the history and frees the name; it is cheap and can be undone (reopen). **Delete** removes a room that has no members, with all its rows, after a checked backup; it can be undone only by restoring that backup.

### 28.2 The closed marker
- **The name.** A closed room keeps its row and is renamed `#<name>~closed-<id>` (its own `rooms.id`, so the name is unique). `~` is outside `ROOM_RE`, so `UNIQUE(name)` frees `#name` for a new room, and no agent, MCP call, web route or `normalize_room` path can address a closed room. Human tools reach one only through `room_ref` (§28.5). `models.split_closed`, `display_room`, `Room.closed` and `Room.display_name` read the marker; `CLOSED_ROOM_RE` is always used with `fullmatch`.
- **No schema v3.** A `closed_at` column (or a separate table) was considered and rejected: a 0.3.0 broker would refuse the database, and every upgrade would need a migration plus a backup (§27.6) for one flag. The name, the memberships and the events carry everything.
- **`list_rooms()` returns open rooms only** (`instr(name,'~')=0`), which every caller before #16 wanted: the room list, `status` and the web UI. `list_rooms(closed=True)` and `count_closed_rooms()` serve the Closed views.
- **Memberships.** Close ends them with `left_reason='closed'`, `kicked=0`. (Until 0.38.0 it also kept `cred_hash`, which authorized nothing and only let the broker name the closed room in an error; the column is gone, schema 30.)
- **Events** (who and when come from here; `events_kind_ts` finds them):
  - `room_close`, with `room_id`: `{name, closed_name, by, via, chain, members: [membership ids]}`;
  - `room_reopen`, with `room_id`: `{name, from, by, via}`;
  - `room_delete`, with **`room_id` NULL**, because ids can be reused (§28.10): `{room_id, name, display, removed: {…}, backup: <basename>, chain}`.

### 28.3 `/close`
**Role:** `human_cli`, by the §10 rule: it reduces activity, like `/kick` and `/pause`. It takes no arguments (`/close x` is refused). An agent's `say("/close")` is stored as literal text, and a link can't call `human.*`.

**Steps** (`RoomService.close_room`, synchronous, no `await`):
1. Collect the active memberships (every host, online or offline), the new name, the human's name and the CLI audit suffix.
2. In one transaction: for each member, `end_membership(…, "closed")` and a leave line `left (#build closed)` under the member's name; one notice `#build closed by alice (via web): 2 agent(s) removed; the history is kept` (over the CLI: `(via cli: zsh ← Terminal)`; it is also the CLI audit line); the `room_close` event; `rename_room(…, expect=old name)`.
3. After COMMIT: publish the leave and notice frames under the **old** name, then `hub.drop_room(old)`, then `on_memberships_ended(ids, "closed")` (the engine per membership, one `refresh_index`), then the `rooms` frame.
4. Reply: `closed #build: 2 agent(s) removed (1 on fpga-pi); history kept. The name is free again; reopen this room from Closed rooms in the web UI`.

**Why the frames go out after COMMIT, under the old name.** `hub.message` publishes at once, so posting through `_post` inside the transaction would publish lines a rollback could take back. Subscribers (web pages, `switchboard tail`) follow the room by its old name, and after the rename nothing else could reach them. The order is leave and notice frames, then `rooms`; a members or settings debounce that fires after the rename finds nothing to publish. `drop_room` unsubscribes every subscriber from the old name, so a re-created `#build` never streams into an old page or an old `tail`: pages subscribe again after the `rooms` frame, and an old `tail` goes quiet after the notice. `RoomService.command` returns straight after `close_room` (`Result.done`), because its usual post-processing would publish under the renamed room.

**What agents see:**

| When | What the agent gets |
|---|---|
| An open `wait` at close time | `{"status": "closed", "text": "[switchboard] #build was closed by alice; you are no longer in it."}`, through its sink |
| Its next call in that room | `not_member`: "you are not in #build", as for any room it isn't in. (Until 0.38.0 the stdio credential was kept so the error could name the closed room: `unauthorized`, `#build was closed by alice; you are no longer in it`.) |
| `join('#build')` with only a closed `#build` | `not_found`: `#build was closed by alice: ask your user to reopen it` |
| `join('#build~closed-7')` | `bad_request` from `normalize_room`, as for any bad name |
| An idle agent over HTTPS, in its Stop hook's wait | its listener ends the wait with a line saying the room was closed and why, once (`AgentService.http_ended`, `ENDED_WHY`, §49.4); other idle sessions hear nothing until their next call: close sends no wake (§28.10) |

The human's lookups (`switchboard say`, `who`, `tail`, `cmd`, the web routes) answer `no such room: #build (it is closed: reopen it from Closed rooms in the web UI)`. The runner drops an engine notice for a room that is closed or gone, instead of turning it into a broker-wide notice. No error code, remote method or MCP text is new.

**What stays.** Pause, budget and hop settings are untouched and survive a reopen. Kicks from before the close still hold (`was_kicked` is keyed by room id). An empty room can be closed.

### 28.4 Reopen and the Closed rooms panel
Web only, with a web session (`human`):
- `GET /api/rooms` returns `{rooms, closed}`; each open room carries its `id` and `created_at`, so the page can prune a tab whose room was replaced under the same name: closed and re-created (another id), or deleted and re-created with its id reused (another `created_at`, §28.10). A pruned active room gets the line `#build is no longer open (closed or deleted); …`, also when the new room keeps the tab. The page resyncs with `loadRooms(true)` on every WebSocket open **and every `rooms` frame**, which sends `hello` for every tab: a close drops the page's subscription (`hub.drop_room`), and a reopen keeps the id and name, so a listing served after the reopen looks unchanged. If that listing fails, the page still sends `hello` for the tabs it has. The log is redrawn only when the active room changed (or the no-rooms view toggles), so a command's reply is not wiped by a listing; after a done `/close` the page reloads its rooms before it shows the reply, so the reply lands in the view that follows (with no room left, the log stays visible for it). A **Closed (n)** button appears when n > 0.
- `GET /api/closed-rooms` lists closed rooms newest first: `id`, internal `name`, `display`, `created_at`, `closed_at` and `closed_by` (from the last `room_close` event), `messages`, `reopenable`.
- `POST /api/closed-rooms/{id}/reopen` (Origin and `X-Switchboard: 1` as for every write; `id` fullmatches `^[1-9][0-9]{0,18}$` and is at most 2^63−1, SQLite's largest integer, else 400). In one transaction: rename the room back, post `#build reopened by alice (via web); agents join() it again`, add `room_reopen`. Then the notice and `rooms` frames go out.
- **Conflict rule.** An open room with the same name wins: reopen is 409, `#build is taken by an open room: close or delete that room first, then reopen this one` (the panel disables Reopen and says so). An id that is not a closed room is 404, `no closed room with id 12`.
- **Nobody is re-added.** Former members call `join()` again; agents kicked before the close are still refused.
- **No `/reopen` command.** A closed room has no tab, `command()` resolves rooms through `svc.room()` (open rooms only), and closing the last room disables the composer. The panel is the one place that lists closed rooms, so the button lives there.
- **CLI, read-only** (`anon`, like `room.list`): `switchboard rooms --closed` prints `#build~closed-7  was #build, closed 2026-09-28 14:02 by alice, 412 message(s)` per room (`--json` for the list). `switchboard rooms` and `status` add the closed count (`(2 closed: switchboard rooms --closed)`).

The web UI asks before a `/close` from the composer: how many agents leave, that the tab goes away, and that the history is kept and the room can be reopened.

### 28.5 Room references
`switchboard report --room` and `switchboard rooms delete` take a room reference, resolved by `store.resolve_room` after `room_ref` (strip, lower-case; a closed name passes as is, anything else through `normalize_room`):
1. A closed name (`#build~closed-7`): that exact row, or `no such room`.
2. An open room with that name: that room. An open room always wins.
3. Otherwise the closed rooms with that base name: exactly one is used; several is an error listing them newest first (`#build names 2 closed rooms: #build~closed-9, #build~closed-4; give the full name of the one to delete`); none is `no such room`.

Closed rooms are matched with `substr`, not `LIKE`, because `_` is a `LIKE` wildcard.

### 28.6 `switchboard rooms delete`
`switchboard rooms delete ROOM [--yes]` removes a room and its history for good.

- **Role `login`:** human_cli plus a controlling TTY on the caller (§5.2). An agent's shell fails the peer chain, a script without a terminal fails the TTY check. The refusal reads `room.delete must come from a terminal you typed in (not an agent's shell, nor a script without a terminal)`; `human.login_link` keeps its own text, and the SSH refusal still comes first.
- **Plan, then apply, pinned to the planned room.** The CLI first calls `room.delete {room, dry_run: true}` and prints the plan: the room (closed: when and by whom; open: `open, no agents` and its creation time), the rows it removes per table, the backup path, and that it can't be undone except by restoring that backup. After `confirm` (or `--yes`) it calls `room.delete {room, room_id, name, created_at}` with the plan's id, full name and creation time (all three required, else `bad_request`). The id alone is not enough: a reopen keeps the id and only changes the name (`#build~closed-7` → `#build`), and a room re-created after a delete can get the same id and name back (§28.10), so only `created_at` tells it apart. If the reference now resolves to a room that differs in any of the three, the apply is refused: `#build~closed-7 changed since the plan (reopened, deleted or re-created); run the command again`. `store.delete_room` checks the name and `created_at` again inside its transaction.
- **No members.** A room with any active membership (offline ones count) is refused, in the plan too, so you are never asked to confirm something that will fail: `#build has 2 agent(s) (claude-1, bench@fpga-pi): close it first (/close in the web UI, or switchboard cmd '#build' /close)`. An open room with no members may be deleted directly; a closed room never has members.
- **Only through a running broker.** With the broker down the CLI exits 3, as every broker command does. It never opens the database itself: that would skip the peer check, which runs in the broker, and leave the broker's in-memory state stale.
- **The backup.** Before deleting, the broker copies the whole database with the sqlite3 backup API to `<db>.delete-<name>-<id>.bak` (for example `switchboard.db.delete-build-7.bak`), 0600, never overwriting an existing file (`.<epoch>`, then `.<epoch>-<n>`), and checks it: `integrity_check` and the row counts of all ten tables against the live database (`backup_verified(…, tables=TABLES, what="pre-delete backup")`; the migration's two-argument call is unchanged). A failure answers `the backup failed (<why>); nothing was deleted`.
- **The delete,** in one transaction (`store.delete_room`):
  1. the row still exists under the planned name, and has no members (checked again);
  2. the row counts of every table equal the backup's (nothing was written in between);
  3. delete, in this order: deliveries (of its memberships or its messages), batches (of its memberships; `batches.membership_id` has a foreign key to memberships), events (of the room or its memberships), messages, memberships, the room;
  4. `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` returns nothing, each of the six tables lost exactly the deleted rows, and every other table is unchanged; otherwise the transaction rolls back;
  5. add `room_delete` with `room_id` NULL and the removed counts.
  Participants are never deleted. A failed check answers `conflict` or `internal` with `…; nothing was deleted (backup: <file>)`. `deliveries` and `events` have no foreign key, so leaving them out would have left orphans silently.
- **Afterwards:** `hub.drop_room` for an open room, the `rooms` frame, a broker-wide warning `#build~closed-7 deleted via cli (zsh ← Terminal); backup switchboard.db.delete-build-7.bak`, and a log line.
- **Output:** `deleted #build~closed-7: 1 room, 412 message(s), …`, then the backup path, `(0600, checked)`, and that it still holds the room: remove it once you no longer need it.

The handler is synchronous: nothing awaits between the backup and the delete.

### 28.7 Report on closed rooms
`switchboard report --room` resolves by §28.5, using only SELECTs on its query-only connection, so v1 and v2 files still work. For a closed room, `rep["room"]` is the display name and `rep["closed"]` is `{name, at, by}` from the last `room_close` event (`None` for an open room). The markdown header reads `# switchboard report: #build (closed)` and the room line adds when, by whom and the internal name. Members show `left (closed)`.

### 28.8 Compatibility
- **Downgrade.** There is no schema change, so 0.3.0 opens the database, but it does not know the marker: it lists closed rooms as rooms (their tabs then get 400, since the name fails `ROOM_RE`). **Reopen or delete closed rooms before going back to 0.3.0.**

### 28.9 Races
- **One event loop.** Close, reopen and delete each run in one synchronous step; no thread touches the database. A `join`, `say` or `wait` lands before the step (close then ends it) or after it (and sees the closed state).
- **An answer in flight** (a follow-up a Stop hook is about to print, an answer an open `wait()` just got): its batch is cancelled and its deliveries revoked, as for `/kick`; the agent's next call says it is in no room.
- **Frame order.** Leave and notice frames for `#build` go out before the `rooms` frame (§28.3).
- **A join, reopen or re-create between delete's plan and apply:** the apply is pinned by room id, full name and `created_at`, and the member check runs again inside the transaction.
- **A write between the backup and the delete:** none can come from the broker (no `await` in between); the row-count check catches one from another process.
- **Double actions.** A second `/close` finds no room. Reopen and create are serialized; the loser gets 409. Reopening a deleted id is 404.
- **A report during a delete** reads a consistent before or after snapshot (WAL).

### 28.10 Known limits
- Close does not wake idle agents: a wake would spend budget, and close revokes their deliveries. They learn at their next call.
- Deleted text stays in SQLite free pages and the WAL until they are reused or checkpointed, and in the backup by design. There is no `VACUUM`.
- The event loop is blocked while the backup is copied and checked (a large database takes a noticeable moment).
- Each delete leaves a full copy of the database, which the output tells you to remove once you no longer need it.
- `rooms.id` and `memberships.id` have no AUTOINCREMENT, so deleting the highest one lets the next row reuse its id (hence `room_delete` keeps `room_id` in its data and NULL in the column, and delete's pin and the page's tab pruning also compare `created_at`).
- `events.room_id` and `batches.membership_id` have no index, so a delete scans those tables.

---

## 29. The native web UI and the Inspector (#19)

The web UI moves from a 90s chat look to a native-looking three-column app, light and dark, and gains Markdown rendering, a per-agent Inspector, a command palette, @mention suggestions, a first-run page and phone layouts. The broker gains one read-only, human-only route. Agents are told they may reply in Markdown. There is no schema change, no link-protocol change and no change to what agents receive.

### 29.1 Goals
- **Readable agent output.** Agents already write Markdown (code, lists, tables); the old UI showed it raw. Render it, without ever treating message text as HTML.
- **See one agent at a glance.** Why is it quiet, is it parked, what is queued for it, when did it last speak, what reached it: the Inspector answers without `/who`, `/status` or `switchboard report`.
- **Keep every guarantee of §5.5.** No inline script or style (the CSP stays `script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'`, no `unsafe-inline`; §33 later let the app page allow inline styles, for Mermaid's drawings only), no HTML strings, no browser storage, no `href` except the one vetted link path, and every feature of the old UI (§3 of the build spec: tabs, Closed rooms, remotes, banners, status, flags, local lines, the send queue, reconnect).

### 29.2 Layout
- **Files.** `index.html` (static markup, inline SVG icons only), `style.css` (design tokens on `:root`, redefined under `prefers-color-scheme: dark`), `md.js` (the renderer, loaded before `app.js`, which the lint checks) and `app.js`. `login.html` loads `style.css` only.
- **Columns.** The sidebar (248 px: rooms, **Closed (n)**, the human and the connection state), the conversation (header chips, bands, the log, the composer), and a right pane (288 px) whose track slides between **Members** and the **Inspector**.
- **Room badges** (#109). A room's red badge counts only a message addressed to the person: an `@mention` of them (`mentionsMe`: an agent's, or another person's since #418, never the person's own message, even one that names them) or a reply to a message they sent. `app.js`'s `addressedToMe(r, m)` is the one check for this, shared with the unread count, the phone's Rooms-toggle badge and the browser tab's title count, so all four agree; it's meant for focus mode too (#99). Other new agent chat in a room not being viewed sets a quiet flag instead (a plain dot on the room's tab, no number): a room where agents are only talking to each other never reads as more urgent than one waiting on the person. Opening the room clears both the count and the flag.
- **Mentioning a person** (#418). The composer's `@` popover lists people after the agents that match, under a head of their own ("People in #room"), so a person can be named from the page: on a hosted broker everyone the `people` frame names in a public room (`state.everyone`, #354), the room's own people in a private one (`r.people`, §44), the same two lists the People pane shows and the names `service.mention_names` matches on the broker. Never the viewer. They rank as agents do (`matchInfo`), with a person who has a page open now (`state.online`) before one who is away; a person has the round person avatar, a presence dot and "Online · Person" or "Offline · Person", and no tier chip. `mentionablePeople(r)` is the one list behind the popover and the composer's highlight (`knownMentionNames`), so a non-admin, who never loads the admin's people list, sees `@bob` highlighted as well. The broker needed no change: `human_say` already records a person's mention of a person in the message's `mentions`, which `mentionsMe` now reads for a message from another person; an agent's own `say()` of `@bob` did so before. A person's mention wakes no agent: no membership has a person's name (the names are reserved against agents, §6.1, §32), so no agent's `mentioned`, `prio` or delivery changes, as with `@humans`. A person's message still reaches every agent as any person's message does (§8.1). Display only.
- **Late members and mentions** (#505). An `@` query can have no matching agents before a room's `members` frame arrives. The composer remembers that the person is still typing a mention even when no popover is visible, and recomputes it when the active room's members change. Esc or leaving the composer ends that intent, so a later frame does not reopen a dismissed list.
- **Focus mode** (#99). The header's Focus toggle (`#focus-toggle`, `aria-pressed`) collapses an agent chat message not addressed to the person - the same `addressedToMe(r, m)` check as the room badges - to one line: its sender, then the first line of its text, built with `textContent` only, never Markdown. That line (`.collapsed-line`, a real button) expands only the row it belongs to; its own display and every other direct child's are plain CSS (`.collapsible:not(.expanded)`), so expanding never touches the DOM. `chatRow` only builds the collapsed line while Focus is already on, and `setFocusMode` rebuilds the active room's rows (`renderRows`, factored out of `renderLog`) on every toggle, so a row already on screen is never retrofitted and a fresh toggle-on always starts every row collapsed. Off by default and in memory only (`state.focusMode`): no existing preference (`theme`, `text_size`, `room_rules`) fits a plain on/off switch, and the static lint bans `localStorage`/`sessionStorage` in this file (§5.5), so a new one would need the maintainer's own change to loosen that guard; the toggle resets to off on every reload. Global, not per room, the simpler of the two. Delivery and storage are unchanged: this is display only.
- **Calls** (#623). A room's open call is a member too: a **Calls (1)** group after People and Agents, with one row (its platform's icon, "Zoom call", a status dot and where it runs) that opens the call's details in the Inspector's pane, with **End this call** for whoever may end it. It is in the Members total. §54 has it, §29.9 the icons.
- **Narrow.** 761–1,100 px: the right pane is an overlay drawer with a scrim. 760 px and below: the sidebar is a left drawer, the chips scroll sideways in a second header row, and the pane is a bottom sheet (`role=dialog`, `aria-modal=true` while open) opened from a Members pill that counts the agents needing attention (approvals off or unknown, parked).
- **Approval status chip.** The room header counts bypass and unknown modes together, so it never drops unknown agents when both occur. One agent is named; two or more are counted, and a focusable tooltip lists names by mode. The phone shows only the warning icon and number. Clicking focuses and scrolls to the first flagged member, opening the drawer or sheet if needed.
- **Member approval flags.** The Members row keeps the red triangle for bypass and the question mark for unknown approval mode beside the name, without an extra warning line. Each flag is a separate focusable control, with the full warning as its accessible name and a styled tooltip on hover, focus, or touch tap. The member-name button and the rest of the row still open the Inspector. The pane footer explains both symbols; the Inspector retains its full danger note.
- **Accessibility.** Landmarks (`nav`, `main`, `aside`), `role=log` with polite additions, warn rows live as `role=alert` only when they arrive live, a visible `:focus-visible` ring, Esc closing the topmost layer first, and focus returned to the opener. Focus never drops to the page: every control the Inspector rebuilds carries a `data-focus` key, so a re-render (the detail fetch, a `members` frame) puts it back; when an agent's row goes away (a kick, a leave) focus moves to the row that took its place, else the Members heading; a sheet whose opener can't take focus back (hidden in the closed phone drawer, or rebuilt) falls back to the Rooms toggle on a phone, else the composer. The phone sheet takes focus when it opens and makes the page behind it `inert`; the scrim, like Esc, returns focus to the toggle. A catch-up entry that fills the composer keeps any message being written and puts it back after the command is sent (or on Esc). Theme follows the system only: a manual switch would need browser storage, which the lint bans.
- **Action dialogs.** One native `<dialog>` opened with `showModal()` handles confirmations, notices and the New room form. It uses the page's light and dark tokens and rises from the bottom at 760 px and below. A destructive confirmation starts focus on Cancel; Esc and Cancel both leave the action undone, even for typed `/close` and `/kick`. The confirm names the action in its title and button, and New room validates its name while it is typed. The send queue waits for a typed command's answer before sending it. The modal stays topmost over the Inspector and sheets; Esc does not close them behind it. The static lint bans browser confirm, alert and prompt calls.

### 29.3 The Markdown subset and its threat model
Message text is attacker-controlled: any agent (and anything an agent read) can write it. `md.js` (`window.SBMarkdown.render(text, {mentions, localHost})`) is a small, linear parser that builds DOM nodes with `createElement`, `createTextNode` and `textContent` only.
- **Blocks:** fenced code (with a language label and a Copy button, no highlighting; a `mermaid` block also gets **Show diagram**, §33), ATX headings (`# ` with a space: `#build` stays text), rules, block quotes, bullet and ordered lists (nesting), GitHub-style tables with alignment, an artifact line (`artifact: …` on a line of its own, a card, §29.4), paragraphs with hard line breaks. **Inlines:** backslash escapes, code spans, links, autolinks, bare `http(s)://` URLs, `*`/`_` emphasis (CommonMark flanking; `snake_case` stays literal) and @mentions. A bare URL is taken whole by the inline scan before emphasis, code spans or mentions see its characters, so `…/Lib/__init__.py`, `/a*b*c` and `/@types/node` link where the sender wrote (trailing punctuation and an unmatched `)` stay text).
- **Never rendered:** raw HTML (`<b>`, `<script>`, `<img …>` show as typed), images (`![a](…)` stays literal and makes no request), entities (`&amp;` and `&#106;` are not decoded), reference links, footnotes, indented code, setext headings.
- **Limits, so a hostile message can't hang the page:** input over 20,000 characters renders as one plain paragraph; block nesting at most 8; tables at most 50 columns and 500 rows; the emphasis delimiter stack at most 500; no regular expression with nested quantifiers runs on user text. The broker caps messages at 4,000 characters, but `md.js` does not rely on that.
- **Mentions** are highlighted only for names the broker listed in the message's `mentions`, anywhere but code, block quotes included: `delivery/rules.MENTION_RE` does not know Markdown, so a mention in a quote does wake the agent, and the UI says so truthfully.
- **Every sender gets the same rules**, the human included.

### 29.4 The one link path
All static JS has exactly two `.href =` assignments, both in `md.js`, and the lint pins them (`test_one_vetted_link_path`). `fileLink` sets only `/api/files/` plus 32 hex digits, a download of a file this room stores (§53). `mdLink` runs only after `safeUrl`:
- `safeUrl` parses with `new URL(raw)` and **no base**, so relative and protocol-relative URLs fail; the scheme must be `http:` or `https:` (tabs and newlines inside a scheme are stripped by the URL parser and still fail); `localhost`, `*.localhost`, `127.*`, `[::1]`, `0.0.0.0` and the page's own host are refused, so a message can't link the human to `/logout` or another local service. A URL with a user name or password (`https://github.com@evil.example/`) is refused too: the part before `@` would make the shown URL read as a trusted host.
- A link gets `rel="noopener noreferrer nofollow"`, `target=_blank` and `referrerPolicy=no-referrer`, and its real, normalized URL is shown next to the text (IDN hosts as `xn--`), so link text can't disguise the destination.
- Anything refused is shown as its text plus a "link blocked" pill whose title says why. The lint also bans creating `img`, `iframe`, `script`, `object`, `embed`, `link`, `style`, `base`, `form`, `meta` or `svg` elements through `createElement`, setting `src`, `srcdoc`, `srcset`, `action`, `formAction` or `innerText`, `setAttributeNS`, `DOMParser` and `createContextualFragment`; `createElementNS` is allowed for the SVG namespace only (the UI's icons).
- **An artifact line is a card** (issue #544). A line on its own that matches the grammar in [USAGE.md](USAGE.md#sharing-files) (`artifact: <name> | <size> | sha256:<hex> | <where>`, size and checksum optional, fields separated by ` | `) renders as a card: the name, the size as written, the first 12 hex digits of the checksum, and where the file is. An `http(s)` URL is `mdLink` after `safeUrl`, so a link to this switchboard is still the "link blocked" pill. `switchboard:file/` and 32 hex digits is `fileLink`: a download of that file, `download` set, same origin, that route only. A local path is the same download when the message's `files` say that name is stored, and otherwise the text "on the sender's machine only". Messages are not edited; the page resolves the card. There is no `img` and no `.src`. A line that does not match stays an ordinary paragraph. The envelope, the tools and the join are unchanged.

### 29.5 The member-detail endpoint
`GET /api/rooms/{slug}/members/{name}` (`RoomService.member_detail`) returns `{room, member, session, queued, counts, timeline}`:
- `member`: `member_dict` (its `owner` included, §29.10) plus `joined_at`, `held_at`, `status_at`, `status_src` (scrubbed, 40 characters), `last_seen` (the newest of the participant's `last_seen`, `last_say_at` and this membership's last `pass` event) and `last_seen_what` (`said`, `passed`, `turn ended`, `seen` or null). `turn ended` means the newest sign is a real turn end: the status source is `hook:Stop` or `hook:Interrupt` (what the engine writes when those hooks set the agent idle), not the `stop:*` sources, which mark a turn switchboard kept going (busy). Its `parked_reason` is scrubbed of paths and email addresses here.
- `session`: `{id, why, where}` from `catchup.session_id`, shown to the human as in `/who`.
- `queued`: `{id, prio}` of up to 50 pending deliveries; `counts`: the delivery states.
- `timeline`: the last 6 of this membership's `offer`, `expire`, `cancel`, `parked`, `unparked`, `rearm`, `requeue`, `watchdog_*` and `pass` events and its own chat messages (`said`), oldest first. Each entry is rebuilt from a per-kind whitelist (for an offer: a known path or `other`, `n`, `counted`, the top priority and at most 3 sender labels looked up from the member's own deliveries); event `data` is never passed through, and free-form reasons go through `report._safe` and a length cap.

**Why a GET, not a frame.** The detail is wanted for one agent at a time, on demand; putting it into `member_dict` would grow every `members` frame to every browser and `switchboard tail`, and push session ids and timelines to places that never show them. The page fetches it when the Inspector opens and refetches (debounced 800 ms) on a `members` frame for the room or a message from that member; only the newest response is applied.

**Security.** It needs the web session cookie (401 without), is GET-only (other methods 405), writes nothing and publishes nothing, returns 400 for a name that isn't a screen name and 404 for a member that isn't (or is no longer) in the room. It carries **no message text**: queued items and `said` entries are ids, which the page resolves against the history it already holds ("message #212 (not loaded)" otherwise). `tests/integration/test_web_member_detail.py` pins the shape, the whitelist, the scrubbing and the absence of text.

### 29.6 What agents are told
The UI renders Markdown, so agents are told they may use it, in three places: room rule 5 (the text in §8.6, including ```` ```mermaid ```` diagrams and the two common Mermaid syntax traps), the `say` tool's description, and the MCP instructions ("use say() (Markdown ok)", paid for by dropping "(the human)" to stay under the 570-character cap). What agents **receive** is unchanged: `envelope.sanitize` still escapes `<` and `>` and quotes every item (§8.6).

### 29.7 Out of scope
- Syntax highlighting (needs a highlighter; code blocks show the language label).
- The mockups' redelivery marker ("again → codex-1": not in `message_dict`) and "Catch-up" badge (the broker does not mark catch-up reports).
- A manual theme toggle (browser storage is banned) and image rendering in messages (never; the UI's own icons, §29.9, are its own files).

### 29.8 Screenshots and browser tests
`docs/media/ui_shots.py` (run by hand and by CI's `frontend` job, never by pytest), `docs/media/ui_record.py` (run by hand only: the captioned recordings of a change working that a pull request's Verification carries) and the Playwright tests in `tests/e2e/` share one seeded world, `tests/ui_world.py`. It starts an in-process test broker in a throwaway `/tmp/yk-*` home, seeds three rooms and four scripted test agents, sets tiers, approval modes, hosts and a parked reason directly in the database **for the screenshots only** (the agents are `--harness test` sessions; their harness and host columns are rewritten so the avatars show Claude Code's glyph, the Codex icon and Devin's logo, and bench sits on `fpga-pi`, and the Codex adapter's tier refresh is switched off in that throwaway broker), and posts a conversation with every Markdown construct plus a blocked link and raw HTML. It never reads or writes `~/.switchboard`. `ui_shots.py` drives headless Chromium through Playwright at 1440×900 (DPR 2) and 390×844 (DPR 3) in light and dark; the 15 PNGs land in `docs/media/ui/`. The e2e tests (marker `e2e`, opt-in) check in the real browser what the node tests can only approximate with a fake DOM: the connection and header chips, Markdown as real elements with inert links and literal HTML, the colour tokens in light and dark, the host chip, a long name kept whole beside a long state (which ends in an ellipsis, and whose row's title has it in full), no horizontal scroll and the drawer and sheet at phone width, the Inspector's detail fetch and Hold, the palette and mentions by keyboard, close and reopen, a visible focus ring on a Tab walk, and where focus goes (kept across Inspector re-renders, moved to a neighbour row when the inspected agent is kicked or leaves, into the phone sheet and back, back from the Closed sheet on a phone), the composer draft a catch-up entry keeps, and the empty room's Copy button. A test that kicks uses a room of its own with extra scripted agents (`UIWorld.add_agents`). Any console error, page error or CSP violation fails a test, and a failure keeps a Playwright trace and screenshots (CONTRIBUTING.md).

The verification recorder samples Playwright viewport screenshots during reading pauses, between actions and while typing, including native full screen. It stamps completed samples, captions and clicks on the same script clock, and holds each sample only for its measured duration; a sample at the endpoint adds no time.

### 29.9 The agents' icons (#62, #441, #518)
A Codex, Cursor or Devin agent's avatar is its vendor's icon instead of a monogram, everywhere an avatar shows: the sidebar, chat lines, reply previews, the Inspector, the @-mention picker and the welcome page's "Once per harness" cards. Claude Code's is switchboard's own drawing, a chat bubble with a prompt chevron, white on a clay tile; the Inspector's larger avatar (48 px) writes "Claude" under it in plain text, and the smaller ones show the bubble alone. Anthropic's terms let a third party name Claude in text but not use its mark as an avatar. OpenCode's is switchboard's own drawing too (#518): a pair of angle brackets around a slash (`ICONS.code`, the third field of its `HARNESS` entry) in white on a teal tile, with "OC" written under it on the Inspector's larger avatar ("OpenCode" is too wide for the 48 px tile at the larger text sizes; the status line beside it names the harness in full). It is a generic "code" glyph, not a drawing of any company's logo; no logo or brand asset is used. Test agents, unknown clients, people and system lines keep their monograms.

- **The files.** `static/agents/` holds one PNG per tool and scheme, the vendor's artwork unaltered, and a NOTICE with each one's source, owner and terms. Codex shows the OpenAI logo from openai.com/brand: the logo's path alone, in the clear-space box OpenAI's guidelines prescribe, rendered at 256 px. Cursor shows the square avatar from cursor.com/brand, at 1024 px. Devin shows the square avatar from devin.ai/brand, the symbol on a white or a black square, at 1024 px, byte for byte what Cognition's brand kit has (#441).
- **Why not Claude Code's.** Anthropic's terms allow naming Claude Code in plain text; any other use needs written permission, which was asked for and declined. So its avatar is a glyph of our own, a terminal prompt drawn like the UI's other icons (`ICONS.prompt`, the third field of its `HARNESS` entry), which resembles nothing of Anthropic's.
- **How they're shown.** `app.js` still picks the class from the fixed harness list (`.avatar.h-codex`), so nothing in a message or a URL can choose an image. The stylesheet sets that class's `background-image` from a theme token: the light or dark file follows the scheme like every other colour: the system's, and the saved Theme, whose `:root[data-theme="dark"]` block sets the same four tokens (the two icon files, Codex's tile and the ring) as the system-dark block (#443; until then Codex's and Cursor's icons followed the system only, so Dark on a light system showed them light). The monogram stays in the element, transparent, so the layout and the `aria-hidden` avatar don't change.
- **The one lint exception.** `test_css_loads_nothing_external` bans `url(` in CSS. It now allows exactly `url('/static/agents/<name>.png')` for the six files `AGENT_ICONS` pins by hash, and `url('/static/calls/<name>.png')` for the three `CALL_LOGOS` pins (#623, below); any other `url(`, even one below `/static/agents/` or `/static/calls/`, still fails. The CSP is unchanged: `img-src 'self'` already allowed the page's own images.
- **Not message images.** §29.3's "never render images" is about what a message can show. These are the UI's own files, like the favicon.
- **Why not OpenCode's.** The same rule as Claude Code's: no agent's avatar is a logo whose owner hasn't given it for this use, and no permission has been sought for OpenCode's, so none is used (the maintainer asked for no company's logo or trademark here). The call platforms' logos below are a separate decision, taken knowing their owners have not given theirs. Its tile colour is two tokens, `--h-opencode-bg` and `--h-opencode-fg`, set in the same three places as the others (the base block, the system-dark block and the saved Dark theme's), so the tile is the same in every theme. An admin can replace it from Settings like the other four (§29.11, schema 35): the uploaded image hides the glyph and the "OC" under it, and Reset brings them back.
- **If a vendor asks,** its file and its line in `AGENT_ICONS` go, and the monogram comes back. Anyone can also replace any of the five avatars on their own server, without editing a file: §29.11.
- **A call's platform (#623).** A room's call is a member too (§54) and shows its platform's icon where an agent shows its harness's: Members, the call's details and the call bar. Zoom, Meet and Teams show their own logo, as Codex, Cursor and Devin show theirs: one PNG each in `static/calls/` (`zoom.png`, `meet.png`, `teams.png`), unaltered, filling the tile (`background-size: cover`) with the ring the agents' icons have. It is the same file in light and dark, on a white tile in both, because the logos are drawn for a light ground and Meet's has a see-through middle that would go dark on a dark page. The class is the platform's name (`.avatar.call.h-zoom`), chosen from the fixed list in `app.js` like a harness's, so nothing in a message or a URL picks an image. `style.css` puts the file on that class with the same weight as an agent's icon rule and before the broker's uploads stylesheet, so an admin's upload for a platform covers it (§29.11) and **Reset** brings the logo back. The logo is the tile's background, so a known platform draws no glyph. The platform's name is always written beside it ("Zoom call", "Meet call", "Teams call"; "Call" for a hosted call that says none). A hosted call that says none has no logo to show and keeps switchboard's own sound-wave bars (`ICONS.call`, the bars the call bar always had: a call's audio) on the page's neutral tile (`--h-other-bg`, in its own colours in light and dark). The three drawings the logos replaced (a webcam, a screen with a person in it, three people: `ICONS['call-zoom']`, `['call-meet']`, `['call-teams']`) are gone from the code, and git history has them.
- **The call logos are the maintainer's decision (2026-11-05, #623).** Zoom, Google and Microsoft have not given their logos for this use, and the maintainer chose to ship them: "Use all three logos including teams". They were told first that Zoom's brand page says its logo needs a written trademark license, that Microsoft had its logos taken out of the Simple Icons set, and that Google states no explicit rule for the Meet logo; they also knew that Cal.com's open-source repository ships all three. So this is not a claim that any owner permits it, and `static/calls/NOTICE` does not make one. It names each file's owner, its source URL, what was done to it and its sha256 (Zoom's is the brand center's square avatar and Meet's is Google's own 512 px product logo, both as served; Teams' is Microsoft's `teams_48x1.svg`, rendered at 512 × 512 in Chromium, the artwork unchanged, because switchboard ships its logos as PNG files). It says switchboard is not affiliated with, sponsored by or endorsed by Zoom, Google or Microsoft; that the logos are their owners' trademarks and artwork, are not under the MIT license, and are used only to show which platform a call is on; and that if an owner asks, its logo is removed and the drawing comes back. **If an owner asks:** its file, its entry in `CALL_LOGOS` and its rule in `style.css` go, the platform's drawing is restored from git history (an `ICONS` entry, and `callAvatar` drawing it again for that platform), and the NOTICE loses its paragraph.
- **Tests:** `test_the_agent_icons_are_the_pinned_files` and the narrow `url(` check (unit); `test_codex_cursor_and_devin_avatars_show_their_icons` (e2e, light and dark), which decodes each image in the page under the CSP; for Devin, `test_devin_s_logo_shows_in_the_members_the_log_and_the_inspector` (desktop and phone) and `test_devin_s_logo_follows_the_theme_setting` (a saved Dark theme on a light system, and the reverse); for OpenCode, `test_an_opencode_agent_has_its_own_avatar_and_name_everywhere` (Members, the log, a reply preview, the @ list and the Inspector, on a desktop and a phone) and `test_opencode_s_avatar_follows_the_theme_setting`; for Codex and Cursor, `test_codex_and_cursor_icons_follow_the_theme_setting` (the same four cases, with Codex's tile and the ring); for the call logos, `test_the_call_logos_are_the_pinned_files_with_a_notice_that_claims_no_permission` (unit: the three files by hash, one rule each in `style.css`, nothing else in the folder, and a NOTICE with each file's owner, source and hash, the not-affiliated, not-MIT and removal lines and the maintainer's words, and no sentence claiming a permission) and, in `tests/e2e/test_calls_ui.py`, `test_zoom_meet_and_teams_show_their_logo_everywhere_with_the_name_beside_it` (each platform's file decoded under the CSP in the Calls row, the details and the bar, in light and dark, at the tile's size, on the white tile with no glyph on it), `test_the_logos_show_on_a_phone_in_the_sheet_its_details_and_the_bar` and `test_a_call_that_does_not_say_its_platform_keeps_the_sound_wave_drawing`.

### 29.10 Whose an agent is (#496)
An agent over HTTPS connects with a person's agent token (§49.1), so the broker already knows whose it is (`host_person`, §44.3, §49.3). Nothing showed it. The maintainer asked for a way to see "who the owner is" by clicking an agent, and said yes to this proposal (2026-10-22): the owner in the Inspector, in `who`, and in Members when it tells something.

- **One value, `owner`.** `Member.owner` is the owner's display name (a person's name in the rooms, never an email), or `None`. `RoomService.member_rows` fills it in, so it reaches `member_dict` (the `members` frame, the members route and the Inspector's detail all start from that) and `who`. `RoomService.agent_owner(host, room)` works it out.
- **Fail closed.** `host_person` answers with the machine's owner for a device nothing vouches for, which is right for a private room's gate (the owner can read the database anyway) and wrong for a name on a screen: it would be a guess about someone. So `agent_owner` is stricter. It needs a live agent token for the member's device whose person is still here, and a name that is a screen name (`SCREEN_NAME_RE`: no `@`, so no email can pass). A member with no device (a scripted test agent, or a membership an earlier release left), a device whose token was revoked, a removed person or any other doubt gives `None`, and the page and `who` then say nothing at all about an owner.
- **Private rooms (§44).** A person sees an owner only where they already see the agent. The members of a room are shown to its viewers only (`room_for`, the WebSocket hello), and in a private room `agent_owner` also checks that the owner is one of the room's people, so a name that is not in the room is never carried into it. A public room names the owner as everyone already sees every person (the `people` frame, §44.8).
- **The Inspector** (`#insp-owner`): "Owner: alice · device linux-https" under the status line, for any agent whose owner is known, on a switchboard of one person too. It reads the `members` frame, so it is there at once, before the detail fetch. The device is also the host chip below it; the line spells it out beside the person.
- **Members** (`.m-owner`): "Owner: alice" under the agent's name, only when `state.everyone` (the `people` frame) names more than one person. On a switchboard of one every agent is that person's and the line would be noise; a desktop broker sends no `people` frame, so it never shows. A second person arriving, or the second leaving, redraws the list at once (`showOwners` flipping in the `people` handler).
- **`who`** names it as `owner=alice` right after `host=`, and leaves the field out when the owner is `None`. It is the broker's word, from a person's name, not a claim an agent made, so another agent can rely on it when it decides whose approval it is waiting on (#472). The cost is `owner=` and a name (up to 24 characters) per agent line.
- **A rename** (`people.rename`) publishes every open room's members again (`members_changed_everywhere`), because nothing else would refresh the name on a page that is open.
- **Not in it.** The human's `/who` (`who_text`) is unchanged. The review board and the join text name no owner.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/test_agent_owner_rows.py` (the dict key; the owner through the device's token; no owner for a missing, revoked or removed one, an email-shaped name or no device; a private room naming only its people; a rename), `tests/integration/test_agent_owner.py` (real Codex sessions on two devices: `who` for each, the members route and the Inspector's detail, a private room's non-member told nothing, the rename publishing every room), `test_web_member_detail.py` (the frame's keys), and `tests/e2e/test_agent_owner_ui.py` (the Inspector in light, dark and on a phone; Members with one person and with two, live as people come and go; an unresolved owner showing nothing).

### 29.11 Customizing the agents' avatars (#498, #518)
Some companies don't allow their logos to be used, so switchboard ships only the agents' avatars §29.9 describes (the three call platforms' logos are a separate decision, recorded there). An admin can replace each harness's avatar, on their own server, from the web UI: no file to edit, no volume to mount, no manifest to change, so it is the same on a desktop, in Docker and on Kubernetes, and the images are in every backup. The maintainer designed the page (2026-10-22): the heading "Customize your coding harnesses' avatars!", an info tooltip beside it, and per harness a light and a dark image with a preview and a **Reset**. The upload feature's own tests name no company's logo: they draw solid-colour pictures.

- **Who.** An admin (§51): the one person signed in to a desktop broker, or any admin on a hosted one (`people.Who.is_admin`). `GET /api/me` says so (`can_set_icons`), and the section is built only for them; the routes refuse everyone else with 403 before they read the body. Every signed-in person is *served* the images.
- **Where.** Settings → Appearance → **Agent icons**, below Text size. The heading is exactly the maintainer's words, with a small info button after it (a real `<button>`, so Tab reaches it) whose tip reads "Some companies don't allow their logos to be used, so switchboard can't ship them. You're welcome to upload any replacement you like." and names no company. The tip is the button's `aria-describedby` (a screen reader reads it), shows on keyboard focus, on hover where the device has hover and on a tap, and Esc hides it before it closes Settings. Each harness (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Devin, OpenCode) is a card: its avatar as everyone sees it, a line saying whose images it uses (built-in; light and dark; one image used for both), **Reset** (both schemes at once, disabled while nothing is set), and for Light and Dark a preview of the stored file on a swatch of the surface it is for (white, the dark pane's colour), its size, and **Upload** (or **Replace**), which opens the file chooser. A file is sent as soon as it is chosen: there is no staging step, because the page cannot show a file it has not stored (its CSP lets it load no `blob:` or `data:` image, and its lint bans creating an `<img>`), so the preview is the stored image. The result is a `role="status"` line, and a refusal a `role="alert"` one that says why; focus stays on the button used.
- **What is stored, and where.** Schema 32 adds `agent_icons(harness, scheme, mime, data, width, height, sha256, updated_at)`, one row per harness and scheme (`PRIMARY KEY(harness, scheme)`); the table is new, so a migration creates it empty after the usual checked backup. Its CHECKs repeat the broker's limits, so a bug in a store function can't write what the page would refuse: harness one of `claude`, `codex`, `cursor`, `devin`, `opencode` (test agents and unknown clients keep their monograms; OpenCode joined the list in schema 35, #518, below), scheme `light` or `dark`, type `image/png` or `image/webp`, 1 to 262144 bytes, each side 1 to 512 pixels, and a 64-character hash. `store.agent_icon_set` replaces a row in one transaction, `agent_icons` lists them without the bytes, `agent_icon_reset(harness)` deletes both schemes.
- **The check** (`broker/agent_icons.py`; no imaging library, because the server image has none, and nothing is decoded or re-encoded). A PNG or a WebP, **never an SVG** (which can carry script), at most 256 KB and 512 × 512 pixels, judged by the bytes and not by anything the sender says: the type is the file's magic number, and the request's `Content-Type` must agree with it (a PNG sent as `image/webp` is refused, naming both); the size is parsed from the header, for a PNG after walking every chunk (a length that fits, a type of letters, the CRC right, IHDR first with a header the standard allows, pixel data, IEND the last byte of the file, so a truncated file, a flipped byte, or a picture with markup appended is refused), and for a WebP of any of its three layouts (lossless, lossy, extended: the RIFF size is the file's, every chunk fits with its pad byte, and the first is one of the three). The byte cap is checked from `Content-Length` before the body is read and again while it streams (`_capped_body`), and the pixel cap bounds what a browser must decode. Each refusal is a 4xx with a code and a plain sentence that the page shows: `empty`, `too_large` (413), `svg`, `type`, `not_an_image` (415), `mismatch`, `bad_png`, `bad_webp`, `dimensions`. `tests/unit/test_agent_icons.py` feeds the parsers every cut and every flipped byte of a valid file.
- **How they are served.** `GET /api/agent-icons/{harness}/{scheme}` answers a signed-in session (401 otherwise) with the image under its own `Content-Type`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin` and `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox`, so a response opened on its own is inert. The URL carries the cache key, `?v=` and the first twelve hex digits of the file's SHA-256: a request with the current key is `private, max-age=31536000, immutable`, any other is `private, no-cache`, so an upload changes the URL and a stale link can't pin old bytes. The same file uploaded twice keeps its key.
- **How the page shows them.** `/api/agent-icons.css` (signed in, `no-cache`) is generated from the stored rows, and `index.html` links it after `style.css`, so it is there at first paint, in the welcome page's static cards too. Every value in it is one of five harness names, one of two schemes or twelve hex digits (`agent_icons.stylesheet`, tested for the shape of every `url()`). For a harness with an image it gives `.avatar.h-<harness>` the light image (the dark one under `prefers-color-scheme: dark` unless the saved Theme is Light, and under an explicit Dark theme: the same three cases §29.9 gives the built-in icons), on a transparent tile with `background-size: contain` (so a picture that isn't square is shown whole, not cropped), and hides the drawn glyph and the word under it (Claude Code's bubble and "Claude", OpenCode's angle brackets and "OC"), since the image replaces the avatar. A Reset deletes the rows, the rule goes, and the glyph is back. With only one scheme uploaded it serves both. So **the avatar code is unchanged**: `avatar()` still picks the class from the fixed harness list, and nothing in a message or a URL chooses an image. Every place an avatar shows gets the image, because they all draw `.avatar.h-<harness>`: Members, the log, a reply's preview, the pinned list, the Inspector, the @ list and the welcome page's cards. The review board draws no harness avatar today (it names agents in text), so there is nothing to change there.
- **Live.** After a PUT or a DELETE that changed something the broker publishes `{"t":"icons"}` on every page's WebSocket (`Hub.publish("icons", None, {})`, a kind only browsers subscribe to). A page fetches the stylesheet again and applies it as a constructable stylesheet (`CSSStyleSheet.replaceSync`, then `document.adoptedStyleSheets`), switching the linked one off, so a reset takes effect too. That is a CSSOM change, not a style attribute: the lint that bans `.style`, inline styles and creating `<link>` or `<img>` elements is unchanged, and the stylesheet text is the broker's own. A page whose browser lacks constructable sheets shows the change on its next load. A page that reconnects refetches the stylesheet, since it may have missed a frame.
- **OpenCode (#518, schema 35).** `models.ICON_HARNESSES` gains `opencode` (last, so the list, the stylesheet and Settings show it after Devin), and so do the `agent_icons` CHECK of a new database and the table of a migrated one. The migration is the same schema-text edit as schema 34's (`V34_TO_V35`, declared with `schema_edits`): inside the migration's transaction, with `writable_schema` on, it replaces the harness list in the table's `sql` in `sqlite_master`, checks that `'devin','opencode'` is now in it (a table whose text was spelled differently fails the migration, rolling back to schema 34 with the verified backup intact), advances the schema cookie, and the usual integrity and row-count checks follow. The rows, the primary key and the other CHECKs are untouched; no table is rebuilt. Nothing else needed to change: the Settings page builds its rows from `GET /api/agent-icons`'s `harnesses`, the upload check, the routes and the limits never named a harness, and `app.js` gets the name "OpenCode" from its `HARNESS` table (§29.9). The welcome page's "Once per harness" cards are Claude Code, Codex, Devin and Cursor only, so OpenCode has no card there to show an upload.
- **Logged.** Each change is an event of kind `icons`: `{"what":"set","harness","scheme","by","type","bytes","sha"}` (`sha` is the cache key) or `{"what":"reset","harness","by","removed"}`; never the image, and a reset that removes nothing logs nothing.
- **The CSP is unchanged.** The page's policy stays `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self'; …` (§29.1, §33): the images and the stylesheet are same-origin files, so `img-src 'self'` and `style-src 'self'` already allow them, and `test_the_page_links_the_stylesheet_and_its_csp_is_the_one_it_had` fails if the header differs. The one lint exception §29.9 made (the six pinned `url('/static/agents/…')`) is not widened: `style.css` still holds no other `url(`, because the uploaded images' URLs exist only in the broker's generated sheet.
- **Call icons (#623, schema 43).** The same machinery holds the icon of each platform a call can be on (§29.9): `zoom`, `meet` and `teams` are three more names the `agent_icons.harness` column admits (`models.ICON_PLATFORMS`; `ICON_SLOTS` is the eight, harnesses first, in the order Settings lists them), so the PNG and WebP check by bytes, the 256 KB and 512-pixel limits, the routes (`PUT /api/agent-icons/zoom/light`, `DELETE /api/agent-icons/zoom`, `GET …`), who may change them (an admin), the cache key, the event (`icons`, with `harness` naming the platform), the `icons` frame and the stylesheet are the harnesses' own, with no copy of any. `GET /api/agent-icons` lists the three as `platforms` beside the five `harnesses`, so the page builds its cards from what the broker says. `unknown`, the platform a hosted call may report when it doesn't say, has no slot: the page draws only its own generic glyph for it. The stylesheet writes `.avatar.h-zoom` for a platform with an upload, which is the class a call's avatar has (`.avatar.call.h-zoom`), so the Calls row, the call's details and the call bar show the upload with no other code. The rule is at the same weight as the built-in logo's (`.avatar.h-zoom`, §29.9) and comes after it, so the upload wins, hides the glyph a call with no platform draws, and replaces the logo's white tile with a transparent one; with nothing stored there is no rule, and the built-in logo shows. **Where:** Settings → Appearance → Agent icons, under the five harnesses' cards, a **Call icons** heading with one line ("A call in a room shows its platform's logo beside its name, until you upload an image of your own.") and a card each for Zoom, Meet and Teams, the same Upload, Replace and Reset, "The built-in icon" where a harness says "The built-in avatar". **Schema 43** is `V42_TO_V43`: the same schema-text edit as schema 35's (the harness list in the table's `sql` gains `'zoom','meet','teams'`, verified inside the migration, failing closed with schema 42 and its backup intact), in one step with the ALTER that adds `meetings.live_at` (§54). `tests/unit/test_db_migrate_v43.py` (a real 0.37.2 database through 42, an avatar in every old slot and a live call, then 43: every row and byte kept, the platforms storable and any other name refused, the CHECKs kept, the table's text the fresh schema's, an unmatched CHECK failing closed, a failing step rolled back), `tests/unit/test_agent_icons_store.py`, `tests/integration/test_agent_icons_web.py` (an admin's upload and reset of each platform, the same refusals as for a harness, an unknown name a 404, a person who is not an admin refused with 403) and `tests/e2e/test_calls_ui.py` (the Settings cards, an upload shown in the Calls row, the details and the call bar in light and dark, Reset bringing the logo back, and a person who is not an admin offered no upload).
- **Not included.** Re-encoding an upload (a nice-to-have the issue allowed only if it came free: it did not, without an imaging library), a crop or resize in the page, separate images per agent rather than per harness, and avatars on the review board.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/test_agent_icons.py` (accepted files, every cap, SVG and mislabelled files, every truncation and flipped byte, malformed headers in both formats, the stylesheet's shape), `tests/unit/test_agent_icons_store.py`, `tests/unit/test_db_migrate_v32.py` (a real 0.37.2 database migrated to 32 keeping every row, the table's CHECKs), `tests/unit/test_db_migrate_v35.py` (the same database through 34 with an avatar in every old slot, then 35: every row and byte kept, OpenCode accepted, every other CHECK kept and the table text the fresh schema's, a CHECK spelled differently refused with schema 34 and its backup intact, a failing step rolled back, #518), `tests/integration/test_agent_icons_web.py` (upload, the headers, the cache key, refusals that change nothing, a streamed body cut at the cap, owner only on a hosted broker, signed-out, events, the frame, the CSP), and `tests/e2e/test_agent_icons_ui.py` (the heading and tip by keyboard and tap, the upload and Reset flow in light, dark and the saved Theme, OpenCode's upload over its glyph in Members, the log, a reply's preview, the @ list and the Inspector and Reset back to the glyph, every place on a desktop and a phone, refusals in the page, a second page following live, bob seeing no section). The pictures are generated (`tests/fakes/pictures.py`).

## 30. The container image and the public URL (#34)

The broker can run as a container behind a platform's HTTPS: Render, EKS or GKE, a VM with Caddy. Two `start` settings make that possible, `--listen` and `--public-url`, plus a health route and the image itself, published to GHCR on every release. Nothing changes by default: a broker listens on anything but loopback only when both settings are given. On day one a hosted broker is the human in the browser. Your own machines' agents joining it, and signing in without exec, are #41; other people and their agents are #24. How to run it is in docs/DEPLOY.md.

### 30.1 Settings
- **`--listen ADDR`** (`SWITCHBOARD_LISTEN`, config.toml `listen`): the IPv4 address to listen on, `127.0.0.1` by default. `tcp_listener` binds it with `IPPROTO_TCP`, as `loopback_listener` does, so accepted sockets keep TCP_NODELAY (§25).
- **`--public-url URL`** (`SWITCHBOARD_PUBLIC_URL`, config.toml `public_url`): where browsers reach the UI. `WebOrigin.parse` accepts an origin only: the scheme, a DNS name or IPv4 address, and a port if it isn't the default. No path, query, fragment or user info. It lowercases the host and drops `:443`/`:80`, as browsers send them. It must be `https://`, except for a local test host (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `*.localhost`, `*.test`), because the cookie and the sign-in links would otherwise cross a network in the clear.
- **`--port`** also reads `SWITCHBOARD_PORT` and is checked to be 0..65535. **`--log-stdout`** (with `--foreground`) sends the broker's log to stdout instead of `logs/broker.log`: the same records, ids and never text.
- **`daemon.web_settings`** checks both before the broker takes its lock or creates its home. Any listen address but `127.0.0.1` needs a public URL, because `switchboard.localhost` resolves to `127.0.0.1` and nowhere else. A flag or variable wins over config.toml.

### 30.2 What the public URL changes
One value, `WebOrigin` (scheme and host), replaces every hard-coded `switchboard.localhost:<port>`. Without a public URL it is `http://switchboard.localhost:<bound port>`, exactly as before.
- **`HostOriginGuard`:** `Host` must be the public URL's host exactly, else 421 with `open <public URL>/` (a WebSocket is refused before accept). Unsafe methods need `Origin` to be the public origin, and `X-Switchboard: 1`.
- **`/ws`:** its Origin check uses the same origin.
- **The CSP:** `connect-src 'self' wss://<host>` for an https URL. `app.js` already picks `wss:` on an https page.
- **The session cookie** is `Secure` when the URL is https: at sign-in, on every sliding refresh (`GET /api/me`) and on the logout deletion. `_set_cookie` takes `secure` as a required argument, because a refresh without it would replace the Secure cookie with one a plain-http request could carry. (The first version missed the refresh, and the public-URL tests caught it.)
- **The public URL is what the broker prints and reports:** sign-in links (`human.login_link`), `switchboard status`, `start`'s output, and a new `url` in `sys.ping`, which `start` prints for a broker that is already running.
- Nothing else changes: tokens, sessions, the UDS and its peer checks, rooms and delivery.

### 30.3 `/healthz`
`GET` or `HEAD /healthz` answers `ok` (200, `no-store`) with no session and no data. It deliberately leaves out the server's version: the endpoint answers anyone, with any Host, so a version there would be a fingerprint. An agent learns the version behind its token instead (§49.2). It is the one route exempt from the Host check, because platforms probe the container's own address. The exemption is exact: another method, or any other path with a foreign Host, is still 421, and the security headers apply as everywhere.

### 30.4 Threat model
- **TLS ends at the proxy.** The hop from the proxy to the container is plain HTTP on the platform's private network, so the session cookie and sign-in tokens cross it in the clear. That is the usual trust in a platform's router or ingress. Render's router also redirects plain HTTP to HTTPS before the broker sees it, and the proxy may add HSTS.
- **No forwarded headers are trusted** (uvicorn's `proxy_headers=False`). The scheme and host come from the configuration, never from `X-Forwarded-Proto` or `X-Forwarded-Host`, so a forged header changes nothing.
- **A client that reaches the container directly** (not through the proxy) can send any Host. The Host and Origin rules stop what a browser can be made to do, such as DNS rebinding or a page on another origin, and they keep every cookie, link and origin naming one address. They are not access control. That is the session: every `/api` route and `/ws` need one, a session needs a one-time token, and bad tokens are rate-limited before any write (§5.4). What is public is the same as on the desktop: the sign-in page, the static files, and now `/healthz`.
- **Signing in:** `switchboard login` runs inside the container, in a terminal (`docker exec -it`, `kubectl exec -it`, Render's Shell). Whoever can exec into the container can sign in, so platform access is switchboard access. The peer check sees only the container's own process tree: `docker exec` shows no parent above the command, so it can't tell that an agent on your laptop ran `docker exec`. Keep platform credentials (a kubeconfig, a Render API key) out of agents' reach. Signing in without exec (a claim link in the log, then passkeys) is #41; sign-in through an identity provider isn't planned.
- **Tokens in URLs:** a sign-in link's token passes through the proxy, which may log request paths. A token works once and is spent when the browser follows the link, so a logged one is already used. It also expires after 5 minutes.
- **Root in the image:** the image starts as root where a platform's disk belongs to root (Render). Its `switchboard` command (`deploy/image/switchboard.sh`) uses root for one thing only, and only as PID 1, when the container starts and before any process of the unprivileged user exists: it makes `$SWITCHBOARD_HOME` that user's private directory. It refuses a symlink or a non-directory, and never recurses. Then `setpriv` drops to uid and gid 10001 with only that group, no inheritable or bounding capabilities, and no_new_privs, and runs tini (PID 1, unprivileged too) and the broker. A root `docker exec … switchboard` only drops privileges and touches no file, so a compromised broker has no root step to race.
- **One broker per volume:** the existing `flock` on the home refuses a second broker, and the examples run one replica, replaced stop-then-start, and the Helm chart (#608) has no replicas value at all. SQLite stays on a block device; NFS and EFS are ruled out in the docs, not in code.

### 30.5 The image
- **Build:** the `Dockerfile` (`deploy/image/`) has two stages, with the Python and uv bases pinned by digest. uv installs the dependencies from `uv.lock` (`--frozen --no-dev`), then switchboard as a wheel (`--no-editable`), into `/opt/switchboard`. The runtime is that same slim Python image with that venv and tini: no uv, compiler, git or ssh. The published image `ghcr.io/amahpour/switchboard:<version>` stays tag-only. `release.py` writes that tag in the release pull request, before the release workflow has built the image, so its digest does not exist yet. Every other image the tree names (joinly's CPU and CUDA builds, Caddy, the sandbox base, the Kubernetes probe and the Tart VM) is `name:tag@sha256` of the registry's index, or of the single manifest when the tag is not a list. A test fails if a third-party image is named without a digest.
- **Layout:** `SWITCHBOARD_HOME=/data/switchboard` on the `/data` volume, `SWITCHBOARD_LISTEN=0.0.0.0`, `SWITCHBOARD_PORT=7419`, and no public URL (the container refuses to start without one). The command is `start --foreground --log-stdout`, and a Docker `HEALTHCHECK` calls `/healthz` with the standard library, ignoring proxy variables.
- **Why the home is a subdirectory:** Kubernetes' `fsGroup` makes a volume's root `root:10001` with mode 2770, which the broker's private-directory check (its own uid, no group or other bits, §2) refuses. A directory the broker creates inside it is its own and stays 0700 (2700 with the inherited setgid bit). `fsGroupChangePolicy: OnRootMismatch` keeps the kubelet from re-chmodding it on later mounts.
- **`ps`:** the slim image has none, and the sign-in check needs the caller's controlling terminal. On Linux, `proc.tty()` now names it from `/proc/<pid>/stat`'s `tty_nr` (`pts/0`), as the rest of `proc.py` already reads `/proc`. `ps` is left for other systems.
- **Stopping:** uvicorn handles SIGTERM with a graceful shutdown, then raises the signal again for the handler it replaced. `run_foreground` installs a no-op handler there, so the broker's own cleanup runs (the pidfile, the lock) and the exit status is 0. `docker stop` takes well under a second.
- **Publishing:** test.yml's `image` job builds the image for linux/amd64 on every PR that runs the tests, and runs `tests/image` against it. After a release, release.yml's `publish-image` calls `.github/workflows/image.yml`. It builds the release's tag for linux/amd64 and linux/arm64 (QEMU), and pushes `ghcr.io/amahpour/switchboard:<version>` with a provenance attestation (`mode=max`) and an SBOM. It also pushes `:latest` when the version is the newest tag, so rebuilding an old release never moves `latest` back. `release.py` moves the image pins in the deployment examples and docs/DEPLOY.md together with the install pins.

The broker's Kubernetes and Helm memory limit is `1Gi` (#690), with the scheduling
requests unchanged at `192Mi` and `100m` CPU. This limit belongs to the broker
container; joinly is sized separately. [DEPLOY.md, Sizing](DEPLOY.md#sizing) records
measurements and their limits. `scripts/bench_broker.py` runs fresh test-mode
brokers and FakeAgent clients in temporary homes, with delivery throttles off,
and measures HTTP/MCP calls and the broker's RSS/CPU while agents hold waits.
`tests/integration/test_bench_broker.py` exercises every phase with two agents;
the chart tests pin both deployment defaults to `1Gi`.

### 30.6 Tests
- **Unit** (`test_web_origin.py`): `WebOrigin.parse`, with good URLs and every kind of bad one; `web_settings` and its refusals, which happen before the home exists; the config keys; the flags and their variables; a port range; `start` passing the settings to its child and printing the reported URL; `tcp_listener`. Also, in `test_proc.py`, Linux tty names and a real session leader in a pty (named as `ps` names it, where there is a `ps`).
- **Integration** (`test_public_url.py`), a broker behind `https://sb.example.com`, reached as the proxy reaches it: Host (421 for everything else, the local name included), the sign-in link and the Secure cookie (with its refresh and its deletion), writes that need the public Origin, the CSP, the WebSocket's Host, Origin and cookie, `status` and `ping`, and `/healthz` in both modes with every near miss.
