Like the other two it comes only from the file or the environment, never from room text. The allowlist guards the broker's side only: joinly takes any `model_name` from whoever connects to it, which is one more reason it must stay private (§11).

What the setting does in joinly (v0.5.4, read from its image): `WhisperSTT` takes `model_name`; with none it picks `base` on a CPU and `distil-large-v3` on CUDA and loads it with `local_files_only=True`; with any `model_name` that is not `None`, even an empty string, `local_files_only` is off and faster-whisper asks Hugging Face for the model, using the files it already has when nothing is newer and, if Hugging Face can't be reached, the copy in its cache. Naming `base` is therefore a lookup on the network too, though it downloads nothing. The model is built when a call's session opens, before the bot joins, once for each call, so the first call that names a model not yet in the cache waits for the download (`large-v3-turbo` is about 1.6 GB), and every live call holds its own copy. So the bridge puts `model_name` in `stt_args` only when `stt_model` is set, and leaves the key out when it is empty (an empty string would turn the lookups on for an install that named nothing). The cache is `/home/app/.cache/huggingface` in joinly's container, and a volume there keeps the download ([DEPLOY.md](DEPLOY.md#the-speech-model-and-a-gpu)). switchboard does not fetch the model: the operator does, ahead, with joinly's own library.

The GPU is a deployment choice and no broker code: joinly's `-cuda` image sets its own device. The proof of concept (#617) put the numbers behind the recommendation: on 24 recorded lines `large-v3-turbo` got 2.4% of the words wrong against 4.3% for `base`, `distil-large-v3` (joinly's choice on CUDA) 4.8%, the large models run at 0.83 to 0.90 of real time on four CPUs and about 3.5 times faster on a GTX 1660 Super, and every model woke the right agent for every call-out in the recordings. So the default stays `base` on a CPU, and the deploy examples suggest `large-v3-turbo` for the GPU: Compose has a `meetings-gpu` profile, the Helm chart `meetings.gpu.enabled` and `meetings.sttModel`, and the Kubernetes patch the same as a comment. The chart adds a writable `emptyDir` at the cache when a model is named (joinly's root is read-only), and the GPU request, `nodeSelector` and `tolerations` only while the GPU is on.

**The first word of a line (#620).** joinly's voice detection (Silero, 32 ms windows) flags speech late: in the recorded call of #617 a median of 73 ms after the first audible sound, a p90 of 214 ms and a maximum of 378 ms. `BasePaddedVAD` keeps one window from before the flag, so the piece Whisper gets starts partway into the first word, and the room's names, which are the hotwords, turn a clipped "Our" or "Hey" into "Claude": a line wakes Claude for nothing, or the name that opens a call-out is lost. The model is not the cause (`large-v3-turbo` makes the same false wake). Replaying that call (24 lines, 17 call-outs, `base`, four 8 ms offsets for a live call's jitter) through joinly 0.5.4's own `SileroVAD`, controller loop and `WhisperSTT._buffer_windows` gave what the live run did: 14.8 of 17 call-outs woke the right agent, one line woke Claude for nothing, and 8.8% of the words were wrong.

The fix is in joinly's `DefaultTranscriptionController`, not in the VAD. While no utterance is open the controller keeps the last 0.3 s of non-speech windows (ten of 32 ms), and when one opens it puts them at the front of its window queue, marked as speech (`WhisperSTT` starts its buffer only on a speech window), with their own times and speakers. In the VAD, keeping k windows back would delay every non-speech window, and so the end of every utterance, by k windows; the controller already has the audio, so nothing waits (+0.01 s median in the replay). The utterance's start time and the `no_speech_event` logic read the VAD's own windows, as before, and the pre-roll goes straight into the queue. On the replay it gives 16 of 17, no false wake and 3.5% of the words wrong, at all four offsets. What it leaves alone: a piece that starts inside an utterance, after a pause shorter than the 0.6 s tail, still starts at the VAD's flag. That is 4 of 30 pieces in the recording, and a ring in Whisper's own buffering for them changed the word errors from 3.5% to 3.3% and no call-out, so it is not built. One side effect: an utterance's segment times begin up to 0.3 s earlier, and `meeting/lines.py` measures its 2 s pause (`PAUSE_S`) between segments, so two utterances less than about 2.3 s apart can now join into one line.

The pre-roll leaves one call-out missed on the replay: "Hi Codex, how many tests failed last night?" is cut at the comma into "High Codex" and the rest, because joinly's Whisper cuts a piece after 0.2 s of quiet once it holds 0.4 s. The bridge asks for 0.35 s and 1.0 s (`STT_MIN_SILENCE_S`, `STT_MIN_AUDIO_S`), and with the pre-roll that gave 17 of 17, no false wake and 3.1% of the words wrong at all four offsets, for 0.14 s more delay (median; 0.20 at the 90th percentile, against joinly's defaults over three runs). Without the pre-roll they gave 16 (15 to 17) and still the false wake, so they depend on it. Twenty-four lines cannot tell one call-out from noise: this is the one line, and it is the same one at every offset.

The setting is `utterance_preroll_seconds` of `transcription_controller_args`, and its default is the fix (0.3; 0 turns it off). The broker does not send it: joinly refuses a setting it does not know, so a broker that sent a new key would break every call for an install that has not mounted the file. The keys in the header stay exactly joinly's, and a joinly without the file works as before. The change is joinly's, offered upstream as joinly-ai/joinly#188; until a joinly release has it, the file goes over the image's copy as the Zoom fix does (§52, DEPLOY.md): read-only, from a commit of our fork, in Compose, in the Helm chart as `meetings.prerollFix.configMap` and in a third Kubernetes patch, `joinly-preroll-fix.yaml`.

**The command** (§10). Role `human`, so a person in the web UI. An agent's `say("/meeting …")` stays literal text, as every command does.

- `/meeting <link>` starts the room's call. Only `https` links are accepted, and only on Zoom (`zoom.us` and its subdomains), Google Meet (`meet.google.com`) and Teams (`teams.microsoft.com`, `teams.live.com`), with a real path and no userinfo. Anything else is refused without echoing the link: joinly's browser would open any URL, and this keeps it off internal addresses. One open call per room. A second start while one is joining or live is refused. The broker as a whole follows at most `max_calls`: a start that would make more calls joining or live, counted over every room (`Store.count_open_meetings`), is refused with `This broker follows at most N calls at once, and that many are open. Leave one first (/meeting leave in its room).` (409; "call" for 1). The check runs after the room's own, so a room that already has a call hears "already transcribing", and before a row is written or the link goes to the bridge, so a refused start leaves no row, no notice and no join. The message gives only the number: the person who asks may not be able to see the rooms that hold the calls, so it names neither them nor a link. A call counts from the moment it is joining, because the bot's browser is already starting, and stops counting when it ends, whether by `/meeting leave`, a failed join, a dropped connection or a restart. A call that has been asked to leave still counts until the bot has left, which is when its memory comes back.
- `/meeting <link> mine` asks the person's own agent to host it without a model seeing the link (§54); the server bot does not join.
- `/meeting leave` ends it.
- `/meeting voice on` and `/meeting voice off` let agents speak in the call, or stop them (**Voice**).
- `/meeting listen <name>` opts that agent into call chatter, and `/meeting listen <name> off` stops it (§52.1).
- `/meeting snapshot` posts a picture of the call's shared screen (**Snapshots**).
- `/meeting` alone says whether a call is live.

All forms except `/meeting listen <name> off` need the web session, the role `human`: an agent's `/meeting ...` is text, as it is for every command, so an agent can never turn voice on or take a snapshot. The two new forms answer "no call is being transcribed" when there is no call.

**What is stored.** Schema 39 adds `'meeting'` to the `messages.sender_kind` and `messages.via` checks (the same `sqlite_master` rewrite as the earlier check changes, after the usual backup) and a `meetings` table: `id`, `room_id`, `state` (`joining`, `live`, `ended`), `started_at`, `ended_at`, `started_by`, `platform` (`zoom`, `meet`, `teams`) and `error`. `meetings_one_open` is a unique index on `room_id` where the state is not `ended`. The full link, query string included (`pwd=`), is held in memory only long enough to pass to `join_meeting`. The row, the notices, the `meeting_join` and `meeting_leave` events and switchboard's own log keep the platform and nothing of the link. The command text is not stored as a chat message. A client library that would log the tool arguments has `https://…` and `pwd=` redacted as the record is created.

**The bridge.** Finished lines enter `ingest.receive`, the same checked posting path as hosted calls (§54). It binds the checked line to the call, then `Incoming.post` stores and delivers it after any snapshot. Both sources store `meeting_id`; the server bot keeps no machine label and its visible behavior is unchanged. `meeting/bridge.py` is the only async part: one `MeetingBridge` on the broker, started and stopped with the lifespan the way `delivery/runner.py` is. One MCP session per call (`fastmcp.Client`, `StreamableHttpTransport`, protocol mode `legacy`, so `resources/subscribe` still exists). The `joinly-settings` header carries the participant name `switchboard (transcribing)` and the room's agents' spoken names as hotwords, captured when the call starts. It subscribes to `transcript://live/segments` and also reads that resource every 3 seconds. Each new participant segment (any other role, including the bot's own speech, is skipped) goes to `lines.py`. `leave_meeting`, then the client is closed, on `/meeting leave`, on room close, on a join failure or a dropped connection, and on broker shutdown. A row still `joining` or `live` when the process starts again is marked ended: that session is gone, so the bot cannot be asked to leave.

**Voice (#593).** Off when a call starts. A person turns it on or off with `/meeting voice on|off` or the **Voice** switch on the bar, which sends the same command. Each change leaves a notice in the room, and saying the state it is already in changes nothing and leaves none. Whether it is on is a set of room ids in the bridge: memory only. A call does not survive a restart (the row is marked ended, above), so neither does its voice, and the next call starts with it off. The room frame's `settings.meeting` is `{state, platform, voice}`, `voice` a boolean, and an older frame without it reads as off.

While voice is on and the bot is in the call, an agent that is a member of the room speaks by starting a `say()` with `@call`. `AgentService._post_say`, which every agent post goes through (the immediate path and a queued one), hands the posted text to `MeetingBridge.speak` after the message is stored and delivered, so the room has the message as written. `speak` does nothing unless voice is on, the bot is in the call, and the text starts with `@call` as a whole word (`@call-1` and `@calls` do not). Otherwise the `@call` is plain text: there is no error and no refusal. `call` is a reserved screen name (`RESERVED_NAMES`, §6.1), so it can never be a member's name and a `@call` is never also an @mention. What is spoken is `meeting/speech.py`'s `speakable`, a pure function: control characters and Markdown marks dropped, a link's text kept, a bare URL, an HTML tag and an artifact line left out, a fenced code block replaced by "code omitted", lines joined with a full stop where they had none, and the whole cut to 600 characters at a word boundary. Nothing left to say speaks nothing. The line goes to joinly's `speak_text` tool in a task of its own, so a `say()` never waits on speech (`speak_text` returns when the line has been said). Turn-taking (§52.2) allows one outstanding line, waits for a speech gap, expires a waiting line after 60 seconds and starts at most one every 30 seconds. A second line is refused with a notice also queued for its author. Turning voice off empties the wait, and the line being spoken finishes. joinly answers an interrupted line with a message that starts `Interrupted`; the room gets a notice that someone spoke over it. A failed `speak_text` is one notice. The task ends with the call.

How an agent learns it is spoken is one line, `Voice is on in this call: start a say() line with @call to have it spoken.`, added by `Engine._render` to a batch that carries a call line while the room's voice is on (`Engine.voice_on`, set by the broker, is `None` in the engine's own tests: off everywhere). It is not in the tools, their parameters or the join text, so the tool list's character budget and `JOIN_BUDGET` are as they were.

The voice goes to joinly with the other settings in the `joinly-settings` header, as `tts_args: {"voice": <tts_voice>}`. joinly's `Settings` model refuses a key it does not know and then ignores the whole header, so the keys are exactly joinly's: `tts_args` holds the arguments of its TTS service, and Kokoro's is `voice`. The speech model goes the same way, in `stt_args` beside the hotwords, as `model_name`, only when `stt_model` is set. So do two arguments of Whisper's piece cutting, `min_silence` 0.35 and `min_audio` 1.0, always (#620, below); both are arguments of joinly v0.5.4's `WhisperSTT`.

**Snapshots (#593).** A person asks with `/meeting snapshot` or the **Snapshot** button (enabled once the bot is in the call). A call line also asks: one that addresses an agent (a call-out, `names.match` says `one`; an ambiguous name addresses nobody) and has the whole word "screen" (`screen.wants_snapshot`, pure). The picture is taken first, with an 8 second limit, and posted before that line, so the agent the line wakes has both in its batch. A failed or refused snapshot never holds the line back. The picture comes from joinly's `get_video_snapshot`, an MCP image: base64 data and a type. joinly takes it by screenshotting the bot's own browser page, cropping the edges and scaling it to 1024 by 576 as a JPEG, in code that is the same for Zoom, Meet and Teams; so whether a shared screen is in it depends on what that platform's web page shows, and a small frame cannot show small text.

The reply is untrusted bytes. The size is checked on the base64 text against `[files] max_mb` before anything is decoded, the type is read from the bytes (`screen.image_kind`: the PNG signature, a JPEG's `FF D8 FF`, or `RIFF....WEBP`), and the type joinly names is not looked at. Anything else (HTML, SVG, a GIF) is refused with a notice and nothing is stored. A good frame is kept as a room file by `Attachments.store_snapshot` (§53: the folder store, the room's total cap, retention, delete with the room), named `screen-<HHMMSS>.<png|jpg|webp>`, and posted as a message holding the artifact line `artifact: <name> | <size> | sha256:<hash> | switchboard:file/<id>`. The page draws it as a download card, and an agent the message reaches gets the usual short-lived `download:` line. From a person's command or button it is that person's chat message (`human_say`, so it reaches every agent that is not quiet, as an attached file does). From a call line it is a `meeting` message from `call`, unmentioned, so it never starts a turn and rides the wake of the line that asked. Schema 41 adds `meeting` to the `files.uploader_kind` CHECK for that one, the same schema-text edit as schema 39 (rows, indexes and keys untouched, the usual backup first), so a file the call took is not recorded as a person's.

At most one snapshot every 10 seconds in a call, by the broker's clock, however it was asked for. A second inside that is refused: the person's command answers with the reason, and a call-out's line is posted anyway with a notice and no new picture. A try that failed counts, so a broken joinly is not asked in a loop.

**Lines.** `lines.py` is pure and takes a clock. Consecutive participant segments become one line until 2.0 seconds of gap on the call's audio clock, or 2.5 seconds of wall clock with nothing new, or 1,000 characters, or a different speaker. Segments arrive in batches about every 3 seconds, so the wall-clock pause is checked only once nothing new has arrived, and a call-out stays in the same line as the rest of its sentence. The room posts at most one line a second. Past 50 waiting, the oldest is dropped and one warning is posted. A line that is only the transcriber's hint list is dropped when its turn comes (**Names**).

**Names.** `names.py` is pure. `claude-1` is spoken `claude one`, `claude 1`, `claude-1` and `claude number one`; digits go up to twenty as words. A match is whole words, case-insensitive, and only an address: the name at the start of the line, after "hey", "hi", "ok" or "okay", or followed by a comma. "I asked Claude yesterday" is not a call-out. A bare base name (`Claude`, `Codex`) matches when exactly one active member's name starts with it. Two members that share it (`Claude` with claude-1 and claude-2) are ambiguous: the line is posted with no mention, then a notice, `Someone on the call said "Claude", and this room has claude-1 and claude-2. Say the number.` One match sets `mentions` to that member's screen name. During unclear audio Whisper sometimes writes its hint list back ("Claude Claude one Codex switchboard", twice in the spike), so `match` takes that out of the line first (`names._find_echo`). A run of three or more hint words in a row that name two or more members, or the participant's own name with one, is an echo. So is a line that is nothing but hint words, with at least one member's name and one of the bot's own words (`switchboard`, `transcribing`), at any length (#616): on a real call the transcript had "Claude switchboard", two words, one under the run of three, so "Claude" at the start counted as an address and the line woke claude-1 with nothing to act on. Names alone ("Claude one") are a call-out, and the bot's word alone, or inside a sentence, is speech. A line that is only echo addresses nobody and is not posted: `Match.echo` is true, and `_post_line` returns before `post_meeting_line`, so the room does not read a line no one said, and agents do not read it as part of the call. It still takes its turn in the queue, so the one-line-a-second gap holds. A run that real words follow is a lead-in, not a whole echo (#602): on a real Zoom call "Okay, Claude one, can you hear me?" came out as "Claude-1 Claude switchboard Claude one can you hear me?", and the whole line had counted as echo, so nobody was woken. Now the run is taken out of the line and the name right before the real words stays, as said, so the room reads "Claude one can you hear me?" (`Match.text`, which is what is posted) and that name is the address; a run that ends the line, or doesn't end in a name, addresses nobody. Words said before a run stay in the posted line, and are not looked at for an address. The sender name is the speaker joinly gave, cut to 40 characters, or `call` when it gave none. Text is cut and cleaned like any input from outside.

**Delivery.** For the matched member the line is priority 1 and mentioned. A line that names one of a member's live claims (§47, §8.1: `SYSINT-645`, `owner/repo#12`) matches its holder the same way (#541). For everyone else it is priority 0. It is never priority 2. Unless the person enabled listening (§52.1), an unmentioned meeting line never starts a turn, the same rule as quiet (§50): `chatter_due` ignores it, it does not end an open `wait()`, and it rides the next real wake. `read()` shows it. The loop guard's hop count neither increases nor resets on a meeting line. The say rate limit does not apply, because the line is not an agent's `say`. A call-out spends the wake budget the way an @mention does.

**Untrusted.** A meeting line is peer text. `envelope.untrusted_sender` is every sender kind except `human` and `system`, so a kind the code does not know fails closed the same way. Framing, the stub on the Stop path, `peer_inline` and the read-first rule treat it like another agent's text. Nothing that `human` grants is granted here: not priority 2, not an unaddressed question, not a rate-limit exemption for a reply to a person, not a notice that has to come from `system`. The envelope labels the line `kind=meeting`, from `call` or the speaker, so an agent can see it came from a transcript. A spoken "/pause" is only words.

**The page.** (The call is also a member of the room, in Members and with its own details: §54, "The call as a member".) While a call is joining or live, the room shows a bar: `Transcribing a call (Zoom)` (or Meet, or Teams), or `Joining a Zoom call…` until it is live. **Leave call** sends `/meeting leave` and puts the focus back on the composer. A transcript line is smaller than a chat message, tagged `call`, with the speaker's name when there is one. The room frame's `settings.meeting` is `{state, platform}` or null, and never a link. An unknown platform is not shown.

**Threat model.** Anyone on the call can say anything, a played recording included. Without listening (§52.1), that text has the same reach as another agent's @mention: it can wake the agent who was named, or who holds a claim the line names, and an agent with approvals off then acts without asking. Keep such an agent out of a room that has a call. Tell the people on the call that it is being transcribed; the name in the participant list says so. joinly has no sign-in and accepts its settings from the client that connected, so it stays on localhost or the compose network and is not published on an open address. On Kubernetes it is a second container of the broker's pod (`deploy/kubernetes/meetings/joinly.yaml`, [DEPLOY.md](DEPLOY.md#meetings-on-kubernetes)): the containers of a pod share one network, so it listens on the pod's `127.0.0.1` and has no Service and no declared port. Its image listens on `0.0.0.0` by default, which would give every pod in the cluster its port at the pod's IP, so the patch sets `JOINLY_SERVER_HOST=127.0.0.1`; `tests/unit/test_deploy_joinly.py` keeps that, the missing port, the exec probes and the security context, and `tests/unit/test_deploy_helm.py` holds the Helm chart (which renders the same container) to the patch. The transcript is stored as ordinary room messages (a private room, export and delete cover it) and nowhere else. The passcode is never stored, shown or logged. `max_calls` is the guard on the bot's memory: joinly has no limit of its own, and a bot asked for more calls than its memory holds is killed for going over it, and every call on it ends. It only tightens: the default is 1, a start past it is refused before it reaches joinly, and raising it is the operator's decision, in the file or the environment, with the memory to match (`2.7Gi × max_calls + 0.5Gi`, [DEPLOY.md](DEPLOY.md#meetings-on-kubernetes)). A room message can never change it.

Spoken text reaches everyone on the call, which is why voice is a person's choice per call and off by default. An agent can be prompted by what someone on the call says, or by anything else it reads, to say something it should not, and with voice on that is said aloud to every participant, where a message in the room is seen only by the room. The text is the agent's, cut to 600 characters, with code, addresses and artifact lines left out, but it is not otherwise filtered. Keep voice off unless the agents in the room are ones you would let speak for you. Nothing an agent can do turns it on. A snapshot can capture anything on the shared screen, including what the sharer did not mean to show: it becomes a room file under the room's retention (30 days unless an admin changes it, §53), readable by everyone who can read the room, and the card is in the history until the room is deleted. Take one only when you mean to, tell the sharer, and shorten retention for a call that shows something private. The picture is never executed or served inline: it is a download of opaque bytes (§53), kept only if its bytes are a PNG, a JPEG or a WebP.

**Not in this step.** The bot does not share its screen. It does not speak on its own: only a person's switch lets an agent's `@call` line out. A snapshot is the bot's view, one frame, 1024 by 576, at the time it is asked for: no video and no reading of what is on it. One call per room, and at most `max_calls` in the broker. No recognition of a voice beyond the name joinly supplies. On Zoom, joinly 0.5.4 supplies none: in a browser without graphics acceleration Zoom shows one tile that follows the speaker, which its lookup doesn't know; joinly-ai/joinly#187 fixes that.

**Tests.** `tests/unit/test_meeting_names.py`, `tests/unit/test_meeting_lines.py`, `tests/unit/test_db_migrate_v39.py`, `tests/unit/test_engine_meetings.py` (a `FakeClock`: an unmentioned line never wakes, a mention wakes only that agent and the earlier lines ride along, the hop count stays put). `tests/fakes/fake_joinly.py` (which also records `speak_text`, can hold a line mid-speech and answer it as interrupted, and returns a small PNG from `get_video_snapshot`) and `tests/integration/test_meetings.py` (hotwords and the participant name, a call-out, talk that names nobody, a line that is only the hint list neither posted nor waking anyone, an echoed lead-in posted without its echo, the ambiguous notice, leave on `/meeting leave`, room close and shutdown, a link off the list, the passcode absent from the database, the notices and the log, an agent's command left as text; and the limit (#607): a second room refused with only the number in the answer, no row and no join for it, the room that holds the call told "already" first, a leave freeing the slot, `max_calls = 2` starting two and refusing a third, and a call that is joining counting while one that failed to join stops; `FakeJoinly.hold_join` keeps a join at the door for that). `tests/unit/test_store_meetings.py` (the count over rooms and states). `tests/e2e/test_meetings_ui.py` (the bar, Leave call, the line, light, dark and a phone). `tests/unit/test_config.py` (the `SWITCHBOARD_JOINLY_URL` variable: used with no file, wins over the file, a blank one leaves the file's, a bad one is a startup error that names it; the same for `SWITCHBOARD_MEETING_MAX_CALLS` and the `max_calls` key, 1 to 50 and nothing else; and for `SWITCHBOARD_MEETING_STT_MODEL` and the `stt_model` key, the four names and nothing else, a repository id and a path refused, the variable's error not repeating its value) and `tests/unit/test_deploy_joinly.py` (the Kubernetes patch keeps joinly on the pod's localhost with no port, probed from inside, with the context it was seen to start in).

For the speech model and the GPU (#617): `tests/integration/test_meetings.py` (`stt_args.model_name` in the `joinly-settings` header when `stt_model` is set, left out when it is not, with the hotwords still there), `tests/unit/test_config.py` (above), `tests/unit/test_deploy_helm.py` (`meetings.gpu.enabled` renders the `-cuda` image and one `nvidia.com/gpu` in the limits and nothing else changes, the selector and tolerations only with the GPU on, `meetings.sttModel` as the broker's variable with a writable folder at joinly's cache, nothing of either by default, and the schema refusing what the broker would refuse), `tests/unit/test_deploy_compose.py` (the `meetings-gpu` profile: the same release's `-cuda` image, one reserved NVIDIA device, no port, and the CPU bot without a `deploy` block), and `tests/unit/test_deploy_joinly.py` (the patch's GPU comment follows its image tag and asks for nothing live; DEPLOY.md names the variable, the models, the profile and the cache).

For the first word (#620): `tests/integration/test_meetings.py` (`stt_args` carries `min_silence` and `min_audio`, and only keys that joinly v0.5.4's `WhisperSTT` takes), `tests/unit/test_deploy_helm.py` (`meetings.prerollFix.configMap` mounts the one file read-only, with its volume only when named, and nothing of it otherwise), `tests/unit/test_deploy_joinly.py` (the third patch over the file DEPLOY.md names, and one commit of the fork in the docs, the patch and the self-test), and the self-test, which mounts the file on both bots, fetched and checked by its sha256, on every platform. The change itself is tested in joinly's own suite (`tests/test_transcription_controller.py`, on the fork), which runs the real `BasePaddedVAD` and controller over a scripted call and checks what the STT is given: the pre-roll, its marking, times and speakers, none inside an utterance, none repeated across two, and `0` as it was before.

For voice and snapshots (#593): `tests/unit/test_meeting_speech.py` (`call_text`, `speakable`, the picture check, `wants_snapshot`, `tts_voice`, the reserved name, and `store_snapshot`'s caps), `tests/unit/test_db_migrate_v41.py`, `tests/unit/test_engine_meetings.py` (the voice line in a batch with a call line), and `tests/unit/test_commands.py` (the new forms and their role). `tests/integration/test_meeting_voice.py`: voice off at the start and only a person's command changes it, with a notice each time; an `@call` line spoken only while it is on, and made speakable; a `say()` never held up by a long line, the queue's bound and its one notice; voice off dropping what waits; an interrupted line and a failed one; the voice in joinly's settings; the wake line only with call lines and voice on; a snapshot by command as a stored file, a card and a download line; a call-out that says "screen" posting the picture first; the ten-second limit by advancing the clock; HTML, a GIF and an SVG refused whatever type they claim; a failed snapshot leaving the line posted; the passcode absent from the database, the notices, the card and the log. `tests/e2e/test_meetings_ui.py` (the bar's Voice switch and Snapshot button, light, dark and a phone, from the keyboard too).

A real call, opt-in (#600, §12.4): `tests/live/test_meeting_selftest.py` runs every feature above against a live Zoom, Meet or Teams call with two real joinly bots, and `tests/unit/test_meeting_selftest.py` checks its comparing, its report, its handling of the link, and when a failed step keeps the container logs, all without a call.

### 52.1 Listening to a call (#697)

A person can let an agent wake on call chatter without being named: `/meeting listen <name>`.
This requires the web's `human` role because it increases exposure to untrusted call text.
`/meeting listen <name> off` also accepts `human_cli`, because it reduces activity.
An agent's `say()` of either command stays literal text. Both require an active room member and
an open call, hosted or server-side. Each change leaves a notice; an unchanged switch is a no-op.
Enabling for approvals off or unknown also posts the existing approvals warning. It grants no
permission and changes no approval mode. Voice remains a separate switch, off at call start.

**Storage (schema 47).** `memberships.listen` is a checked boolean, off by default.
`listen_call_id` references the current call and `listen_wakes` is its nonnegative count.
Disabling and re-enabling on that call preserves the count; a new call starts a new allowance.
Every call-end path clears its listeners in `Store.end_meeting`, including a failed join, a hosted
relay's departure, shutdown and recovery after restart. A migrated membership starts off with no
call and zero wakes; the migration keeps the old rows and a schema-46 backup.

**Timing and bounds.** Only pending, not-yet-notified call chatter from `listen_call_id` can drive
a listening wake. Older calls' lines can ride along, but cannot start a turn on a new call.
The pure `chatter_due` policy uses `[delivery] listen_quiet_s` (15 by default), measured from the
room's latest chat message, or `listen_hold_s` (40), measured from the oldest waiting call line.
These times must be greater than zero and at most 3,600 seconds. An agent's own chat posts also
move the room's quiet clock; notices do not. Ordinary `quiet_s`, `max_hold_s` and unaddressed chatter
holds keep their values. Listening explicitly opts into call chatter even for a quiet membership;
quiet still holds its ordinary unaddressed agent chatter. `wait()` uses the same call timing.
`chatter_wakes_at` uses those same deadlines for Members' next wake time.

The room's hourly budget, peer batch boundary, pause, hold, busy and approval gates all still apply.
`[delivery] listen_max_wakes` defaults to 40 (integer 1 to 10,000) for each membership on each call.
An offer driven by the listening deadline spends one, even when the batch limit leaves its
call lines for a later delivery. Ordinary chatter and priority wakes do not spend this cap. Offers count even if they expire, so failed delivery cannot buy
unbounded retries. A priority wake, explicit `read()` or `/wake` does not spend this allowance.
The last allowed listening offer turns listening off and leaves a room notice. Toggling cannot
reset the cap; a new call has its own. The counter is stored rather than held in engine memory.

**Call lines stay stubs on the Stop path.** Showing them inline in a listener's Stop wake would
cost fewer turns, but would put untrusted call text into the follow-up Claude Code treats as the
next user message. The §8.6 stub rule exists to prevent exactly that. A listener instead gets one
line near the top: `N call lines: call read("#room") to hear them.` Only a count and the cleaned
room name enter that line, and fitting reserves space for it. `read()` and `wait()` remain tool
results that show peer text inline. Opening up the Stop path is a separate issue and needs the
maintainer's yes.

Tests: `test_listen_mode.py` covers the clocks, holds, gates, private and shared caps, stored state,
old call lines, ending a call, command roles and warnings, and the #694 status-meeting replay.
`test_db_migrate_v47.py` migrates the real release fixture, checks the backup, defaults and CHECKs.
`test_meeting_listen.py` exercises the command, agent literal text and bridge departure over HTTPS.


### 52.2 Taking turns when agents speak (#697)

All `@call` speech uses one pure policy, `meeting/turns.py`, for the server bridge and a hosted
call. It covers every agent's line, including a listener's reply after the last listening wake
has switched listening off. Voice remains a person's switch; nothing here enables it.

**One slot and one start per 30 seconds.** The slot includes a queued line and a line being
spoken. A second offer is refused rather than queued behind it. The original room post stays;
a fixed notice says `Another @call line is waiting or being spoken; this line was not spoken.`
The room sees the notice, and its author gets a priority-0 delivery of it in their next batch
or `read()`. It is a system notice containing the validated member name and the fixed sentence,
never a quotation of the refused line. It does not move the chat quiet clock. Starts are at
least 30 seconds apart, measured from the preceding start. Voice off clears a waiting line;
Voice back on preserves the interval and a line still being spoken keeps its slot.

**Wait for a gap, and expire stale answers.** A new participant segment records when speech
was last heard, using the broker's clock. Replayed segments and the bot's own speech do not
move it; neither do room chat or notices. A queued line may start only after 1.5 seconds without
new speech, and after its 30-second interval. If it is still waiting at 60 seconds from its
offer, it is dropped with `A queued @call line waited 60 seconds and was dropped instead of
being spoken late.` The author gets this notice too. Expiry wins over starting at that exact
time. The bridge's worker waits on an event or the next deadline, and rechecks the clock after
either; it never holds the `say()` request. `speak_text` keeps the slot until it returns.
An interruption is still noticed and never retried.

**The hosted relay completes its exact turn.** The newest speech poll alone can claim a ready
line. Its response has one `lines` entry and a positive `speech_id` (or empty `lines` and null
`speech_id`). The slot stays occupied after polling: the broker cannot infer that the relay
has finished speaking. Only the same call's key can report `done: <speech_id>` at the existing
speech route. It must be an integer from 1 to 2^63-1; a boolean, string, negative or oversized
id refuses the request. A wrong or repeated id cannot complete another active line.

The relay reports completion with a zero-wait speech request after `speak_text` returns,
including an interruption or failure. A claimed line dropped locally before it starts (Voice
went off) is completed too, without speaking. Completion uses a separate request so it does not
wait for the long poll's timeout. A lost completion is retried without repeating speech. Every
response uses the same Voice check as the ordinary poll.
A claimed id that is malformed never means permission to speak; a valid id identifies only
one line, even if a response carries more. The call-key door, body bound, rate limit and end
checks are unchanged. An id is not a credential and grants no harness permission.

The new relay also supports an older broker with no `speech_id` field. An older relay cannot
report completion to this broker, so after its first line the slot stays occupied until the
call ends. Upgrade the broker and hosting plugin together; do not guess completion and free
an unknown slot on a timer. Turn state, including ids and the interval, lives only for the call
and resets when a new call starts. No schema changes.

Tests: `test_meeting_turns.py` uses explicit times for the gap, expiry, occupied slot, interval
and Voice toggle; `test_listen_turns.py` exercises both real broker paths with a `FakeClock`,
including the refusal in the author's next read, malformed completion ids and the hosted gap;
`test_relay_turns.py` proves that completion follows actual speech and malformed ids never
speak. Existing voice, hosted-call and relay integration tests keep the full posting path.


## 53. Attachments (#544)

A room can hold a file. The message still names it with the artifact line (§29.4). The bytes live beside the database. No new tool or parameter: the agents' tool list and `JOIN_BUDGET` stay where they are. Agents learn the line from the eighth fixed room rule (#588), which every join carries: without it nothing an agent read mentioned the line, so an agent shared a file only when a room's own rules or a pin said how. The tool list had no room for it under its approved 3,900 characters.

**Storage.** Schema 40 adds `files`, `file_grants` and `broker_settings`. A file row is its id (32 hex digits), room, name (cut to 200), size, sha256, the content type the sender claimed (kept and never used to serve), who uploaded it (`human`, `agent`, or `meeting` for a snapshot a line on a call asked for, §52), the message that names it, `pending` or `stored`, and when. The bytes are behind `FolderStore` in `files.py`: `adopt`, `path`, `delete`. The only backend is `<home>/files/<id>`, mode 0600 in a 0700 directory, written by renaming a temp file into place. A later object store can implement those three operations. It is not built.

**Limits.** `[files]` in config.toml: `max_mb` (1 to 1024, default 25) for one file, `room_mb` (default 500) for the stored bytes in one room. A `Content-Length` past the cap is refused before the body is read. A body with no length is stopped as it is read, and the partial file is deleted. Pending rows do not count toward the room total.

**A person.** The page's attach button, or a drop on the room, `POST`s the bytes to `/api/rooms/<slug>/files` (the signed-in session and `X-Switchboard`, the same check as every other write). The broker stores the file and the page posts the artifact line with `<where>` `switchboard:file/<id>`. The card's link is `fileLink` only: `/api/files/<id>`, `download` set. `safeUrl` is not loosened. Any other link to this switchboard stays blocked.

**An agent uploads with a one-time link.** `say()` text that holds an artifact line whose `<where>` is a local path (not `http(s)`, not the switchboard form; a fenced line is an example and is skipped) records a pending file. The `say()` result gains one line, and only when there is one: `To share <name>, run: curl --fail -T '<path>' <base>/api/upload/<token>`. The path is one single-quoted shell word, except that a leading `~/` becomes `"$HOME"/` before the quoted rest (#587): agents write home-relative paths, and a quoted `~` reached curl as a literal. The token is 32 random bytes, valid 10 minutes, this file only, stored as its sha256. `<base>` is the broker's public URL when it has one, otherwise the origin it answers on (`http://switchboard.localhost:<port>`), because a request to `127.0.0.1` fails the Host check. `PUT /api/upload/<token>` needs no sign-in: the token is the capability. A request that sends an `Origin` is refused, so a page cannot use the link. The size cap applies. When the line gave a digit size or a sha256, a mismatch deletes the bytes and says so; the token is then used. Over the cap does not use the token. On success the file is `stored` and the hub sends a `file` frame. Messages are not edited. The page turns the card into the download when that frame arrives, or when it loads the message's `files`.

**An agent downloads with a short-lived link.** When `read()` or a delivery shows an agent a message that names a stored file, the rendered text gains `download: <base>/api/files/<id>?t=<token>`. Ten minutes, reusable until then, not written into the message, not shown to other members, not logged. History `read(before=)` adds the same line. The token is the capability. The grant remembers which membership it was made for; the download does not check that membership, because the URL carries no agent credential.

**Downloads.** `GET /api/files/<id>`: a signed-in person `RoomService.visible` says can read the room, or a valid token. If `t` is present, only the token authorizes. Always `Content-Disposition: attachment` with a filename of token characters, `Content-Type: application/octet-stream`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. Never inline. A file in a room the caller cannot read is a 404, the same body as a missing file.

**Lifetime.** `switchboard rooms delete` removes the rows and the bytes (§28.6). Closing a room keeps them. Retention is `broker_settings` key `files_retention_days`: 1 to 365, or 0 for never. Absent means 30. A stored file is due at `stored_at` plus that many days. A pending upload is due when its token expires; one with no token expiry is due, so it cannot stay. `file_expired` is a pure function. The broker sweeps once at start and then hourly on its clock. The log names file ids, never tokens.

**The setting.** Settings → Files, for an admin (`Who.is_admin`; the owner on a desktop broker). `GET` and `PUT /api/settings/files` with `{"days": N}`. Anyone else is 403 and does not see the group. Shortening asks first, in the page's confirm dialog: "Files older than N days will be deleted at the next cleanup."

**Threat model.** An upload is untrusted bytes. It is never served as a page, so HTML or SVG in it cannot run on switchboard's origin. The content type the client sent is stored and ignored when serving. A token is a capability for one file, random, stored only as a hash, 10 minutes, an upload used once. It is not logged (the access log is off; the files log names ids) and not written into a message. The download URL exists only in the agent's tool result. Who may read a room is `RoomService.visible`, checked on every session download. A valid download token is itself permission: it was made when that agent was shown the message. A private room's file is a 404 to anyone outside it.

**Tests.** `tests/unit/test_files.py` (expiry with a `FakeClock`, the local-path parse, the download filename, the curl line). `tests/unit/test_db_migrate_v40.py`. `tests/integration/test_attachments.py` (a person's upload and the download headers, the curl line, one use, an expired token, the size cap, a sha256 mismatch deleted, a download link that works for 10 minutes, a private room's 404, room delete, the sweep, and the token absent from the database, the log and every message). `tests/e2e/test_attachments_ui.py` (attach, drop, the admin's retention warning, a non-admin, light, dark and a phone).

## 54. Hosted calls: a person's machine pushes the call in (#623)

The meeting bot of §52 can run on a person's own machine instead of beside the broker, for one call in one room, and push what it hears into the room. Their GPU and their model do the listening, and the broker's memory is not used. This is the premise (README, "Bring your own infra") applied to meetings: the infra is the person's, and the broker only receives lines. The maintainer said yes to the design in #623 on 2026-11-05 (in conversation: "Yes" to "Is #623 a yes to start building?"), and to what it adds to the guards: routes and a new way into a room (items below and §11). It first added a tool as well, which did not fit the tool list's budget; on 2026-11-05 the maintainer was asked "Should I take the tool out and have the plugin's script register the call instead?", with the cost stated as "one more address on the server accepts the plugin's script key", and said "Yes to all three". So **there is no tool**: every agent turn carries the tool list, hosting a call is rare, and this way the model never handles even the key's hash. It is built in phases, one pull request each. **Phase 1 is the broker's side of the pushed path** (most of this section). **Phase 2 is the call as a member of the room** ("The call as a member", below): a row in Members, its details, End this call and its platform's icon, for the broker's own bot's call as much as a hosted one. **Phase 3 is the relay and the host skill** ("The relay and the host", below): `switchboard meeting relay`, a Compose file for joinly and the relay, the Claude Code plugin's host script and skill, and GPU or CPU. **Phase 4 uses one checked-line entry point for the server bot and hosted calls**, carries the server's voice to the relay, and adds Codex's token-based host skill. **Phase 5 adds `/meeting <link> mine`**, a private one-use handoff from the person in the room to their own host script, without a model seeing the link.

**The path.**
1. The host script makes a key for the call on the person's machine: `sbc_` and 64 hex, in a file only its owner reads. It prints the key's fingerprint (below), and shows neither the key nor its hash to the model.
2. The script registers the call, with a POST to `/mcp/meeting/host` of the room, that sha256, the platform and the model, under the credential the plugin's other scripts already hold (an agent token, or a listener key), and starts the relay. The person asks their agent to host the call, the agent runs the plugin's script, and the harness's own permission prompt covers running it. Neither the key nor its hash passes through the model, the tool list or any tool call: only the hash passes the network, to the broker (the same pattern as a listener key's claim, §49.11).
3. The relay on the machine joins the call as joinly does (§52), and uses the key at the three routes below: `POST /mcp/meeting/push` for what it hears, and `POST /mcp/meeting/speech`, a long poll, for what is to be spoken.
4. A person ends the call from the call bar (the same **End call** control). The key stops working at once.

**Registering** (`POST /mcp/meeting/host`, `broker/call_http.py`'s `MeetingHost`, then `meeting/hosted.py`'s `HostedCalls.register` and `open`). The route is opened by what the plugin's scripts already present at `/mcp/listen` and `/mcp/session`, through the same door (`AgentAuth.door`, §49.4, §49.11): an **agent token** (`sbat_`, as `Authorization: Bearer` or the plugin's `X-Switchboard-Token`), or a **listener key** (`sbl_`, `Authorization: Bearer`), which stands for its sign-in's token and acts only for its own session. It is in `AGENT_PATHS` (the page's Origin rule does not apply, because the route refuses any request that has an `Origin` itself, as `/mcp` does). It answers POST only, and refuses: no credential, a token that is not live, a listener key that is unknown (`unknown_key`) or whose sign-in was revoked (`revoked`), a **call key** (`sbc_`: its three routes are its own), a **session cookie** (a cookie authenticates nothing here: a request with only one is a 401), and any request with an `Origin` (a browser's). The body is JSON, at most 4 KiB: `room`, `key_hash`, `platform`, `model`, the optional boolean `from_room` (the private handoff below), and, for a token, an optional `session`.

*Whose call, and for which room.* What the call is comes from the **credential**, never from the body: its owner is the token's person (`owner_person_id`, None the owner, §32.2), its machine's label is the token's device name when the call began (a host name such as `work-laptop`, §49.1), and the name shown is the person's. A body that names a machine or a person is not read. **The room must have a member on that machine**: a live session with a live membership whose device is the token's (a device name is unique among live tokens, so a member of another token's machine never counts). With a **listener key** the session is the key's own, which the broker already knows (a hook of the key's connection named it, or its first request pinned it, §49.11) and is **never taken from the body**: a body that names another session is refused as at the listener (403 `wrong_session`), a key that is for no session yet is refused (409 `no_session`) and is not pinned by this route, and the room must have a member of that session and its stored harness (Claude Code or Codex, §49.11). With a **token**, the body may name a session (the id its agent's harness gave it) and the room must have a member of that session on the machine; with none named, any member on the machine will do. A room that does not exist, one the machine has no member in and one its agent left are the same refusal (403 `not_member`, which names only the room the script asked for), so the route tells a script nothing about rooms it is not in. The room may be asked for by a name it had before a rename (#316).

*What else is checked.* The arguments are claims, held to a value or cut: `key_hash` must be exactly 64 lower-case hex characters (the key itself is refused, with a sentence that does not repeat it), `platform` one of `zoom`, `meet`, `teams` or `unknown` (empty is `unknown`; any other value is refused, never kept), and `model` text, cleaned and cut to 80 characters. It is refused, in this order, when: the token has made five tries in the last minute (429 `rate_limited` with `Retry-After`: every try counts, a refused one too, by the broker's clock, and a listener key counts against its token); the room is not one the machine has a member in (403 `not_member`); the body is wrong (400 `bad_request`); the room already has a call, whichever source it has (one source for a call); the person already hosts a call, from any of their machines (one hosted call per person: the owner's tokens are the owner's); the broker already follows `[meeting] max_calls` calls (§52: the count is every call that is joining or live, the bot's own and hosted ones, and the sentence gives only the number); or the key's hash is already some call's, ended or not (a key names one call for ever, a unique index): each 409 `conflict`. It needs no `joinly_url`: a hosted call does not use the broker's bot. The answer is `{room, state: "joining", platform, host, fingerprint}`: the call's state and the key's fingerprint, never a key or a hash. The room reads `alice is hosting a Zoom call from alice-laptop`, and the room's `settings.meeting` and an event `meeting_host` (platform, host, person, fingerprint) follow.

**The private room handoff (phase 5).** `/meeting <link> mine` is still a human-only room command. It parses the same Zoom, Meet and Teams links as the server path, with a 2,000-byte ceiling matching the host's private file. It never starts the server bot and needs no `joinly_url`. `HostedCalls.request` finds active room memberships whose active session's device has a live agent token belonging to the command's person. No token means no proof of ownership: an unvouched host never falls back to the owner. With none, it says no agent is acting for the person in this room and removes any earlier pending request for that person and room.

With eligible members it holds one `_Request` in memory, keyed by `(room_id, person_id)`, for 600 seconds by the broker's clock. Its URL is excluded from representations. A repeated request replaces that person's prior request for this room. The room gets a notice saying the person asked their own agent to host the call; a short human message mentions all their eligible agents and asks for the host skill with `--from-room` and empty stdin. Other memberships are excluded from delivery of this instruction. It contains no link or passcode; ordinary delivery rules still apply, including holds, wake budgets and approval waits. The command's raw text is never posted. The `meeting_mine` event keeps only the platform. There is no call row yet and no slot is reserved. Requests expire on the tick and before a claim, and are dropped on room closure, broker shutdown or restart. Starting any call in that room cancels its remaining pending requests.

The existing `POST /mcp/meeting/host` accepts the JSON boolean `from_room` (absent/false keeps direct hosting unchanged; a non-boolean is refused). After its existing token/listener-key session and room-membership checks, `HostedCalls.register` looks up only that credential's person and room. No pending request is a generic 409, which reveals nothing about another person's requests. Normal registration limits and key validation apply; failure leaves the request available until expiry. A successful registration consumes it synchronously, with no await between checking, opening and consuming, so the first of several eligible agents to register wins. A supplied platform is still checked against the existing allowlist; the stored platform is then the parsed link's, never a claim from the script. Invalid UTF-8 links are refused before being held or encoded. The response adds `link_b64`, an ASCII base64 transport of the UTF-8 link; this is secret transport, not encryption. The response already has `Cache-Control: no-store`. It is returned once, even if the answer is lost, and is never an agent-tool response, notice, frame, log or database field.

Both packaged host scripts implement `start --from-room`: they read no stdin, add `from_room: true` to their existing registration, decode the response into the same private link file the relay uses for stdin, and print neither the response nor its secret field. Base64 avoids JSON/shell escaping differences, including quotes and Unicode. The sh script uses the system's `base64` (`-d`, with `-D` fallback); PowerShell uses its built-in decoder. A missing, oversized or malformed field fails closed before Compose starts. A refusal uses local fixed wording rather than repeating a server-supplied message. `--no-join` cannot combine with `--from-room`, so a setup check cannot consume a real request. Codex can use its thread-bound script key after the §49.11 helper setup. The skills distinguish this human handoff from other room text, tell the model never to read the link file/response, and stop on a refusal. The shell's normal permission prompt still covers starting containers. No route, tool, tool parameter, tool description, credential scope or guard changes in this phase.

**Handoff tests.** `test_meeting_mine.py` covers owner and other-person credentials (token and listener key), targeted wake, wrong person/room, one use, first successful registration, failed validation, malformed modes, expiry, replacement, missing/revoked/left members, room close, restart, and full database/history/log scans. `test_meeting_mine_host.py` runs both isolated packages' sh and PowerShell scripts against scripted registration responses and drives the actual room-command → script → registration → private file → relay → scripted joinly path. It checks secrets never reach output, child arguments/environment or broker storage. The existing server-call integration tests remain unchanged. `tests/live/test_live_claude_mine.py` runs it for real: a real Claude Code, signed in through the browser, is woken by the person's `/meeting <link> mine`, runs the skill with `--from-room` and an empty stdin, and its relay joins a real Zoom call; the meeting's id is then in no Claude Code transcript, database file, room message or relay log, and End call and `stop` leave nothing running.

**The key and its fingerprint.** `meeting/keys.py`, pure. A key is `sbc_` and 64 hex, the prefix apart from the agent token's `sbat_` and the listener key's `sbl_` so a secret scanner can spot it and no route takes one for the other. The broker keeps only `sha256(key)` of the whole string, `sbc_` included, in `meetings.key_hash`. The **fingerprint** is the first eight hex characters of that hash with a middle dot, `7f3a·91c2` (`keys.fingerprint`): short enough to read out and compare, and it names no more of the hash than eight characters. The relay prints the same fingerprint when it starts, so the owner can check the call bar's against their own machine's. A key is a bearer for the three routes and for nothing else. It is refused on `/mcp` (the tools), `/mcp/listen`, `/mcp/session`, `/mcp/meeting/host`, the hook routes, `/mcp/status`, the web API and as a person's session, and these three routes refuse an agent token, a session, a listener key and any request with an `Origin` (a browser's), and answer only POST. The words of a refusal: `unauthorized` (401: no call key at all), `unknown_key` (401: a key that was never registered, or the wrong shape, which look alike), `revoked` (401: the machine's token was revoked, and the call ends on the spot), `ended` (410: the call is over, whoever ended it). The relay treats a 410 as "leave".

**Pushing** (`broker/call_http.py`, `HostedCalls.push`). The body is JSON `{"segments": [{"speaker", "text", "start", "end"}, ...]}`, at most 100 segments and 256 KiB. Each is checked and the whole push refused (400, or 413 for size) if any is not: text is a string (cut to 2,000 characters), `start` and `end` finite numbers (not a boolean, NaN or infinity), `speaker` a string or nothing (cleaned and cut to 40 characters: it is a claim, and a source can put any name in anyone's mouth, which is why the line shows whose machine heard it). No role is taken: a pushed segment is always the call's speech, and the relay leaves the bot's own out. **The first accepted push, with nothing in it too, marks the call live**: the relay's empty first push says it is in the call, so the bar changes from joining to transcribing and the room reads `Transcribing a Zoom call from alice-laptop.`. A push of a segment the call was given before (the last 4,096 are remembered) takes none of it, so a relay that retries a push whose answer was lost does not post a line twice, as the broker's own bot skips a segment it has read already (§52). The answer is `{"accepted", "duplicate", "ended": false}`.

**What a pushed segment becomes** is what the broker's own bot's becomes, by the same code (`meeting/ingest.py`, which the bridge uses too, with no change in what it does): the same `Assembler` joins segments into lines (§52, "Lines"), and both sources enter `ingest.receive`. It returns an `Incoming` holding the checked text and the call row; `Incoming.post` stores and delivers it with the call id and, only for a hosted call, its machine label. The server bot may await a snapshot between receiving and posting, keeping the picture before the line. The checks are shared: the transcriber's hint list written back is dropped (#616, #602), `names.match` picks out who was called out and an ambiguous name is told to the room, the speaker is cut to 40 characters, the text to 1,000, and the line is posted as a **`meeting` message** (`sender_kind=meeting`, so agents read it as untrusted peer text, never as their person's, §52 "Untrusted"). Posting is paced as the bridge's is, one line a second (`LINE_GAP_S`), with at most 50 waiting and one warning when the oldest is dropped. A line that is open is closed by a tick after 2.5 s of the broker's clock without a word. The delivery rules are §52's: only the member a line names, or whose claim it names, is woken; the line rides along to the others.

**Limits** (all in `meeting/hosted.py`, and none moves with a setting):

| | |
|---|---|
| a body | 256 KiB (413) |
| segments in one push | 100 (413) |
| a segment's text, a speaker | cut to 2,000 and to 40 characters |
| segments (and requests) a second | 10 sustained, a burst of 100: an allowance of 100 that refills at 10 a second by the broker's clock; an empty push or a poll costs one; refused whole with `429 rate_limited` and `Retry-After` |
| a speech poll's wait | the poll asks, 0 to 30 s (25 if it says nothing), far inside the Ingress's 16 minutes (DEPLOY.md); a non-number is 400 |
| hosted calls | one per person, and with the broker's own bot's, at most `max_calls` |

**Speaking** (`HostedCalls.speech`, `speak`, `set_voice`). The same rules as §52's voice, over a poll. Voice is off when a call starts, a person turns it on or off with `/meeting voice on|off` or the bar's switch, and the state is memory only. While it is on and the host is in the call (it has pushed), an agent's `say()` that starts with `@call` is made speakable (`meeting/speech.py`) and offered to the relay under the one-slot turn policy (§52.2), by `AgentService._post_say`, which hands the posted text to the hosted call as well as to the bridge. The poll's answer is `{"lines": [...], "speech_id": int|null, "hints": [...], "voice": bool, "ended": bool, "tts_voice": str}`: the lines to say (only while voice is on, each given once, to the newest poll only), the room's names as the transcriber's hotwords (`names.hotwords`, each cut to 40 characters, at most 100: the relay sends them to joinly as it sends its own), the voice switch, whether the call is over, and the server's `[meeting] tts_voice`. Before opening joinly, the relay reads that setting from its first poll and checks the same 1–32 lowercase-letter, digit and underscore shape as config. A missing or malformed field keeps the relay's `--voice` fallback, so it still works with older servers. It is answered at once when there is a ready line, the voice switch changes, the call ends or a newer poll arrives (an older one is answered with nothing); otherwise after the wait. The waits are events of the broker's loop, not sleeps. The hints are read at each round, so a member who joins is heard for within one. A hosted bot sends no snapshot yet: `/meeting snapshot` says so, and the bar's button is off.

**Ending** (`HostedCalls.stop`, `end`). A person's **End call** is `/meeting leave`, the command the bar's button already sends. For a hosted call it is allowed to the **person whose call it is** and to an **admin** (the owner is one, §51); anyone else is refused (`forbidden`, "Only alice, who hosts it, or an admin can end this call."). Ending posts what is left of the transcript (the line being assembled and the lines waiting, without the pacing, as the bridge does at its end), marks the row ended, which refuses the key at once (`ended`), answers a waiting poll with `ended: true` so the relay can leave, takes the call out of the room's settings, and records a `meeting_leave` event. The lines already pushed stay in the room with their call and their machine. A hosted call also ends, with a notice that says why and the same phrase in the row's `error`, when:

| | row `error` | notice |
|---|---|---|
| its machine's token is revoked (Settings, Agents), seen at the next tick or the next request | `its machine's sign-in was revoked` | `The call ended: its machine's sign-in was revoked.` |
| it has not gone live ten minutes after it began (`JOIN_WINDOW_S`; joinly's own join is given 45 s and a first start also pulls images and a 1.6 GB model), however much its key was heard from meanwhile | `its host never started sending` | `The call ended: its host never started sending.` |
| it is live and has heard nothing from its key, no push and no poll, for five minutes (`QUIET_S`: over twice the 130 s `WAIT_DROP_GRACE_S` gives a wait nobody answers); a poll that is waiting is the host being there | `its host went quiet` | `The call ended: its host went quiet.` |
| its room is closed | none | `The call ended because the room closed.` |
| the broker stops (`shutdown`), or is killed and starts again (`MeetingBridge.recover` ends every open call, hosted ones too, §52) | none, or `the broker restarted during the call` | `The call ended because the broker stopped.`, or the restart's |

The timeouts are read from the broker's clock in a tick (`HostedCalls.tick`, every 0.5 s from a task of the broker's lifespan), so a test steps the clock and calls it; none sleeps.

**The bot left** (`POST /mcp/meeting/leave`). On 2026-11-06, asked whether to add the route, with the cost stated as "the call's own key would be the only thing that can use the new address, and it could only end that one call", the maintainer said "I approve." (recorded on #623). Only `AGENT_PATHS` and the call key's route list widen: an agent token, listener key, cookie and any `Origin` are refused as at push and speech. The call comes only from the bearer key, never a room or call id in the body. A JSON object carries `reason`: `stopped`, `join_failed` or `joinly_lost` produces respectively `The call ended: its bot stopped.`, `The call ended: its bot could not join.` or `The call ended: its bot lost the call.` Any other value (including `ended` and `left`) becomes the generic `The call ended: its bot left.`; raw reasons are never stored or repeated. It uses the same ending path above and answers `{ended: true}`. A later request is 410, a harmless no-op when the room already ended the call. The relay makes one bounded best-effort request on every exit, after any last push, before closing its broker connections. Its original exit code is preserved. A broker out of reach still has the timeouts above. No tool, parameter or tool description is added.

**What is stored (schema 42).** Every pushed line is traceable from the person to the line: the person, the **agent token** that asked (named in their settings, so the label is that machine's name), the **key** (only its hash), and **each line with its call**. `meetings` gains `source` (`server` for every call that was there, or `hosted`), `key_hash` (64 hex, unique across all calls, NULL for the bot's own), `token_id` (the token that opened it), `owner_person_id` (None: the owner), `host` (the machine's label, cut to 24 characters as device names are, kept as of the start so renaming or reusing a device name later cannot change what a line says), `model` (the host's claim, 80), and `lines_pushed` (the number of lines it has posted, counted in the same transaction as the line). When the call was created is `started_at`. `platform` also admits `unknown`. `messages` gains `meeting_id`, and a pushed line also carries the host in `sender_host` (a host name, as for an agent's device, §49), which is what the page shows after the speaker; since phase 4 the server bot's lines also carry `meeting_id` and increment the call's count, with no machine label. The visible server call details stay as before. The migration (`V41_TO_V42`) adds the columns with `ALTER TABLE` (every call that was there reads `server` and no key, and every line no call), makes the key unique, and edits the platform CHECK with the same schema-text step as §52's, which `_migrate` verifies and rolls back from if the new CHECK is not in the table's text. The events `meeting_host` and `meeting_leave` carry the fingerprint and never the hash or the key. Deleting a room removes its calls and their lines (§28.6: messages first). **Schema 43** (phase 2) adds `meetings.live_at`, when the call went from joining to live (`Store.set_meeting_live`, by the broker's clock; NULL while it is joining and for a call that was live before the step), so the call's details can say "Live since 14:02" and mean it: `started_at` is when the call was registered, and a call may spend minutes pulling an image and a model before its first push. The same step lets an admin upload the icons of the three platforms: §29.11.

**The page.** `settings.meeting` for a hosted call is `{state, platform, voice, source: "hosted", host, fingerprint}`, and for the bot's own call the keys it had (never a link, a key or a hash). While a call is joining or live the bar says `Transcribing a call (Zoom) · on alice-laptop · key 7f3a·91c2` (or `Joining a Zoom call… · on …`; with `platform: unknown` it names none, `Transcribing a call · on …`, and appends ` · agents can speak` while voice is on). Its button is **End call** for a hosted call (**Leave call** for the bot's: ending a hosted call ends its key), and **Snapshot** is off. A transcript line pushed by a hosted call says `via alice-laptop` after the speaker, from `host` in the message; the line keeps it after the call ends. All of it is `textContent`, and it fits a phone. The bar's icon is the platform's, the same as the call's row has (below), and an admin's upload for the platform shows there too.

**The call as a member (phase 2, #623).** The maintainer's decisions (2026-11-05): the member is a "call" and the group is **Calls**, though the feature and the command stay "meeting"; each call shows its platform's icon, as agents show their harness's, with the platform's name beside it, which an admin can replace (§29.11); the icon is the platform's own logo, which the maintainer later chose to ship (§29.9). A room has at most one open call, so the group has at most one row.
- **The row.** In Members, after People and Agents, a **Calls (1)** group with one row, counted in the Members total (and the header pill's). It shows the platform's icon (its logo on a white tile in light and dark; the sound-wave drawing on the neutral tile for a call that says none) with a status dot (solid green while the call is live, dashed while it is joining, as an agent that has not started yet), **Zoom call** (Meet, Teams; **Call** when a hosted call says `unknown`), and where it runs under it, after the state: `Live · alice-laptop`, `Joining… · alice-laptop`, or `Live · the server` for the broker's own bot. The label is the machine's, as the bar says it (the token's device name when the call began, never anything the body of a request said). The row comes from `settings.meeting` alone, by `callOf`, the one test the bar, the row and the details share (joining or live, on a platform the broker sends, and for a hosted call one that names its machine), so what is open is the same everywhere in the page and a call the broker's frame says nothing usable about shows nowhere. It is greyed as "last known" while the socket is down, as the agents' list is.
- **The details** open from the row in the Inspector's own pane, with the Inspector's keys and focus: a click or Enter on the row opens them and puts focus on the call's name; Back and Esc close them and put focus back on the row (on a phone, where the pane is a sheet, Esc closes the sheet first, as it does for an agent's Inspector, and Back returns to Members with focus on the row); a redraw (a frame, the broker's answer) keeps focus on the control it was on (`data-focus` keys); and when the call ends with focus in the details, the pane returns to Members and focus goes to the last agent's row, or the Members heading when there is none, as for an agent that was kicked: never to `<body>`. They follow the room: a `room` frame (voice turned on, the call going live) redraws them at once, and a `meeting` line (the count of lines sent) asks the broker again, debounced as the Inspector's refetch is. They close with a notice from the room when the call is over, and when the page changes room. They show the title (**Zoom call**); `Live since 14:02 · transcribing` (`live_at`, or `Live · transcribing` when it was not recorded) or `Joining…`; a kind chip (`hosted bot` or `server bot`) and where it runs (the machine's label, or the server); one line on what the call does ("It pushes what it hears into this room as call lines. Nobody wakes unless called out by name." for a hosted call, and for the broker's own bot that it listens and posts what it hears the same way); and Details: **Hosted on**, **Started by**, **Key fingerprint** with a copy button (hosted calls only, the first eight characters of the key's hash as the bar has it, never the key or the hash), **Model** (a hosted call, when it said), **Lines sent** (a hosted call only; the server bot's lines are counted internally too, but its visible details stay as before) and **Voice**.
- **The per-viewer answer.** Whether this viewer may end the call is not the same for everyone, and `settings.meeting` goes to everyone in the room (the settings frame and `GET /api/rooms`), so it cannot ride in it. The page asks the broker for it: **`GET /api/rooms/{slug}/call`** (`web.py`, `RoomService.call_detail(room, person_id, admin)`), for a signed-in person who can see the room (`room_for`: a private room they are not in is the same 404 as one that does not exist, so its name is not confirmed, §44). It answers `{room, call}`, `call` null when nothing is open (or only a call the page may not show), else what the frame says plus `source`, `started_by`, `started_at`, `live_at` and, for a hosted call, `model`, `lines` and **`can_end`**. `can_end` is the rule `/meeting leave` holds (§54, "Ending"): for a hosted call the person whose it is or an admin (`HostedCalls.may_end`, the same function the command asks), for the broker's own bot whoever may use Leave call, which is anyone who can run a command in the room (§52). It is computed on the server from the viewer's session (`Who.person_id`, `Who.is_admin`), so **no person's identifier is sent**: the answer holds a boolean, not the call owner's id, the token's id, the call's id, the key or its hash (`tests/unit/test_call_detail.py` pins the keys). `started_by` is the owner's display name, which the room's own notice already says ("alice is hosting a Zoom call"); in a private room it is given only when that person is one of the room's people, as §29.10 does for an agent's owner, and otherwise it is null and the page leaves the row out. It is a GET for the reasons §29.5 gives the Inspector's (read on demand, nothing added to every viewer's frame) and one more: the answer depends on who is asking. It writes nothing and publishes nothing, and other methods are 405.
- **End this call** is in the details only when the answer says `can_end` (a person who may not is shown no button, and nothing that says why: the room's own text says who hosts it). It sends `/meeting leave`, the command the bar's button and a typed line send, so the server decides again and a button the page shows wrongly is refused in words (`tests/integration/test_meeting_hosted.py`). Under it: for a hosted call "Only alice and an admin see this. The next push with this key is refused; lines already sent keep their label." (the owner's name, or "the person who hosts it" when the room may not name them), for the broker's own bot "The bot leaves the call. What it already posted stays in the room." The bar follows the same answer: for a hosted call its **End call** is hidden until the page has asked and shown only when `can_end` is true, so bob is not offered a button the server would refuse. The bot's **Leave call** stays for everyone, as before.
- **Tests (phase 2).** `tests/unit/test_call_detail.py` (the fields; `can_end` for the call's person, an admin, the owner, an ordinary person and a viewer with no person; the broker's own bot's call allowed to everyone and carrying none of a hosted call's fields; no id, key or hash in the answer; none, an ended call and a call the page can't show; a private room naming only its people; the settings frame still carrying nothing per viewer), `tests/integration/test_call_detail_web.py` (the route on a team server: each viewer's `can_end` for the owner's call and another person's, the frame the same for both, the refused command, the fields and no secret, the line count, the end, a signed-out request, a room that is not visible, other methods, a private room, and a desktop broker's own bot), `tests/unit/test_db_migrate_v43.py`, `tests/unit/test_store_meetings.py` (`live_at`), the node harness's `call_member` (`tests/unit/test_web_app_behavior.py`: the row, the count, the details, End this call by the answer, the call going away, a call with no platform, a platform the page has none for) and `tests/e2e/test_calls_ui.py` in a real browser (the bot's call and a hosted one, with the details of each; joining turning to live by the host's first push; every platform's logo, in light and dark and on a phone, and the sound-wave drawing for a call that says none; End this call shown to the call's person and an admin and not to an ordinary person, and ending a hosted call refusing the next push; an admin the owner made; keyboard and focus; light, dark and a phone with every control inside the screen and no sideways scroll; and the platforms' uploads).

**The relay and the host (phase 3).** The broker's side is above. The machine's side is three things, all in switchboard's repository and none needing anything installed but Docker.

- **The relay** (`meeting/relay.py`, `switchboard meeting relay`, in switchboard's own image) is `bridge.py` turned around: the broker doesn't reach out to it, it reaches in. It reads the call's key and the meeting link from files only their owner can read, or from stdin (`--key-file` and `--link-file`, with `-` for stdin), never from an argument or the environment; a file others can read is refused unless `--no-mode-check` says the files are a container's mounts. Both are held as a `Secret` that prints as `<secret>`, so a stray f-string or traceback shows nothing. It prints the key's fingerprint, then asks the broker for the room's names (`POST /mcp/meeting/speech` with `wait` 0), connects to joinly with them as hotwords, joins the call, and marks it live with an empty first push. Then two loops run until the call ends. One reads joinly's transcript when joinly says it changed (or every 3 s) and pushes each new segment, in batches of at most 100; the bot's own speech (`role` other than `participant`) is not pushed. The other long-polls `/mcp/meeting/speech` for the `@call` lines (while voice is on) and has joinly say them, one at a time, dropping the waiting ones when voice goes off.
