A `429` is waited out for `Retry-After` (at most a minute at a time), an error or a `5xx` is retried after 1, 2, 4 ... 30 seconds, and a broker that stays away as long as it would itself wait before it ends a call (`QUIET_S`, 300 s) ends the relay. `ended` in a poll, a `410` or a `401` makes it leave the call and exit. A call ended while the relay is still joining is heard (the voice loop runs from the start) and the join is dropped. SIGTERM (`docker stop`) pushes what was heard last, leaves, and exits. **Its exit code says why:** 0 the call ended, 2 a key or link that can't be used, 3 the key was refused, 4 joinly could not join, 5 joinly was lost, 6 the broker could not be reached, 7 stopped. It shares joinly's client code with the broker's bot (`meeting/joinly.py`: the settings header, the transcript parsing, the redaction of a link from the MCP libraries' logs), and the numbers it holds the broker to are copies of `hosted.py`'s that a test keeps equal. Its HTTP is the standard library's, in a thread a request, closed from outside so that a stop doesn't wait out a 25 s poll; a plain `http` address is refused unless it is this machine's own or `--allow-http` says the network is trusted, because the key is a bearer.
- **The Compose file** (`plugins/claude/meeting/compose.yaml`; DEPLOY.md, "Meetings on your own machine") starts the relay and joinly for one call, one Compose project each (`switchboard-call-<fingerprint>`). joinly has no sign-in, so it has **no address**: it shares the relay's network (`network_mode: service:relay`) and listens on that network's `127.0.0.1`, with no port published. It **ends with the relay** by sharing its process namespace (`pid: service:relay`): when the relay's first process ends, the kernel ends the rest of the namespace, joinly's included, so End call from the room leaves nothing running and nothing has to watch. Both run with a read-only root, no capability and `no-new-privileges`. The two joinly fixes (§52) are mounted from the plugin's data folder and **pinned by sha256** in `meeting/fixes.pins`: a file whose hash differs is refused and removed. **GPU if there is one, CPU if not** is a Compose profile: `gpu` is joinly's `-cuda` image with one NVIDIA device and a named volume for the model, and `large-v3-turbo`; `cpu` is the plain image and joinly's own `base`, with no download.
- **The host script** (`plugins/claude/bin/meeting-host`, with `windows/meeting-host.ps1` for PowerShell and `meeting-host.cmd`, and the skill `skills/host-meeting-bot`) is what the agent runs when its person asks. `start` checks everything that can be wrong before a key exists (Docker and Compose answer; the credential; the fixes; the images, pulling a missing one), reads the link from stdin into a 0600 file in a 0700 folder, makes the key (`sbc_` and 64 hex) there, registers its hash at `/mcp/meeting/host`, checks that the fingerprint the server gives back is the one it made (and does not start if not), starts the project, prints the fingerprint, the device and the model, and returns. `stop` takes the project down and forgets its files; `status` lists the calls and the relay's last lines; `plan` prints the choice and starts nothing. Neither the link nor the key is ever an argument, a variable or output: the link goes from stdin to a file by the shell's own `printf`, the credential reaches `curl` on its stdin, and the containers get the files as a mount. A link given as an argument is refused, so is a link that is not an `https` address on Zoom, Meet or Teams, and the server's address must be `https` (or this machine's own, or allowed with `SWITCHBOARD_RELAY_ALLOW_HTTP=1`). **The GPU is detected with three local questions that pull nothing:** `nvidia-smi -L` lists a GPU, `docker info` lists an `nvidia` runtime (the Container Toolkit), and the machine is not a Mac. A flag forces either, and the output says what it overrode. The skill says to run it only when the person asks, with the link in a quoted heredoc, never to repeat the link, to tell the person the fingerprint and the device, and how to stop. The sweep at each `start` removes the containers and folders of calls that have ended.

**Codex hosting (phase 4).** `plugins/codex/skills/host-meeting-bot` runs the same shell and PowerShell host and Compose project. The marketplace installs only the plugin's subdirectory, so resources cannot point into a sibling plugin. The five host files are packaged identically in both roots (`tests/unit/test_meeting_plugin_packages.py` keeps them equal); a release pins both Compose files. The shared scripts detect the Codex manifest and use `SWITCHBOARD_AGENT_TOKEN` first, otherwise their own process key (§49.11), never a Claude credential. Codex's shell must also have `SWITCHBOARD_URL`, and defaults to `~/.cache/switchboard` for data (`--data` overrides it). With a token, an agent on its device must be in the room and the script uses the existing any-member-on-the-device rule. With a process key, it sends its own thread id and only that thread can host. Phase 4 shipped token-only; #637 adds a local helper key claimed through browser sign-in (§49.11), without adding a route, request field or tool. The key is pinned to a Codex thread and cannot use the listener or session-report routes. The departure route below widens `AGENT_PATHS` with the maintainer's yes of 2026-11-06.

**Tests (phase 4).** `tests/integration/test_meeting_shared_path.py` drives both sources through `ingest.receive`, checks their stored call ids, speaker and host labels, and the named agent's wake. The server bot's existing integration tests stay unchanged. `tests/integration/test_relay_and_broker.py` checks the configured server voice in the speech response and in hosted joinly's header before it joins; `tests/unit/test_meeting_relay.py` checks valid, missing and malformed voice fields and the fallback. `tests/integration/test_meeting_codex_host.py` runs an isolated plugin's token-based start and stop and refuses an unconfigured browser-only host even with Claude credentials nearby. #637 adds the private helper and thread-key path, preserving those refusals. Package equality and both release image pins are tested too.

**Threat model** (SECURITY.md has it with the others).
- **A pushed line is a claim, owned by whoever pushed it.** It is no harder to fake than a person typing "here is what we said", and the speaker names Zoom gives are chosen by whoever joined. So what matters is the label and the limits. A pushed line is a `meeting` message, never read as a person's; it has the same reach as any call line (it can wake the agent named in it, §52), and the page says whose machine heard it. The speaker is only text.
- **It adds a way into a room**, with no setting to turn it off (the maintainer, 2026-11-05: "what matters is the label and the limits, not who may turn it on"). The whole of what the call key can do is push call lines into one room's one call, within the limits, read that call's `@call` lines, its room's names and whether voice is on, and end that same call. It cannot read a room, post as anyone but `call` or a speaker's name, call a tool, run a command, sign in, or touch another call. It ends with the call, and the person or an admin ends it from the call bar.
- **The registering route widens the credentials that exist, by one address.** A token could always act for its device's agents, and a listener key could listen and report for one session; now both can also register a call (the cost the maintainer was asked about, above). What they get from it is a call that is the person's own, in a room one of their machine's agents is already in, at five tries a minute, with the limits of this section, that the person or an admin ends from the call bar and that times out by itself. A listener key's file is readable by any process of the same user, the agent's shell included (§49.11), so a prompt-injected agent could register a hash nobody holds the key to; that holds the person's one hosted call and the room's call slot until the ten minutes pass or a person ends it, and gives no one a way in.
- **A key is as safe as the file it is in.** It is made on the machine and kept in a file only its owner reads, so a process of the same user on that machine can read it, an agent's shell included: it could push lines into that one call or end it. It gets nothing else, and the call bar shows whose machine and which key it is. It is never in the database, a log, a notice, a frame or a message (`tests/integration/test_meeting_hosted.py`, the secrets test).
- **A call is hosted only when its person asks their agent to.** There is no tool for it, so a peer's text has nothing to call in the tool list, and nothing in the tool list tells a model to host a call. The script that registers it is the plugin's, run by the agent through its shell, which is the harness's own permission prompt; the registration needs the credential of a machine that has an agent in the room.
- **The broker never connects out to an address a person gave.** The relay connects in over HTTPS, and joinly on the person's machine has no address of its own: it listens on the relay's loopback, shared through the Compose file (it has no password). A link given directly to the agent stays on the machine, but that model sees it in its transcript; the skill forbids repeating or saving it. With `/meeting <link> mine`, the broker holds the link briefly in memory and gives it once to that person's credential-backed script, without showing it to a model. Neither path puts it in a process listing, child environment, log or repository file. The handoff also excludes it from broker storage and room messages.
- **The machine's files are the person's.** The link (until the call is joined) and the key are 0600 files in a 0700 folder, and the relay, which runs as the person, deletes the link once it has joined and the key when it ends. Any process of that user can read them while they exist, a prompt-injected agent's shell included (above).
- **What the machine fetches is pinned.** The two joinly fixes come from commit-pinned addresses and are used only if their sha256 is the pinned one (a changed file is refused and deleted, and `tests/integration/test_meeting_host.py` checks the pins against the self-test's and DEPLOY.md's). joinly is pulled by digest (`name:tag@sha256` of the index; the CUDA build has its own digest, and appending `-cuda` to the CPU reference is not that image). switchboard's relay image stays the release tag: the release pull request pins it before that image is built (§30.5).
- **The relay's own reach is small.** It runs as the person with a read-only root, no capability and `no-new-privileges`, talks to the server over HTTPS (a plain `http` address only for this machine's own, or when the person says the network is trusted), and to joinly on its own loopback. It has no tool, no token and no way to read a room: its key does the three things above.
- **The cost is the broker's to bound**: one hosted call per person, `max_calls` across the broker, the limits above, and the timeouts.

**Not yet.** A snapshot from a hosted bot.

**How a command the agent runs reaches the route (settled with a live run, Claude Code 2.1.307).** The route takes what the plugin's hook scripts take, but a command the model runs through its Bash tool is not a hook, and the open question was what it is given. A real Claude Code with the plugin from this checkout, in a throwaway home against a throwaway broker (`tests/live/test_live_claude_host.py`, and the recorded payloads `tests/fixtures/payloads/claude/PostToolUse_bash_plugin_env.json` and `PostToolUse_bash_plugin_skill.json`), answered it:

- **A Bash command is given `CLAUDE_PID`, which is Claude Code's own pid** (the script's grandparent: `claude`, then `bash`, then `sh`), and `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT` and the `CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_*` pair. **It is not given `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT`, `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA`, any `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_OPTION_*` variable (so not the typed token or the server's address), or any `SWITCHBOARD_*` variable.**
- **A plugin skill's text has its placeholders filled in before the model sees it:** `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`, `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}`, `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}`, `${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}` and `${user_config.server_url}` (a non-sensitive setting; the token, a sensitive one, is not given to a skill). So the skill's one command carries the plugin's folder, its data folder and the server's address as arguments, and the command the model runs is the one with the paths in it.
- **The listener key is in the plugin's data folder even when a token was typed.** `bin/headers` makes it for every connection, and the server's claim of it (§49.11) happens on any request that carries a live token, typed or signed in. So the credential for a Bash command is always `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}/listener-key-${CLAUDE_PID}`: the data folder from the skill's argument, the pid from the variable. With the data folder missing the script looks for the one folder under `${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-~/.claude}/plugins/data/` that holds this pid's key; a pid names exactly one. A token in the script's environment (`SWITCHBOARD_AGENT_TOKEN`) is used if there is one, for a person who runs the script by hand or from a harness whose shell has it.
- **Codex (phase 4, 0.158.0).** A live Codex with a temporary home and the installed plugin loaded `host-meeting-bot` and ran its packaged `meeting-host plan --cpu`. Both `SWITCHBOARD_AGENT_TOKEN` and `SWITCHBOARD_URL` were present in its shell; only booleans were recorded, never their values ([the sanitized observation](https://github.com/amahpour/switchboard/blob/main/tests/fixtures/codex_meeting_host_shell.json)). The plan registered no call. The model's direct request had to follow Esc cancelling the held Stop hook, as USAGE says. The unchanged seven-scenario Codex live suite passed too. The old live setup stopped Codex 0.166.0 at hook review before a model call; #635 fixes the test-only composite hash setup (§12.4), with no prompt approved. The later #637 helper (§49.11) supplies a thread-bound script key for browser-signed-in hosting, without reading Codex's credential store.
- **Not recorded for Cursor, Devin or OpenCode.** They read the two variables from the environment the harness was started in; whether their shells inherit them is still unrecorded. A token needs no session named: with none, any member of the machine will do.

**Tests.** `tests/unit/test_meeting_keys.py` (the hash, the fingerprint, the shapes), `tests/unit/test_meeting_ingest.py` (the shared posting rules), `tests/unit/test_meeting_hosted_rules.py` (registering: which room a machine may host into, a named session and its harness, a left or ended member, a renamed room, the five tries a minute by a `FakeClock`; opening and its refusals; the key's check; assembling and pacing; the rate limit; a retried push; what a body may be; every way a call ends and the timeouts by the clock; voice; the speech poll woken by events), `tests/unit/test_listener_keys_rules.py` (`session_of`), `tests/unit/test_store_hosted_meetings.py`, `tests/unit/test_db_migrate_v42.py` (from the real 0.37.2 database, the constraints, the backup, a new database equal to a migrated one). `tests/unit/test_mcp_server_static.py` and `tests/integration/test_agents_http_cursor.py` are as they are on `main`: the tool list is the one `main` serves, 11 tools and no more than 3,900 characters. `tests/integration/test_meeting_hosted.py`: a scripted relay (an HTTP client with a key) and a Claude Code session on a machine (a token, or a listener key) against the real broker on a `FakeClock`: the call is registered by the route, and the tool list has no tool for it; a spoken name wakes only the member named, with the machine on the line; voice gives `@call` lines to the poll and a waiting poll is answered by the event; End call answers the poll `ended` and refuses the next push, keeping the lines labelled; a call that never goes live or goes quiet ends by the clock; a revoked token, a restart and a killed broker end it; a wrong key, a revoked one, one never registered, another route, an agent token, a browser's Origin and the wrong method are refused at the call's routes; the registering route refuses no credential, a revoked token, an unknown or revoked listener key, a call key, a session cookie and an Origin, a member of another machine, and a body that is too big or wrong; a listener key registers only for its own session; the five tries a minute; one source per call and `max_calls`; the limits over HTTP; the key nowhere in the database, the log or any frame; and on a team server who may end a call. `tests/integration/test_listener_keys.py` (a key is refused at a call's routes). `tests/e2e/test_meetings_ui.py` (the bar, End call and the "via" tag, light, dark and a phone, and End call refusing the next push).

**Tests (phase 3).** `tests/unit/test_meeting_relay.py`: the relay against a scripted broker and a scripted joinly, both in memory, with its pauses and its clock as numbers (the first request for the names before joinly is touched; the first push empty; a segment pushed once and the bot's own speech not at all; 250 waiting segments as 100, 100 and 50; a `429` waited out and its batch resent; the growing pause and its bound and the broker that stays away; `410`, `401` and `ended` each leaving the call with their exit code; a call ended while joining; a failed join; joinly still starting and joinly that never comes; joinly dropping; SIGTERM pushing the last words; `--no-join`; voice lines spoken in order, dropped when voice goes off, a failed speech, an interruption; the poll gap; nothing the relay says or sends holding the key or the link; the files burned; the key and link files' modes, a link to a file not followed, an error that repeats nothing; the broker's address; the HTTP client against a socket, its `Retry-After`, a non-JSON answer, and an abort that ends a waiting poll), and the numbers kept equal to `hosted.py`'s and to `cli.py`'s defaults. `tests/integration/test_relay_and_broker.py`: the real `Relay`, its HTTP client and its MCP client against the real broker and `tests/fakes/fake_joinly.py`: the room's names reach joinly as hints, the first push makes the bar live, a spoken name wakes only that agent, voice speaks an `@call` line once and made speakable, End call makes the relay leave and exit 0 and refuses the next push, a `429` from the real broker is waited out with every segment taken, an unknown key stops it before joinly is touched, a revoked sign-in stops it, a call ended while it is joining. `tests/integration/test_meeting_host.py`: the script, in `sh` and (where `pwsh` is installed) PowerShell, with a stand-in `docker` that records its arguments and the `SB_` variables, a `curl` wrapper that records the same, and a stub server: the GPU choice for a card with and without the toolkit, with neither and on a Mac, and a flag forcing each; the registration (the hash, not the key; the room, platform and model; the fingerprint compared); one project named for the key; neither the link nor the key nor the credential in any argument or variable or output; the files' modes, private from the moment they exist; `--no-join`; refusals from the server told in its words with nothing left behind; everything that can be wrong found before a key exists; the credential found from `CLAUDE_PID` and the data folder from the pid, and the script run against the real broker with a Claude Code session's listener key (the key it made is the one it registered, and a second try for the room is refused in the server's words); the fixes checked, replaced, refused and made public; a missing image pulled first; `--add-host`; `stop`, `status` and the sweep; the Compose file's privacy and its tie to the relay (parsed, and read by `docker compose config` where Docker is). `tests/unit/test_release_script.py` (the Compose file's image is a pin a release moves). `tests/live/test_live_claude_host.py`: a real Claude Code, signed in through the browser, asked in words, uses the skill and the script, and the real joinly and a relay built from the checkout come up, with End call stopping both (the run that settled the question above). `tests/live/test_meeting_selftest_hosted.py`: a hosted bot starts and stops cleanly with no call, and the six steps with the bot hosted, here or on another machine.

## 55. The home page (#626)

The web UI opens on one page that answers three questions across every room the person can see: what is waiting on me, what got done while I was away, and what is live. Below them are the rooms, as the sidebar has them. It is a query and a view over what the broker already stores. What it keeps is per person: a "looked at" time (§55.4) and, since #663, how far they have read each room and which board cards they set aside (§55.8).

### 55.1 What the maintainer decided

1. **Home is the first screen every time switchboard opens.** A link to a room (`/#build`) is explicit navigation and still opens that room, and so does reloading while in a room (the address names it). Opening the address with nothing after it opens Home, on a desktop and on a phone. Home sets the address back to the bare path.
2. **"Done since you last looked" is a page only.** There is no digest, no notification and no email. Nothing is sent to anyone.
3. **Finished turns with no visible result are left out.** A turn that ended with a `pass` said nothing, so it has no line. Only what someone can read is a line.

### 55.2 The data (`broker/home.py`, `GET /api/home`)

One answer for the signed-in person, from the stored rows, never for anyone else and never about a private room they are not in (§44; the rooms come from `RoomService.visible`).

- **Waiting on you.** Two kinds of card, newest first, capped at 50 (`waiting_total` says how many there were).
  - *A question:* the newest chat message in a room that is addressed to this person and that they have not answered. "Addressed" is the page's own unread test (§29): an @mention of them, `@humans`, or an agent's reply to something they said. Another person's message counts only when it names them or everyone, and a person's own message never counts. "Answered" means they said anything in that room later (`Store.home_lines`), and a message they have read no longer counts: only messages after the person's seen mark for the room are questions (§55.8). One card a room, with the count of the others under it. Only the last seven days are looked at (`WAIT_DAYS`).
  - *A review-board call:* each open board that has items a person must decide, by the board's own `needs_person` (§37.2: an open question, a contested finding the raiser can no longer answer, an unconfirmed fix). A board the person set aside with **Not now** has no card until the board changes (§55.8). Each item carries the moves a person may make on it, which are the board page's: answer (with an option), concede (to an agent in the room) or drop (with a reason) a contested finding, confirm a fix.
- **Done since you last looked.** Newest first, capped at 30: a board handed off (who posted the review), a board settled and waiting for its handoff, a call that ended (its line count and the members its lines addressed, from `messages.meeting_id`), and, for each agent in each room, its latest message since, when it is not addressed to the person (those wait above instead). "Since" is the person's stored time, at most seven days back, and the last day for someone who never looked. Not done work: a join, a leave, a notice, a pass.
- **Live now.** The room's open call (`RoomService.call_detail`, so a private room's host is named only to its people), and each agent that is busy or holds a live claim (§47) with its status line and when the claim ends. An agent in two rooms is listed in each.
- **Rooms** are drawn by the page from what it already holds (§29): most recently active first, their red counts and quiet dots.

### 55.3 Answering a card does what answering in the room does

A card has no route of its own for answering. A reply goes through `POST /api/rooms/{slug}/say` with `reply_to`, and a board call through `POST /api/rooms/{slug}/review` with the move the board page sends. So the room shows the same message and the same board line, the agents are woken the same way, and the same refusals apply (a stale move is refused and its reason shows on the card). **It never approves anything for an agent** (§11): a card answers a question or makes a call a person already may, and nothing on it can answer an agent's permission prompt, change a setting or start anything.

### 55.4 "Since you last looked"

`meta` holds one time per person (`home_seen:<person>`), moved by `POST /api/home/seen` with `{"at": t}`: never into the future, never back. The page sends the broker's own time from the answer it showed when the person leaves Home for a room, or when they press **Mark as seen**. Reloading Home therefore shows the same done work until they leave it, and nothing is lost to a refresh.

### 55.5 Live

The page already receives every frame of every room it follows (§29). While Home is shown, a message, room, members or review frame asks `GET /api/home` again after 0.75 s (one request however many frames arrive), and with a room shown only the sidebar's Home count is on view, so only a frame that can change it asks (a message to this person or from them, or a board's move; not members, settings or other chatter), and no more than once in 10 s. Opening Home with that slow refresh waiting replaces it with the quick one. The drawing is skipped when nothing in the answer changed, so a half-typed reply, a card opened for a call and the keyboard's place all survive a refresh.

### 55.5.1 What a build costs (#649)

The build runs on the broker's event loop inside the route, as every route does, so a slow one stalls every page and agent. One person with 50 rooms of 300 messages each took a median of 257 ms at first and takes 23 ms now (`tests/integration/test_home_perf.py` fails above 100 ms). Three things made the difference: each room is read once, by `Store.home_lines`, which walks the room's newest 300 chat messages from the end of the index (so history does not add cost) and lets SQL find the messages that name the person, reply to them or are an agent's latest, making a `Message` only of those few, with the page's own test still deciding in Python; Live is built from `Store.live_agents`, three queries for every room, instead of the Members pane's `members`, whose queue counts and wake times Live does not show; and a call's `call_detail` is asked only for a room that has one. There is no cache: it was not needed, and a cache would have to be invalidated by every kind of change this page reads.

### 55.6 The page

The sidebar's **Home** row is above the rooms and shows the count of cards. Home replaces the log, the composer and the members pane (`#app.home-open`). On a phone it is one column and the header's Rooms button opens the drawer; a card's buttons go under its words, at least 44 px tall, and nothing scrolls sideways. By keyboard: Tab runs the sidebar, then the cards in reading order; **Reply** and **Decide** open their form in place, **Not now** on a board card hides it (§55.8), Escape closes it and returns to the button, Ctrl+Enter (or Cmd+Enter) sends a reply; once a card is answered focus goes to the next card's button, or to the page when none is left. A polite status line says what was sent.

### 55.7 Not in the first build

An ambiguous call-out ("Someone said 'Claude'; this room has claude-1 and claude-2") is told to the room as a notice and answered by saying the number on the call. A card could not answer it the way the room does, so it is not a card. A pull request opened, as a line of done work, is not recognized either: switchboard does not read pull requests (§37.1), so a board handed off is the closest thing it knows.

### 55.8 Cards go away once seen (#663)

The maintainer's decisions: opening a room counts as seeing its questions, and a review board's card stays until its items are decided, with **Not now** to hide it until the board changes. Both are kept on the server per person, so they hold across browsers and devices.

**Schema 45** adds two tables, both empty after the step and both removed with a deleted room (`_ROOM_ROWS`, §28.6: a room's id is reused, and a mark left behind would hide a new room's first messages). `person_key` is `room_people`'s, 0 for the owner.

- `home_seen(person_key, room_id, seen_id)`: the newest message id the person was shown in that room.
- `home_snoozes(person_key, room_id, review_id, snoozed_at)`: a board they set aside, and when.

**The seen mark.** `POST /api/rooms/{slug}/seen` with `{"id": n}` moves the signed-in person's mark in that room forward to `n` (`Store.home_mark_seen`, one transaction). It never moves back (a lower `n` changes nothing, and the answer is the mark as it stands) and is cut to the room's newest message id, so it can never hide a message that has not been written. Anything but a whole number from 0 up, or a body with another field, is a 400. A room the person cannot see is "no such room", as for every room route (§44), and stores nothing. A person can only move their own: the key is the session's, never in the body. `Store.home_lines` takes the mark and adds `id > mark` to the question part of its one SQL pass, so Home stays as cheap as in §55.5.1 (`tests/integration/test_home_perf.py` still holds it under 100 ms).

The page posts the mark with the newest id it holds for a room (`r.lastId`) when the room is opened (`selectRoom`, so from **Go**, a done line, a room row or the sidebar) and when the person leaves the room for Home, which covers a question that arrived while they had it open. It posts at most once for the same id. Home waits for any mark in flight before it asks for itself, so the card is gone when it is drawn, and a room opened from another room refreshes the sidebar's count once the mark is stored.

**Not now.** `POST /api/rooms/{slug}/home-snooze` with `{"url": board}` sets aside that room's open board for the signed-in person at the broker's clock (`Store.home_snooze`; pressing it again moves the moment forward). Only an open board of a room they can see can be named; anything else is a 404 and stores nothing. The board's card is left out of that person's Home while the board has not changed since: `board_changed_at` is the newest `updated_at` among its items, so a new item, or a move on one, is later than the moment and the card comes back by itself. Nobody else's Home is affected. A closed board's snooze is simply never read again.

**Tests.** `tests/integration/test_home_page.py` (the routes, the seen mark and Not now), `tests/unit/test_db_migrate_v45.py` (schema 45), `tests/unit/test_web_app_behavior.py` (the page under node: first screen, a reply, a board call, leaving, the mark sent on opening a room, Not now), `tests/e2e/test_home_ui.py` (the real page: phone, keyboard, the room showing the answer, Go and back, Not now).

---

## 56. A room's whiteboard (#656)

One whiteboard per room, shared by whoever can see the room. This section covers storage, merge, the agent tool and the sandboxed drawing pane (#657). Mermaid conversion and the storyboard play view are §56.9 (#658). Nothing here fetches a service or runs a browser: the elements are Excalidraw's own shape, the page renders them, and the broker builds agent shapes itself.

### 56.1 What is stored (`store.py`, schema 46)

`room_boards` is one row per room: a `seq` and one JSON list of elements, at most 1,000,000 bytes. `room_board_changes` is the last 200 accepted changes, oldest dropped as a new one is written: who, when, the seq, and which ids (at most 80 of them), not a copy of the drawing. Deleting the room deletes both (`Store._ROOM_ROWS`), or the row-count check on delete would fail.

A reader sees a committed board or the previous one. The load and the write share one transaction.

### 56.2 The merge (`board.py`)

Excalidraw's own rule, per element. The higher `version` wins. A tie goes to the lower `versionNonce`, and an equal nonce keeps what is already stored, so the same element sent twice changes nothing. A delete is a tombstone (`isDeleted`) that stays in the list, so a later copy of that id cannot revive it by accident. The whole element is the unit: two people editing different fields of one shape at the same version can lose one of the edits. That is the rule the spike chose, and the agent tools send the smallest bumped change so a move does not also rewrite the words.

Caps, from the spike: 1,500 elements live and deleted together, 1,000,000 bytes, 200 characters of text, 40 elements in one agent call, 500 points on a line. A coordinate is clamped to ±20,000. Text from a person is cut to the same 200, so what an agent reads back stays bounded.

### 56.3 One tool, and a skill

`board(room, action, …)` is one tool, the same shape as `review` (§37): the description is one line that names the actions, and the how-to is a `board` skill, the same file in the Claude Code, Codex and Cursor plugins. The actions are `note`, `box`, `connect`, `frame`, `move`, `edit`, `delete`, `read` and `draw_mermaid` (§56.9). The broker mints an id for a new shape. `read` returns the summary the spike measured (frames in reading order, shapes with ids, arrows as `start -> end`) by default; `text="image"` asks an open board page for a picture (§56.8). Leave `x` and `y` out and the broker places the shape.

With `to` naming a frame, automatic placement starts inside it and skips occupied slots; the enclosing frame itself is not an obstacle.

A change is rate-limited like a post and, when it is kept, shares that post's clock (`participants.last_say_at`). Inside the window the call is refused, not queued: a say can wait its turn, and a drawing that waited could land on top of a newer one. `read` is not limited. The tool list measured 3,653 before this tool and 4,165 after, and the cap is that size (`test_tool_list_fits_the_review_budget`). The maintainer's answer "1. Yes" to "Can I raise it and start #656 tonight" is the yes for that one tool and nothing else.

An accepted change posts one notice, `{who} changed the board`, with none of the shape text in it. It is a notice, so it wakes nobody (§8: delivery runs for chat). The hub then fans the accepted elements out as kind `board`.

### 56.4 The socket

`hello` ends with `{t:"board", room, seq, elements, full:true}`, after the `members` frame. A page sends `{t:"board", room, elements}` and, when the merge keeps something, every page on that room gets `{full:false, elements}` of just what was kept. A frame larger than 16 KB is parsed only when it starts with `{"t":"board"`. Anything else the client sends still closes the socket. A page frame is not rate-limited: the person can already post, and the pane sends a batch when a stroke ends, one notice per batch that changes something.

`switchboard tail` does not print the shapes. Its subscription is messages and notices, and `board` is not one of those kinds.

### 56.5 Security

Shapes from outside are a claim, run through one allowlist (`clean_element`).

- Text only. `link` is forced null, from an agent and from a person. A URL in the text stays characters.
- An image, an embed, an iframe or a magic frame is refused. A person may also send a line or a pen stroke; an agent may not. Any other type fails closed, and so does an unknown action.
- A field the allowlist does not name is dropped, so a script has nowhere to live. The pane draws it as canvas text, never HTML.
- Only a member can change the board. For an agent that is the membership the other tools already require. For a page it is `RoomService.visible`: a private room the person is not in is no room, and the socket closes.
- What another agent reads back is `sanitize`d with no message-length cut (the board's own caps bound it), so `<` cannot close a harness tag and the words are data.
- A board that would pass the byte cap is refused. A page that sends one is told nothing changed; an agent is told the board is full.

### 56.6 Browser views

The drawing pane and optional picture are §56.8; Mermaid conversion and storyboard playback are §56.9.

### 56.7 Assumptions

- The history is the last 200 change records, not a snapshot per change.
- Person and agent text share the 200-character cut.
- A page may round-trip `line` and `freedraw` so #657 can. `image`, `embeddable`, `iframe` and `magicframe` cannot.
- One notice per accepted page frame. A page that sends on every pointer move would fill the log; the pane sends when the stroke ends.
- Schema 46: #663 took 45 first (§55.8).

**Tests.** `tests/unit/test_room_board.py` (the merge, the caps, each action, the summary, the rows and a room delete, with a `FakeClock`), `tests/unit/test_db_migrate_v46.py`, `tests/unit/test_hub.py` (a `board` frame stays in its room), `tests/integration/test_board_tool.py` (the tool, the envelope, the rate limit, two sockets, a hostile element, a private room).


### 56.8 The sandboxed drawing pane (#657)

**Approval.** The maintainer chose option A on 2026-11-08, recorded on [#671](https://github.com/amahpour/switchboard/pull/671): "Yes. I agree with the recommendation", answering "A. The exceptions for the board box only". This approves five exceptions for the board document only: inline styles, framing by switchboard alone, browser-made data/blob images and workers, one fixed sandboxed iframe in the static checks, and scoped CORS for modules and fonts. It covers nothing more. The main app's CSP and headers are unchanged.

**The document.** `/board` is a public shell with no embedded room, session or credential. It gets exactly `BOARD_CSP` in `broker/auth.py`: `default-src 'none'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; connect-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data: blob:; worker-src 'self' blob:; font-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; frame-ancestors 'self'; form-action 'none'`, and `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN`. No inline script, evaluation, WebAssembly exception or external host is allowed. All other responses keep their previous headers, including the strict CSP on the assets themselves. Only existing JS, CSS and font files below `/board-assets/` get `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, with no credentials allowed; errors and every other path get no CORS exception.

**The boundary.** `whiteboard.js` constructs exactly one fixed `/board` iframe on demand, with `sandbox="allow-scripts"` and no `allow-same-origin`, plus the browser's `credentialless` flag. Its opaque origin cannot access the parent or cookies. The main page alone owns the authenticated socket. It checks `event.source`, the opaque origin, message keys, an instance id and the current room before accepting a response. Both sides use `board-rules.js` to cut, clamp and allowlist elements like `board.clean_element`; the broker runs that function again. Text is drawn as canvas text, never inserted as HTML. Links, images and embeds are rejected. Library and import controls are absent; paste is disabled. The build removes the hard-coded external font fallback and serves its bundled fonts locally; CJK and emoji use the machine's installed fallback fonts.

**The page.** The header's Whiteboard button switches from the chat log to the board. Back to chat returns focus to it. Changing rooms, renaming or losing a room closes the frame; returning opens a fresh one with the stored scene. Light/dark settings follow the app. The same pane fills the phone's available space. The parent caches the board frames it already receives from the hub; it sends a full scene on opening and merges later changes by version and nonce. The frame sends hand-drawn changes on pointer-up and debounces keyboard edits. Restoration repairs bindings and measures bound text in the browser, wrapping and centering it in its existing container without changing target dimensions. A local-font loading event repeats this derived layout in cold browsers; it never writes an edit. Font/layout changes do not echo as edits. Straight bound arrows derive their endpoints from the actual targets' rotated bounds, preserving both bindings and target dimensions; targets are never passed back through skeleton conversion. This fixes the spike's arrow-to-existing-shape bug.

**An optional picture.** `board(action="read", text="image")` uses the existing tool schema and leaves its measured budget unchanged. A transient request id is sent through the room's board frames to an already-open pane, which rasterizes through Excalidraw. The parent accepts only a bounded ArrayBuffer from that frame. A visible-room socket answers `{t:"board", room, request, result}`. The broker accepts one PNG for that pending room, checks base64, PNG structure and CRCs, at most 600,000 bytes and 1,200 pixels a side, and rejects a picture if the stored revision changed. It returns native MCP image content, never a persisted picture or a new kind of element. At most 16 requests may wait; each expires after four seconds and cleans up on cancellation. Without a page, the result asks the person to open Whiteboard. No browser process is launched by the broker. Pictures, like the text summary, are peer data.

**The build.** Excalidraw 0.18.1, React/React DOM 18.3.1 and esbuild 0.25.12 are pinned by `scripts/board/package-lock.json`. `build.mjs` takes an output path and dependency folder, so the approved download folder stays outside the checkout. It bundles the frame adapter, copies the exposed unmodified local fonts, retains bundled license comments and writes a NOTICE with MIT and font licenses, package notices and the byte count. The unused built-in Mermaid/AI UI and non-English interface locales are excluded. Unexposed fonts have no downloadable faces, and CJK uses system fallbacks. Excalidraw's dist also carries excalidraw.com's own service settings: its Firebase browser config (a public Google API key, which GitHub's secret scanning flagged on this branch), and its share, collaboration, AI and Plus export settings. Nothing in the board reads them and its CSP allows no connection to them, so the build empties them and fails if the pinned chunk stops matching; `test_the_board_ships_none_of_excalidraw_coms_service_settings` keeps a rebuild from bringing them back. This keeps the source archive within the existing 4 MB packaging guard; no guard is raised. The static lint pins every emitted byte, and rebuilding twice is compared byte for byte. Built files are committed; `node_modules` never is. This follows §29.9's reviewed-asset model, not a CDN.

Tests: `test_board_page.py` proves the exact policy and its isolation from existing responses; `test_board_browser.py` compares browser cleaning to the broker; `test_board_tool.py` checks pending PNG requests and room/byte/dimension guards; `test_board_ui.py` draws by pointer, follows agent tools live in light/dark/phone, verifies the opaque boundary and cookie-free document request, reads a PNG, and checks arrow endpoint geometry. The existing static guards still scan all handwritten page code and pin the complete vendor build.


### 56.9 Fresh flows and storyboard playback (#658)

`board(action="draw_mermaid", text=...)` adds a fresh flowchart, with no new tool or input fields. Its one-line description stays within the existing measured tool-list budget. Source is limited to 8,000 characters and 16,000 bytes. Only `flowchart` and `graph` are supported initially. Configuration directives, styles, images and unsupported diagram kinds are rejected; standalone click directives are removed. Markup punctuation is made inert before Mermaid's temporary DOM layout and normalized back only in the element cleaner, when labels are canvas text. Encoded markup is inert too.

The broker asks an already-open pane to convert through the existing board hub frames. Requests have a random id bound to room and kind, expire after eight seconds and share the picture path's global cap of 16. An agent has at most one conversion pending. `@excalidraw/mermaid-to-excalidraw` 2.2.2 is a lazy chunk in the pinned board build. It uses the existing pinned Mermaid 11.17.2 asset through one exact board-asset alias with scoped CORS; the original static path's headers are unchanged. The same opaque document, CSP and postMessage checks as §56.8 apply. No new exception is needed.

The page validates the converted elements before forwarding. The server validates every element as agent input, at most 40, remints every id and group, and remaps internal references. It cannot replace or bind to an existing shape. Placement uses the latest live scene's rotated bounds and all stroke points: to its right with an 80-unit gap, or below when the right side is full. If it cannot fit within the coordinate limits, or any element or board cap fails, the entire conversion is rejected. Membership and the shared post rate limit are checked again after conversion. One successful import writes one change, one notice and one hub delta. No page means a bounded error asking the person to open Whiteboard; no headless process is started.

**Play frames** requests fullscreen on the parent panel, with a viewport-filling fallback when the browser does not support it. The view is read-only. Frames are ordered by their row (100-unit bands), then x, matching the summary's reading order. Each frame's name is its caption, drawn by the parent with textContent. Previous/Next, arrow keys and Escape/Exit play navigate without a server write. The sandbox displays only that frame and its children and fits its camera to them; updates still arrive live. Exiting restores the complete editable scene and focus to Play frames. Pending person edits are flushed before entering; image reads still export the complete board. The phone uses the same view and controls. Exporting a storyboard is outside this issue.

Tests: `test_board_mermaid.py` checks source/type/byte/id/placement caps, including rotated shapes and long strokes; `test_board_conversion.py` drives the real MCP/WebSocket request and races it with leaving and posting; `test_board_story.py` converts hostile text without browser errors, edits a converted label and plays six frames on desktop and phone without changing stored data.
